# LUTzy — Phase 2 Implementation Spec

### Non-destructive render pipeline + RAW develop controls

_Generated by a multi-agent workflow grounded in the LUTzy source. Audience: power users. The derive-LUT feature is preserved as a value-add._

> [!NOTE]
> **Status of this draft (read before implementing):**
> - **7 of 8 components are specced.** The *tone/color adjustment nodes* component (CIColorControls / CIExposureAdjust / CIVibrance / CIHighlightShadowAdjust mappings) did not finish in the generating run and is pending a follow-up pass.
> - **Where per-component sections conflict with the Executive Summary, the Executive Summary's "ground-truth corrections" block is authoritative** — it was reconciled against the real source and the installed `CIRAWFilter.h` SDK header after the component drafts were written.
> - Several per-component "color-correctness" review flags reflect a *deliberate* deferral of working-color-space precision and Display P3 to the Open Questions section, not defects.
> - This is a design spec, not committed code. Validate against the source before building.

---

## 1. Executive summary

## LUTzy Phase 2 — Executive Summary

**What we are building.** Phase 2 turns LUTzy from a single-shot LUT applier into a non-destructive, ordered render engine for power users, plus RAW develop controls. The work is governed by the BINDING architecture already chosen: one `Sendable`/`Codable`/`Equatable` value type (`EditDocument`) is the single source of truth for "the look"; one pure function (`RenderPipeline.buildImage`) folds it into ONE lazy `CIImage`; one `actor RenderEngine` owns the only `CIContext` and rasterizes that image at two scales (preview `CGImage` / export `Data`) through ONE color seam (`WorkingSpace`). The derive-LUT feature (`RecipeExtractor`) stays physically decoupled and keeps its own neutral RAW baseline.

**Why this shape.** It simultaneously delivers four things the real code lacks today: (1) **Swift 6 readiness** — the non-`Sendable` `ImageProcessor` singleton (a `final class` holding a Metal `CIContext`, captured into `Task.detached` in `exportDialog`/`deriveRecipe`) becomes an actor; only `Sendable` values cross the boundary. (2) **Structural preview/export parity** — today `renderPreview` and `export` are two code paths that merely agree; after Phase 2 they are the SAME `buildImage` call differing only by a `RenderScale` value. (3) **Undo / presets / per-image edits for free** from the `Codable` document. (4) **A single color seam** collapsing the 3 hardcoded sRGB sites (`CubeLUT.makeFilter:135`, `ImageProcessor.export:180`, and the IMPLICIT preview-raster site `ImageProcessor.swift:109` where `createCGImage` passes no color space) into one `WorkingSpace.current`, future-proof for Display P3 without committing to it.

**Ground-truth corrections (verified against the real files + the installed Xcode `MacOSX26.5.sdk` `CIRAWFilter.h` + Swift 6.3 toolchain — these override conflicting claims in the per-component specs, several of which contradict each other):**
- **This is a SwiftPM package, not an Xcode project.** Only `Package.swift` is git-tracked (`swift-tools-version:5.9`, `platforms:[.macOS(.v14)]`, single `executableTarget`). Every instruction about "add the file to the .xcodeproj app target" or "set `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY` in an xcconfig" is WRONG: files under `LUTzy/` are auto-globbed; strict concurrency is a `swiftSettings` entry; a test target must be added to `Package.swift` AND the app split into a `LUTzyKit` library + thin `@main` executable (`@testable` cannot import an executable).
- **CIRAWFilter properties — the two RAW specs flatly contradict each other; the header settles it.** The class is `NS_CLASS_AVAILABLE(12_0, 15_0)`. ALL of `isSharpnessSupported`(121), `isMoireReductionSupported`(145), `isContrastSupported`(129), `isDetailSupported`(137), `isLocalToneMapSupported`(153), `isColorNoiseReductionSupported`(113), `isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported`(105), `isLensCorrectionSupported`(97) **EXIST** and SHOULD gate their knobs. `gamutMappingEnabled` is **settable** (`readwrite`, line 92). `shadowBias`/`boostAmount`(0...1)/`boostShadowAmount`(0...2)/`baselineExposure` are plain `readwrite float` with no gates. **EDR is a SINGLE knob `extendedDynamicRangeAmount`** (line 161, 0...2) with **NO per-property availability macro → it inherits the macOS-12 class floor and is callable UNGUARDED on the v14 target.** There is **NO** `isEDRModeEnabled`/`isEnableEDRModeAvailable`/`enableEDR` bool — delete it everywhere. **`isBaselineExposureAvailable` and `isDustRemovalSupported` do NOT exist (fabricated).** **The ONLY RAW knob needing an `#available` guard is highlight recovery** (`highlightRecoveryEnabled`/`isHighlightRecoverySupported`, lines 87-88, `NS_AVAILABLE(16_0)`). (Correction to an earlier draft of this very summary: EDR does NOT need `#available(macOS 15)`.)
- **`CubeLUT.id` is `String`** (`let id: String`, line 6 — a file path, or synthetic `derived://name/UUID`), not `UUID`. The pinned `LUTID(raw: UUID)` design will NOT compile. `LUTID` must wrap a `String` and be **deterministic for file-backed LUTs** (use `id`/path) so persisted/undo documents resolve after a `LUTLibrary.scan` (which rebuilds every `CubeLUT` and is triggered by `saveDerivedLUT`). Correctness requirement, not a nicety. `CubeLUT` is currently only `Identifiable, Hashable` (== / hash by `id` only) — add `: Sendable` (its only stored payload is `private let tableData: Data`, which is `Sendable`).
- **Already done — do not redo:** `ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral(at:)` (line 74) is already the single shared neutral-baseline static (used by `loadRAW:85` AND `RecipeExtractor.derive:89`); extension routing is already unified on `ImageProcessor.supportedExtensions` (consulted by `ImageCollection.loadFromFolder:44`). There is no `isImageFile` symbol. The only RAW-vs-standard split is the **private** `ImageProcessor.rawExtensions` (line 19) used inside `loadImage:56` — `RenderEngine.makeSource` needs access to it (widen scope), it is NOT a duplicate to consolidate.
- **Non-bugs — do not "fix":** `PreviewView` has no undefined `preview` symbol and no FIXME; `sideBySideView` (lines 35-61) correctly uses `originalPreviewNSImage`/`previewNSImage` and compiles. `FilmstripView` contains ZERO `ImageProcessor`/`generateThumbnail` references; BOTH thumbnail sites are in `ImageCollection` — the `Task.detached` in `generateThumbnails():100-101` AND a **synchronous** call in `addFromData:65` (the easily-missed one).
- **`CGImage` is NOT `Sendable`** under Swift 6. The pinned "return `CGImage` across the actor, zero `@unchecked`" is contradictory. Use `-> sending CGImage?` (region-based isolation; available in the 6.3 toolchain) — preserves BOTH the zero-`@unchecked` promise AND the zero-extra-copy benefit over returning bytes.
- **EXIF/ICC metadata cannot be injected through `CIImageRepresentationOption`** — that enum (`CIContext.h:671-772`) declares only depth/matte/HDR keys, no `kCGImageProperty*` channel. Metadata preservation (if pursued) must use `CGImageDestination`, NOT `context.jpegRepresentation(options:)`. Treat metadata as a SEPARATE, later step.

**Scope discipline.** Headline deliverables: (A) the non-destructive ordered pipeline, (B) RAW develop controls. Metadata/ICC embedding, Display P3 enablement, and `kCIContextWorkingColorSpace` precision changes are explicitly OUT of the v1 critical path — the architecture leaves clean seams for all three. The whole migration is sequenced so the app builds and ships at every step, starting from today's single-shot `applyLUT`.

## 2. Architecture — Synthesis, anchored on Approach 2 (Declarative EditState + stateless RenderEngine), with two explicit grafts: (a) the LUT-by-stable-ID indirection and Codable/Equatable value-state from Approach 2 as the spine; (b) a CLOSED enum of adjustment nodes (`AdjustmentNode`) rather than `[any AdjustmentNode]` existentials — taking the *ordering/extensibility* idea from Approach 1 and Approach 3 but rejecting the existential array because it breaks Codable/Equatable/Sendable cleanliness, which is the whole point of the value-state spine. From Approach 3 I graft the lazy single-CIImage chain (no inter-node rasterization), early downscale via `CIRAWFilter.scaleFactor` for RAW, and the `actor RenderEngine` owning the only CIContext. The result: one Sendable/Codable `EditDocument` value type is the single source of truth; one pure `RenderPipeline.buildImage(...)` folds it into one lazy CIImage; one `actor RenderEngine` rasterizes that image at two scales (preview/full) through one explicit `WorkingSpace`; derive stays physically decoupled by never constructing an `EditDocument`.

**Why this approach.** WHY APPROACH 2 IS THE SPINE, JUDGED AGAINST THE REAL CODE: The single most valuable property for this codebase is a Codable/Equatable/Sendable value-state model, and the real code makes this unusually high-leverage for three concrete reasons. (1) Concurrency: `ImageProcessor` is a non-Sendable `final class` singleton (ImageProcessor.swift:9,16) holding a Metal `CIContext` (line 13), captured into `Task.detached` in `exportDialog` (AppViewModel.swift, `[processor, exportFormat]` capture) and reached from `RecipeExtractor` and `FilmstripView.generateThumbnail`. Under Swift 6 these are hard data-race errors. A *value* state model means the only thing crossing the isolation boundary is `Sendable` data, and `CIImage`/`CIContext` never escape the actor. Approach 1's `[any AdjustmentNode]` makes the recipe itself awkward to prove Sendable/Codable (existentials need a type tag + manual coding and cannot synthesize Equatable), so it fights the very seam we most need. (2) The real `processedImage`/`selectedLUT`/`sourceImage` triple (AppViewModel.swift:13,18,19) is a single baked CIImage with no history — there is no undo, no presets, no per-image persistence. A Codable `EditDocument` delivers all three for free and `ImageCollection` already wants per-image state. (3) Equatable enables cheap change-detection to drive a debounce, which the real applyLUT lacks (its `Task {}` inherits @MainActor and runs the full-extent filter graph on the main thread — a confirmed responsiveness bug). WHY THE CLOSED ENUM GRAFT (rejecting Approach 1's protocol existentials): Approach 1 sells extensibility, but for a zero-dependency app whose nodes are a small, known set of CIFilter wrappers, an `enum AdjustmentNode` gives the same ordered-list composability while preserving automatic `Codable`/`Equatable`/`Sendable` synthesis and avoiding per-node boxing/dynamic dispatch in the fold. Adding a node is one enum case + one switch arm — trivially as extensible as conforming a new struct, with strictly better serialization ergonomics. This is the decisive graft. WHY THE LAZY-CHAIN + actor GRAFT FROM APPROACH 3: Approach 3 correctly insists nothing rasterizes between nodes (lean on CI deferred evaluation), downscales EARLY (via `CIRAWFilter.scaleFactor` for RAW, which is cleaner and higher-quality than the current post-hoc affine transform at ImageProcessor.swift:90), and isolates the CIContext in an `actor`. These three are pure wins over Approach 2's sketch and I fold them in. WHY NOT APPROACH 1 OR 3 WHOLE: Approach 1's existential array is the wrong serialization substrate (see above) and its RAW-as-source-factory-but-nodes-as-array asymmetry is real but I keep that asymmetry deliberately (RAW develop genuinely must rebuild CIRAWFilter from the URL — you cannot chain develop onto an already-developed CIImage). Approach 3 is very close to my synthesis but (i) leaves adjustments as an enum already (good) yet returns raw bytes (`RenderResult`) across the actor boundary, which forces the @MainActor caller to rebuild an NSImage and adds a copy on every preview tick; I instead return `CGImage` (which is `Sendable`-safe to hand back and trivially wrapped by `NSImage(cgImage:size:)` on the main actor) — simpler and zero extra copy; and (ii) Approach 3 sets `kCIContextWorkingColorSpace = extendedLinearSRGB` + `RGBAh`, which is a behavioral change to *everyone's* render and risks out-of-gamut hue shifts on the 8-bit JPEG/PNG export path — I defer that to a documented open question rather than baking it into the binding decision. NET: Approach 2's value-state spine + Approach 1's ordered-node concept realized as a closed enum + Approach 3's lazy chain / early RAW downscale / actor isolation. This maximizes Swift-6 cleanliness, gives undo/presets for free, makes preview/export parity structural, and keeps derive provably decoupled.

## LUTzy Phase 2 — Canonical Render Architecture (BINDING)

The design has four layers with strict dependency direction:

```
EditDocument (value, Codable/Sendable)   ← the look, serializable, undoable
        │  describes
        ▼
RenderPipeline.buildImage(...)           ← pure fn: EditDocument → ONE lazy CIImage
        │  evaluated by
        ▼
actor RenderEngine (owns the ONE CIContext)  ← rasterize at .preview / .full
        │  hands back
        ▼
CGImage  → @MainActor wraps NSImage(cgImage:)   (preview)
Data     → write(to:)                            (export)
```

`RecipeExtractor` sits OUTSIDE this stack entirely. It never imports `EditDocument` and never calls `RenderEngine`. Its only shared vocabulary with the pipeline is the *value* `RAWDevelopSettings.neutral`, which documents (and, in a debug assertion, enforces) the neutral-baseline contract.

---

### Layer 1 — The data model (value types, all `Sendable`, all `Codable` except where noted)

```swift
import CoreGraphics

/// THE single source of truth. Replaces the implicit
/// sourceImage + selectedLUT + processedImage triple in AppViewModel.
/// Codable → undo/redo stack, copy-paste-look, per-image persistence in ImageCollection.
/// Equatable → cheap change detection to drive the render debounce.
struct EditDocument: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    var rawDevelop: RAWDevelopSettings = .neutral   // only meaningful when source is RAW
    var adjustments: [AdjustmentNode]  = []          // ordered, non-RAW tone/color stages
    var lut: LUTSettings               = .none       // LUT reference (by ID) + intensity
    // workingSpace is NOT stored here — it is app/output config, not part of the look.
    // (Keeps the document portable across a future sRGB→P3 switch; see colorSeam.)
}

/// RAW develop knobs == CIRAWFilter native properties (macOS 12+, confirmed in SDK header).
/// Optionals mean "leave CIRAWFilter at its decoder default". `.neutral` = all defaults
/// = byte-identical to today's CIRAWFilter(imageURL:).outputImage.
struct RAWDevelopSettings: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    var exposure: Double?            = nil   // .exposure (stops)
    var neutralTemperature: Double?  = nil   // .neutralTemperature (Kelvin)
    var neutralTint: Double?         = nil   // .neutralTint
    var shadowBias: Double?          = nil   // .shadowBias
    var boostAmount: Double?         = nil   // .boostAmount
    var boostShadowAmount: Double?   = nil   // .boostShadowAmount
    var contrastAmount: Double?      = nil   // .contrastAmount
    var localToneMapAmount: Double?  = nil   // .localToneMapAmount
    var sharpnessAmount: Double?     = nil   // gate on isSharpnessSupported (NB: SDK uses *Supported)
    var detailAmount: Double?        = nil
    var colorNoiseReductionAmount: Double?     = nil
    var luminanceNoiseReductionAmount: Double? = nil
    var moireReductionAmount: Double?          = nil
    var extendedDynamicRangeAmount: Double?    = nil   // gate on isEnableEDRModeAvailable
    var enableEDR: Bool?             = nil   // .isEDRModeEnabled
    var isGamutMappingEnabled: Bool? = nil

    static let neutral = RAWDevelopSettings()   // ALL nil → decoder defaults

    /// Applies ONLY user-touched keys; consults availability/support flags per loaded image.
    func apply(to f: CIRAWFilter) {
        if let v = exposure                 { f.exposure = Float(v) }
        if let v = neutralTemperature       { f.neutralTemperature = Float(v) }
        if let v = neutralTint              { f.neutralTint = Float(v) }
        if let v = shadowBias               { f.shadowBias = Float(v) }
        if let v = boostAmount              { f.boostAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = boostShadowAmount        { f.boostShadowAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = contrastAmount           { f.contrastAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = localToneMapAmount       { f.localToneMapAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = sharpnessAmount,            f.isSharpnessSupported { f.sharpnessAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = detailAmount             { f.detailAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = colorNoiseReductionAmount     { f.colorNoiseReductionAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = luminanceNoiseReductionAmount { f.luminanceNoiseReductionAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = moireReductionAmount,       f.isMoireReductionSupported { f.moireReductionAmount = Float(v) }
        if let on = enableEDR, f.isEnableEDRModeAvailable { f.isEDRModeEnabled = on }
        if let v = extendedDynamicRangeAmount, f.isEnableEDRModeAvailable { f.extendedDynamicRangeAmount = Float(v) }
        if let g = isGamutMappingEnabled    { f.isGamutMappingEnabled = g }
    }
    var isNeutral: Bool { self == .neutral }
}

/// CLOSED enum (graft: ordered-node idea from A1/A3, but no existentials → free
/// Codable/Equatable/Sendable synthesis, no per-node boxing in the fold).
/// All cases are resolution-INDEPENDENT (normalized units) so preview==export.
enum AdjustmentNode: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    case exposure(ev: Double)                       // CIExposureAdjust
    case colorControls(brightness: Double, contrast: Double, saturation: Double) // CIColorControls
    case highlightShadow(highlights: Double, shadows: Double)  // CIHighlightShadowAdjust
    case temperatureTint(temp: Double, tint: Double)          // CITemperatureAndTint (creative WB)
    case vibrance(amount: Double)                   // CIVibrance
    // Extension point: add a case + a switch arm in apply(to:). No protocol churn.

    func apply(to image: CIImage) -> CIImage {
        switch self {
        case let .exposure(ev):
            return image.applyingFilter("CIExposureAdjust", parameters: [kCIInputEVKey: ev])
        case let .colorControls(b, c, s):
            return image.applyingFilter("CIColorControls",
                parameters: [kCIInputBrightnessKey: b, kCIInputContrastKey: c, kCIInputSaturationKey: s])
        case let .highlightShadow(h, s):
            return image.applyingFilter("CIHighlightShadowAdjust",
                parameters: ["inputHighlightAmount": h, "inputShadowAmount": s])
        case let .temperatureTint(t, tint):
            return image.applyingFilter("CITemperatureAndTint",
                parameters: ["inputTargetNeutral": CIVector(x: t, y: tint)])
        case let .vibrance(a):
            return image.applyingFilter("CIVibrance", parameters: ["inputAmount": a])
        }
    }
}

/// LUT referenced by STABLE ID (graft from A2), never by the non-Codable CubeLUT object.
struct LUTSettings: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    var lutID: LUTID?     = nil      // resolves through LUTLibrary / engine registry
    var intensity: Double = 1.0      // 0...1  (UI shows 0–100%)
    static let none = LUTSettings()
}

/// Stable identity for a LUT (file-backed OR derived-in-memory). UUID-backed.
struct LUTID: Codable, Sendable, Equatable, Hashable { let raw: UUID }
```

### Layer 1b — Source + render-target descriptors (not part of the look)

```swift
/// Describes how to (RE)produce the source CIImage so RAW can be re-developed per render.
/// A URL, not a live CIImage → nothing non-Sendable is captured in app state.
struct ImageSource: Sendable, Equatable {
    enum Kind: Sendable, Equatable { case raw, standard }
    let url: URL
    let kind: Kind          // decided by ONE shared extension set (see openQuestions)
    let nativeExtent: CGSize // cached at open; used to compute preview scaleFactor
}

/// The ONLY difference between a preview render and an export render.
enum RenderScale: Sendable, Equatable {
    case preview(maxSize: CGSize)   // 1600x1200 today (was AppViewModel.maxPreview)
    case full
}
```

### Layer 2 — The color-space seam (single source of truth)

```swift
/// Collapses the 3 hardcoded CGColorSpace.sRGB sites (CubeLUT.makeFilter:135,
/// ImageProcessor.export:163, and the IMPLICIT preview raster at ImageProcessor.swift:92)
/// into ONE value. Default .sRGB; .displayP3 reserved (future-proof, not wired to UI).
enum WorkingSpace: String, Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    case sRGB
    case displayP3
    var cgColorSpace: CGColorSpace {
        switch self {
        case .sRGB:      return CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!
        case .displayP3: return CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.displayP3)!
        }
    }
    static let current: WorkingSpace = .sRGB   // ONE line to flip later
}
```

### Layer 3 — The pure pipeline (ONE function, lazy single CIImage)

```swift
/// Stateless. The ONLY place the look becomes a CIImage. Builds ONE lazy graph —
/// nothing rasterizes between nodes (graft: A3 laziness). Used by BOTH preview and export.
enum RenderPipeline {
    static func buildImage(source: ImageSource,
                           document: EditDocument,
                           lut: CubeLUT?,          // already resolved from document.lut.lutID
                           scale: RenderScale,
                           space: WorkingSpace) -> CIImage? {
        // STAGE 0+1 — SOURCE + RAW DEVELOP (rebuild CIRAWFilter from URL; develop is live)
        var image: CIImage
        switch source.kind {
        case .raw:
            guard let f = CIRAWFilter(imageURL: source.url) else { return nil }
            document.rawDevelop.apply(to: f)                 // user settings ON the edit path
            if case let .preview(maxSize) = scale {           // DOWNSCALE EARLY, RAW-native
                f.scaleFactor = previewScaleFactor(native: source.nativeExtent, max: maxSize)
            }
            guard let out = f.outputImage else { return nil }
            image = out
        case .standard:
            guard let ci = CIImage(contentsOf: source.url) else { return nil }
            image = ci
            if case let .preview(maxSize) = scale { image = earlyDownscale(ci, max: maxSize) }
        }
        // STAGE 2 — ORDERED TONE/COLOR (lazy; resolution-independent nodes)
        for node in document.adjustments { image = node.apply(to: image) }
        // STAGE 3 — LUT WITH INTENSITY (mix in working space; see intensityModel)
        if let lut {
            image = lut.applyWithIntensity(to: image,
                                           intensity: document.lut.intensity,
                                           colorSpace: space.cgColorSpace)
        }
        return image   // STILL un-rendered. Core Image evaluates only at rasterize time.
    }
}
```

### Layer 3b — `CubeLUT` changes (remove hardcoded sRGB, add intensity, cache filter)

```swift
extension CubeLUT {
    /// Build the cube filter ONCE per color space; reuse across slider ticks.
    /// Fixes the per-apply reallocation at CubeLUT.swift:141 (33^3 table).
    func makeFilter(colorSpace: CGColorSpace) -> CIFilter { /* cache keyed by colorSpace.name */ }

    /// Intensity 0...1 blended in the WORKING SPACE (deliberate; new behavior).
    func applyWithIntensity(to input: CIImage, intensity: Double, colorSpace: CGColorSpace) -> CIImage {
        let f = makeFilter(colorSpace: colorSpace)
        f.setValue(input, forKey: kCIInputImageKey)
        guard let graded = f.outputImage else { return input }
        if intensity >= 1 { return graded }
        if intensity <= 0 { return input }
        // CIDissolveTransition at time=intensity blends input→graded linearly in working space.
        return graded.applyingFilter("CIDissolveTransition",
                    parameters: [kCIInputTargetImageKey: input, kCIInputTimeKey: 1.0 - intensity])
               .cropped(to: input.extent)   // guard transition extent growth
    }
}
```

### Layer 4 — The concurrency seam (`actor` owns the only CIContext)

```swift
/// Swift-6 clean: CIImage/CIContext are born and die INSIDE this actor.
/// Only Sendable values cross in (EditDocument, ImageSource, WorkingSpace, RenderScale)
/// and a Sendable CGImage/Data crosses out. Replaces ImageProcessor's GPU duties.
actor RenderEngine {
    static let shared = RenderEngine()
    private let context: CIContext
    private var lutRegistry: [LUTID: CubeLUT] = [:]   // file-backed + derived LUTs by ID

    init() {
        if let dev = MTLCreateSystemDefaultDevice() { context = CIContext(mtlDevice: dev) }
        else { context = CIContext() }
        // NOTE: no explicit kCIContextWorkingColorSpace here — see openQuestions.
    }

    func register(_ lut: CubeLUT, for id: LUTID) { lutRegistry[id] = lut }
    func unregister(_ id: LUTID) { lutRegistry[id] = nil }

    /// ONE definition → scaled preview. Returns CGImage (Sendable to hand back).
    func renderPreview(source: ImageSource, document: EditDocument,
                       maxSize: CGSize, space: WorkingSpace) -> CGImage? {
        let lut = document.lut.lutID.flatMap { lutRegistry[$0] }
        guard let img = RenderPipeline.buildImage(source: source, document: document,
                          lut: lut, scale: .preview(maxSize: maxSize), space: space)
        else { return nil }
        return context.createCGImage(img, from: img.extent,
                                     format: .RGBA8, colorSpace: space.cgColorSpace) // EXPLICIT space
    }

    /// SAME definition → full-res file. Same builder, same context, same space.
    func export(source: ImageSource, document: EditDocument, to url: URL,
                format: ExportFormat, space: WorkingSpace, quality: CGFloat = 0.95) throws {
        let lut = document.lut.lutID.flatMap { lutRegistry[$0] }
        guard let img = RenderPipeline.buildImage(source: source, document: document,
                          lut: lut, scale: .full, space: space)
        else { throw ImageError.processingFailed }
        let cs = space.cgColorSpace
        let data: Data?
        switch format {
        case .tiff: data = context.tiffRepresentation(of: img, format: .RGBA16, colorSpace: cs)
        case .png:  data = context.pngRepresentation(of: img,  format: .RGBA8,  colorSpace: cs)
        case .jpeg: data = context.jpegRepresentation(of: img,  colorSpace: cs,
                       options: [.init(rawValue: kCGImageDestinationLossyCompressionQuality as String): quality])
        }
        guard let d = data else { throw ImageError.exportFailed }
        try d.write(to: url)
    }
}
```

`generateThumbnail` (currently on `ImageProcessor`, used by `FilmstripView`) moves onto `RenderEngine` as `func thumbnail(...) async -> CGImage?` (it already uses a CGImageSource path, so it just needs to live behind the actor).


### Core model
`EditDocument` (Codable, Sendable, Equatable) is THE model and replaces the de-facto render state `sourceImage`/`selectedLUT`/`processedImage` (AppViewModel.swift:13,18,19). It has three fields: `rawDevelop: RAWDevelopSettings`, `adjustments: [AdjustmentNode]`, `lut: LUTSettings`.

Three deliberate modeling decisions, each justified against the real code:

1. ADJUSTMENTS ARE A CLOSED ENUM, NOT `[any AdjustmentNode]`. This is the central graft. The brief's Approach 1 proposes protocol existentials for extensibility; I reject that because an existential array cannot synthesize `Equatable`/`Codable` and needs a manual type-tag coder, which directly undermines the value-state spine that earns us undo + Swift-6 cleanliness. A closed `enum AdjustmentNode` is equally composable/ordered, adds a node in one case + one switch arm, and gets `Codable`/`Equatable`/`Sendable` for free. For a zero-dependency app whose node set is a small, known list of CIFilter wrappers, this is strictly better.

2. RAW DEVELOP IS NOT A NODE IN THE `adjustments` ARRAY — it is a separate `rawDevelop` field consumed only at the source stage. This asymmetry is forced by reality: `CIRAWFilter` must be rebuilt from the URL and its properties set BEFORE `outputImage`; you cannot chain develop onto an already-developed `CIImage`. This is why `ImageSource` carries a URL, not a live CIImage. (Approach 1 reached the same conclusion; Approaches 2/3 also keep develop separate.) `RAWDevelopSettings` uses Optionals where nil = "CIRAWFilter decoder default", so `.neutral` (all-nil) is byte-identical to today's `loadRAW` output.

3. LUT IS REFERENCED BY `LUTID` (UUID), NOT BY THE `CubeLUT` OBJECT. `CubeLUT` holds a non-Codable float `tableData` blob; embedding it would make `EditDocument` non-Codable and bloat every undo snapshot. The `LUTID` resolves through a registry on `RenderEngine` (and `LUTLibrary` for file-backed LUTs). The derived-in-memory LUT from `deriveRecipe` is registered under a fresh `LUTID` so its `EditDocument` resolves — this is the one new wiring task on the derive path.

`WorkingSpace` is intentionally NOT stored in `EditDocument` — it is app/output configuration, not "the look". Keeping it out makes a saved document portable across a future sRGB→P3 default flip. `ImageSource` and `RenderScale` are also outside the document (they are "what/how to render", not "the edit").

Forward-compat: every field has a default and all sub-fields are Optional or have identity defaults, so older saved documents decode cleanly as the schema grows. A `version: Int` field should be added to `EditDocument` before first ship to make future migrations explicit.

### Render model (preview == export)
ONE function renders both: `RenderPipeline.buildImage(source:document:lut:scale:space:) -> CIImage?` (pure, stateless) folds the `EditDocument` into a SINGLE lazy CIImage graph — source → RAW develop → ordered adjustments → LUT(intensity) → output — with NOTHING rasterized between stages (graft from Approach 3; lean entirely on Core Image deferred evaluation). The actor then rasterizes that one image at one of two scales.

Preview and export are the SAME definition, evaluated by the SAME builder, through the SAME CIContext, differing ONLY in the `RenderScale` value and the rasterization call (CGImage vs Data encoder). This makes preview/export parity a STRUCTURAL property, not a convention. Compare today's split: preview re-renders via `renderPreview` while export reuses a cached `processedImage ?? sourceImage` (AppViewModel.swift export path) — two different code paths that merely happen to agree. The new model has one path.

SCALE HANDLING — DOWNSCALE EARLY (fixes a confirmed perf bug):
- RAW: set `CIRAWFilter.scaleFactor` BEFORE `outputImage` (cheaper demosaic at preview size). This replaces the current post-hoc affine `transformed(by:)` at ImageProcessor.swift:90 and means heavy adjustment/LUT nodes operate on ~1600x1200 px, not full extent. Today `applyLUT` builds the full-extent graph and only bounds the final raster (ImageProcessor.swift:90-92) — and worse, runs it inside a `Task {}` that inherits @MainActor, so the whole filter graph executes on the main thread. Moving evaluation into `actor RenderEngine` gets it off the main actor; downscaling early keeps it cheap.
- Standard images: an early Lanczos/affine downscale right after load, before nodes.
- `.full`: scaleFactor = 1 / no downscale; native extent throughout.

RASTERIZATION RETURNS CGImage, NOT BYTES (refinement over Approach 3): `renderPreview` returns `CGImage?`. `CGImage` is reference-counted but immutable and safe to hand back across the actor boundary; the @MainActor caller wraps it with `NSImage(cgImage:size:)` for `previewNSImage`. This avoids Approach 3's `RenderResult` byte-copy on every preview tick. Export returns `Data` written to disk inside the actor (no large image crosses out at all).

RESOLUTION-INDEPENDENCE INVARIANT: every `AdjustmentNode` case must use normalized/extent-relative units (the current set — exposure, color controls, highlight/shadow, temp/tint, vibrance — are all inherently scale-invariant). Any FUTURE pixel-sized node (grain, blur, sharpen) MUST express its radius in normalized units or preview and export diverge. This invariant is the price of early downscaling and must be stated in the node-component spec.

PARITY CAVEAT (must be flagged downstream): `CIRAWFilter.scaleFactor`/draft demosaic can differ subtly from full-quality decode. Mitigation options for the preview component spec: (a) accept the small interaction-time delta, or (b) render interactive previews at draft and settle a final full-quality preview on idle. Default to (a) for v1; (b) is a known enhancement.

### LUT intensity model
LUT intensity is NEW behavior (it does not exist today — `CubeLUT.apply(to:)` at CubeLUT.swift:140 is full-strength only, and `lutIntensity` exists nowhere). It is modeled as a first-class field `LUTSettings.intensity: Double` in 0...1 (UI surfaces 0–100%), serialized in the `EditDocument`.

It is realized as the LAST stage of `RenderPipeline.buildImage` via `CubeLUT.applyWithIntensity(to:intensity:colorSpace:)`: build the graded image with the cube filter, then blend the PRE-LUT input toward the graded output using `CIDissolveTransition` at `time = intensity` (with `.cropped(to: input.extent)` to contain the transition's extent). intensity >= 1 returns graded; intensity <= 0 returns input (both short-circuit, skipping the blend filter).

It is NOT a parameter buried inside `CubeLUT.apply`-the-color-op — it lives in the pipeline as a distinct blend so it is honored IDENTICALLY in preview and export (because both call the same `buildImage`). This is the key contrast with the brief's incorrect assumption that an intensity blend already exists to preserve; there is no prior behavior, so the blend's color space is a deliberate design choice, fixed here.

DELIBERATE CHOICE — BLEND IN THE WORKING SPACE: the dissolve mixes pre/post-LUT in `WorkingSpace.cgColorSpace` (the same space the cube interpolates in), not in linear light. This is a defined, documented behavior. Because it changes the look of every sub-100% LUT, switching it later (e.g. to a linear-light mix) is a visible change and must be treated as a versioned behavior change. Flagged in open questions for visual sign-off.

WHY NOT a separate `IntensityNode` in the `adjustments` array (Approach 1's framing): intensity is intrinsically coupled to the LUT (it blends specifically the pre-LUT vs post-LUT pair), so it belongs with `LUTSettings`, not as a free-floating ordered node that could be placed anywhere. Keeping it adjacent to the LUT reference is both clearer for power users and structurally correct.

PERF: the cube filter is cached on `CubeLUT` keyed by color-space name (fixes the per-tick reallocation at CubeLUT.swift:141); only the lightweight dissolve is rebuilt per intensity change. Cache invalidation on a future P3 switch is automatic because the key includes the color-space name.

### Color-space seam
ONE seam: `WorkingSpace` (enum, default `.sRGB`, `.displayP3` reserved). Its `.cgColorSpace` accessor is the single source of truth, replacing the THREE hardcoded `CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!` sites the brief calls out PLUS the latent fourth (implicit) site:

1. `CubeLUT.makeFilter` inputColorSpace (CubeLUT.swift:135) → `makeFilter(colorSpace:)` receives `space.cgColorSpace`. THIS IS THE LUT INTERPOLATION SPACE.
2. `ImageProcessor.export` colorSpace (ImageProcessor.swift:163) → `RenderEngine.export` passes `space.cgColorSpace` into all three encoders (tiff/png/jpeg). THIS IS THE OUTPUT ENCODING SPACE.
3. THE IMPLICIT FOURTH SITE — `renderPreview`'s `createCGImage` passes NO colorSpace today (ImageProcessor.swift:92), so the preview raster uses the CIContext default while export forces sRGB. This is a real latent preview/export color mismatch. The new `RenderEngine.renderPreview` ALWAYS passes `space.cgColorSpace` to `createCGImage`, finally unifying preview and export rasterization. Fixing this is a correctness requirement, not a nicety.

CRITICAL INVARIANT: the LUT interpolation space (site 1) and the output encoding space (site 2) MUST move in lockstep — they are decoupled literals today that merely both happen to be sRGB. Because both now read the SAME `WorkingSpace` value threaded through `buildImage(...space:)`, a P3 switch updates them atomically; you cannot change one without the other. This is enforced structurally by threading one value, not by discipline.

`WorkingSpace` is NOT stored in `EditDocument` (it is output config, not the look) — `RenderEngine.renderPreview`/`export` take it as a parameter, sourced from `WorkingSpace.current`. Default `.sRGB` satisfies "future-proof for P3 WITHOUT committing to P3 now"; enabling P3 is the one-line `static let current` change plus addressing the 8-bit-gamut open question.

The two force-unwraps the brief flags (ImageProcessor.swift:163, RecipeExtractor.swift:81) funnel through the single `cgColorSpace` accessor; both `CGColorSpace.sRGB` and `CGColorSpace.displayP3` are guaranteed-resolvable system names, so the force-unwrap is safe and localized to one place.

EXPLICITLY OUT OF THIS SEAM: I do NOT set `kCIContextWorkingColorSpace`/`kCIContextWorkingFormat` on the CIContext in this binding decision (Approach 3 did). That changes intermediate precision for every render and the working-space primaries, with out-of-gamut risk on the 8-bit export path — see open questions. The seam governs INPUT-to-LUT and OUTPUT spaces; the CIContext working space stays at the CI default for now.

### Derive integration
Derive keeps its neutral baseline by PHYSICAL DECOUPLING, not by shared parameters. `RecipeExtractor` (a struct, RecipeExtractor.swift:73) never imports `EditDocument`, never references `RAWDevelopSettings` (except as the documented contract value), and never calls `RenderEngine` or `RenderPipeline`. It therefore CANNOT inherit the user's develop settings — the hard constraint is satisfied by construction, the strongest possible guarantee.

Concretely, three things stay exactly as they are in the real code:
1. Its OWN neutral RAW baseline: `derive` continues to open `CIRAWFilter(imageURL: rawURL).outputImage` at decoder DEFAULTS (RecipeExtractor.swift:86-89). This is, by definition, equal to `RAWDevelopSettings.neutral.apply(to:)` (which sets no keys). To make the contract explicit and catch future drift, add a debug assertion / doc comment naming `RAWDevelopSettings.neutral` as the baseline definition. This also retires the comment-only duplication the maps flag between `loadRAW` and the derive baseline: both are now documented as "the `.neutral` render", even though derive deliberately keeps its own call site.
2. Its OWN CIContext: `derive` keeps `makeContext` / its separate context (RecipeExtractor.swift, ~419). It does NOT use `RenderEngine`'s actor context. This is intentional and preserved — the derive fitting pipeline (lanczos-scale RAW to JPG, box-blur edge mask, render to RGBA8 byte buffers, bin source→target into the N^3 cube) is a batch sampling job, not an interactive render, and must not contend with or depend on the interactive engine.
3. Its OWN sampling color space: derive renders its sample buffers in sRGB (RecipeExtractor.swift:81). DECISION: derive's sampling space stays sRGB and is NOT routed through `WorkingSpace.current`. Rationale and the subtle correctness point: a derived `.cube` encodes a transform FROM the baseline-render space TO the JPEG space, and is later APPLIED by `CubeLUT.makeFilter` in `WorkingSpace.current`. For the derived LUT to be self-consistent, the space it is FIT in must equal the space it is APPLIED in. Today both are sRGB. If `WorkingSpace.current` flips to P3 LATER, derive must fit in P3 too, OR the derived LUT must record the space it was built in so apply can match. v1 keeps everything sRGB so derive-fit-space == apply-space trivially; the P3 future requires stamping the build space onto the LUT (noted in open questions). The safe v1 rule: derive's render space is sRGB; if/when P3 is enabled app-wide, revisit derive in lockstep — do NOT blindly thread `WorkingSpace.current` into derive's sampler, because the neutral RAW baseline it fits against is itself an sRGB-default render.

The ONE new wiring task: `deriveRecipe` builds an in-memory `CubeLUT` and currently `selectLUT`s it. Now it must `RenderEngine.register(lut, for: newLUTID)` and set `document.lut.lutID = newLUTID` so the live-preview `EditDocument` resolves the derived LUT through the registry. This is small and localized.

### Concurrency model
Strategy: make the GPU the only thing behind an isolation boundary, and make everything that crosses the boundary a Sendable VALUE. This is why the value-state spine (Approach 2) is the right foundation for this specific codebase.

1. `actor RenderEngine` owns the SINGLE Metal `CIContext`. This replaces `ImageProcessor`'s GPU duties. `CIImage`, `CIFilter`, and `CIContext` (all non-Sendable) are created and consumed ENTIRELY inside the actor — they never escape. Inputs crossing in are all Sendable value types (`EditDocument`, `ImageSource`, `WorkingSpace`, `RenderScale`, `URL`, `LUTID`). Outputs crossing out are `CGImage?` (preview) or `Void`/`Data` written to disk (export) — `CGImage` is safe to hand back; no large mutable buffer crosses on the hot path.

This directly dissolves the confirmed hazards: today `ImageProcessor` is a non-Sendable `final class` singleton (ImageProcessor.swift:9,16) holding a `CIContext`, captured into `Task.detached` in `exportDialog` (`[processor, exportFormat]`), reached from `RecipeExtractor`'s caller, and used from `FilmstripView.generateThumbnail`. Under Swift 6 strict concurrency these are data-race errors. With the actor, `exportDialog` becomes `try await RenderEngine.shared.export(...)` across a clean boundary; the `[processor]` capture and the `MainActor.run` hops disappear. `generateThumbnail` moves onto the actor as an `async` method, fixing the FilmstripView path too.

2. `ImageProcessor` is dissolved. Its non-GPU helpers (extension/type predicates, `ExportFormat` enum, `ImageError`) move to free functions / stay as value types. There is NO non-Sendable singleton left.

3. AppViewModel stays `@MainActor`. `@Published var document: EditDocument` (Sendable value) replaces the `sourceImage`/`selectedLUT`/`processedImage` triple. The current preview path runs the filter graph on the main thread (today's `applyLUT` uses a `Task {}` that inherits @MainActor — a confirmed responsiveness bug). The new path: a debounced `renderTask` calls `await RenderEngine.shared.renderPreview(...)` OFF the main actor, then writes `previewNSImage = NSImage(cgImage:size:)` back ON the main actor. The existing single `previewTask?.cancel()` pattern is kept but upgraded: because evaluation is now off-actor and the engine can check `Task.isCancelled` between stages, cancellation actually saves work (today it only saves the final assignment).

4. Debounce + change detection: `EditDocument: Equatable` lets the VM coalesce rapid slider changes (power users WILL drag develop/intensity sliders continuously) into a debounced render, which the current code entirely lacks. `showOriginal` (the Space-hold A/B) stops re-rasterizing from scratch (current behavior re-runs `renderPreview` on every key event); instead it caches two `NSImage`s (graded + at-intensity-0 / original) and swaps them, or renders an `EditDocument` with `lut.intensity = 0`.

5. `RecipeExtractor` concurrency is UNCHANGED and stays separate: it remains a value-type struct invoked from `Task.detached`, with its own context. Because it touches no shared mutable singleton anymore (it never used `RenderEngine`), its detached usage is clean. Its inputs are URLs + an options value — all Sendable.

6. Swift 6 readiness posture: the project pbxproj sets no explicit `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY` or `SWIFT_VERSION` override today (defaults apply), so this design is forward-looking. The actor-isolation + value-state model is what makes flipping on strict concurrency (or migrating to the Swift 6 language mode) a non-event rather than a rewrite. No `@unchecked Sendable` escape hatches are needed anywhere — every boundary crossing is provably safe.

## 3. Component specs

### Pipeline core (node model + single render path)

**Verdict:** major-issues (concurrency OK · color FLAG · preview/export OK)

**Summary.** Defines the value-state spine and the ONE render function for LUTzy Phase 2. Introduces three new files: EditDocument.swift (the Codable/Sendable look: RAWDevelopSettings + closed-enum AdjustmentNode array + LUTSettings), RenderTypes.swift (WorkingSpace color seam, ImageSource, RenderScale, LUTID, ImageError move), and RenderPipeline.swift (the pure `buildImage(...) -> CIImage?` fold + CubeLUT intensity helper). It modifies CubeLUT (parameterize makeFilter on colorSpace, add applyWithIntensity, add LUTID, filter cache), and is consumed by the actor RenderEngine (separate component) and AppViewModel (separate component). RecipeExtractor stays physically decoupled — it already shares only ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral and its own sRGB literal, both preserved. This spec covers ONLY the model + pure render path; the actor/concurrency rewrite and the SwiftUI/ViewModel wiring are separate components and are referenced, not specified here. NOTE vs brief: the claimed PreviewView `preview`-undefined FIXME bug does NOT exist in the real file, and the neutral-baseline duplication is ALREADY fixed via ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral — both corrected below.

**Implementation:**

## Component: Pipeline core (node model + single render path)

Scope: the **value-state model** and the **one pure render function**. Out of scope (separate components, referenced only): `actor RenderEngine` (concurrency/rasterization), the `AppViewModel`/SwiftUI wiring, and the RAW-develop *UI*. This component is pure data + one pure function; it imports `CoreImage`/`CoreGraphics`/`simd` only and has zero AppKit/SwiftUI dependency, which keeps it trivially testable and `Sendable`.

### Corrections to the brief (verified against the real files)
1. The brief/maps claim `PreviewView.sideBySideView` references an undefined `preview` with a FIXME. **It does not** — the real `PreviewView.swift:52-57` correctly passes `viewModel.previewNSImage`. No such bug exists; nothing to do here.
2. The brief claims the neutral RAW baseline is duplicated between `loadRAW` and `RecipeExtractor`. **Already fixed**: `ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral(at:)` (ImageProcessor.swift:74) is a single shared static; `RecipeExtractor.derive` already calls it (RecipeExtractor.swift:89). The derive-decoupling design below builds on this existing seam, it does not need to create it.
3. The extension sets are **already consolidated** in `ImageProcessor` (`rawExtensions`/`standardExtensions`/`supportedExtensions`, lines 19-32). The only remaining duplicate is `ImageCollection.isImageFile` (ImageCollection.swift:77). `ImageSource.Kind` classification (below) reuses `ImageProcessor.rawExtensions`; folding `ImageCollection` onto it is a small follow-up (open question), not required for this component.

---

### File 1 — NEW `LUTzy/Models/EditDocument.swift`

```swift
import CoreImage
import CoreGraphics
import simd

// MARK: - EditDocument — THE single source of truth for "the look"

/// Replaces the implicit `sourceImage` + `selectedLUT` + `processedImage`
/// triple on AppViewModel (AppViewModel.swift:13,18,19). A pure value:
/// Codable → undo/redo, copy-look, per-image persistence; Equatable → cheap
/// change-detection to drive a render debounce; Sendable → crosses the
/// RenderEngine actor boundary with no data-race risk.
///
/// NOTE: `workingSpace` is deliberately NOT stored here — it is app/output
/// configuration (see WorkingSpace), not part of a portable look.
struct EditDocument: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    /// Schema version. Bump when adding fields that need migration logic.
    /// All fields below already have defaults, so older payloads decode cleanly,
    /// but an explicit version makes future migrations intentional.
    var version: Int = 1

    /// RAW develop knobs. Only consulted when the source is RAW. `.neutral`
    /// (all-nil) reproduces today's `ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral` output
    /// byte-for-byte (it sets no CIRAWFilter properties).
    var rawDevelop: RAWDevelopSettings = .neutral

    /// Ordered, resolution-INDEPENDENT tone/color stages applied AFTER RAW
    /// develop and BEFORE the LUT. Order is the array order (index 0 first).
    var adjustments: [AdjustmentNode] = []

    /// LUT reference (by stable ID, never the CubeLUT object) + intensity.
    var lut: LUTSettings = .none

    static let identity = EditDocument()

    /// True when this document is a pure pass-through (see "Identity" section).
    var isIdentity: Bool {
        rawDevelop.isNeutral && adjustments.isEmpty && lut.isIdentity
    }
}

// MARK: - RAWDevelopSettings — Optional map onto CIRAWFilter native properties

/// Each property is Optional; `nil` means "leave CIRAWFilter at its decoder
/// default". Therefore `.neutral` (all nil) == current neutral baseline.
/// Property names mirror the macOS-12+ CIRAWFilter native API. Availability/
/// support flags are consulted in `apply(to:)` where the SDK provides one.
struct RAWDevelopSettings: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    var exposure: Double?                       // .exposure (stops, -10...10)
    var neutralTemperature: Double?             // .neutralTemperature (Kelvin)
    var neutralTint: Double?                    // .neutralTint (-150...150)
    var shadowBias: Double?                     // .shadowBias (0...1)
    var boostAmount: Double?                    // .boostAmount (1.0 default, 0=off)
    var boostShadowAmount: Double?              // .boostShadowAmount
    var contrastAmount: Double?                 // .contrastAmount
    var localToneMapAmount: Double?             // .localToneMapAmount (0...1)
    var sharpnessAmount: Double?                // .sharpnessAmount  (gate: isSharpnessSupported)
    var detailAmount: Double?                   // .detailAmount (0...3)
    var colorNoiseReductionAmount: Double?      // .colorNoiseReductionAmount
    var luminanceNoiseReductionAmount: Double?  // .luminanceNoiseReductionAmount
    var moireReductionAmount: Double?           // .moireReductionAmount (gate: isMoireReductionSupported)
    var enableEDR: Bool?                        // .isEDRModeEnabled (gate: isEnableEDRModeAvailable)
    var extendedDynamicRangeAmount: Double?     // .extendedDynamicRangeAmount (0...2, same gate)
    var isGamutMappingEnabled: Bool?           // .isGamutMappingEnabled (default true)

    static let neutral = RAWDevelopSettings()
    var isNeutral: Bool { self == .neutral }

    /// Applies ONLY user-touched (non-nil) keys to a live CIRAWFilter,
    /// consulting availability/support flags where the SDK exposes one.
    /// Called at the SOURCE stage of RenderPipeline.buildImage for RAW inputs.
    @available(macOS 12.0, *)
    func apply(to f: CIRAWFilter) {
        if let v = exposure                      { f.exposure = Float(v) }
        if let v = neutralTemperature            { f.neutralTemperature = Float(v) }
        if let v = neutralTint                   { f.neutralTint = Float(v) }
        if let v = shadowBias                    { f.shadowBias = Float(v) }
        if let v = boostAmount                   { f.boostAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = boostShadowAmount             { f.boostShadowAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = contrastAmount                { f.contrastAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = localToneMapAmount            { f.localToneMapAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = detailAmount                  { f.detailAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = colorNoiseReductionAmount     { f.colorNoiseReductionAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = luminanceNoiseReductionAmount { f.luminanceNoiseReductionAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = sharpnessAmount, f.isSharpnessSupported     { f.sharpnessAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = moireReductionAmount, f.isMoireReductionSupported { f.moireReductionAmount = Float(v) }
        if f.isEnableEDRModeAvailable {
            if let on = enableEDR                { f.isEDRModeEnabled = on }
            if let v = extendedDynamicRangeAmount { f.extendedDynamicRangeAmount = Float(v) }
        }
        if let g = isGamutMappingEnabled         { f.isGamutMappingEnabled = g }
    }
}
```

> NOTE on the SDK gate names: the brief flagged uncertainty (`*Available` vs `*Supported`). Use whichever the SDK header declares for each property (e.g. `isSharpnessSupported`, `isMoireReductionSupported`, `isEnableEDRModeAvailable`). The RAW-develop UI component owns the full availability matrix; this component just guards the writes. If a given gate name is wrong, the compiler will reject it — there is no silent failure.

```swift
// MARK: - AdjustmentNode — CLOSED enum of ordered tone/color stages

/// Closed enum (NOT `[any AdjustmentNode]`): gives ordered composability with
/// FREE Codable/Equatable/Sendable synthesis and no per-node boxing in the
/// fold. Add a stage = one case + one switch arm.
///
/// INVARIANT (load-bearing for preview/export parity): every case MUST be
/// resolution-INDEPENDENT (no pixel-sized radii). The current set is inherently
/// scale-invariant. A future pixel-sized node (grain/blur/sharpen) MUST express
/// its radius in normalized units (fraction of min(extent.w, extent.h)) or the
/// downscaled preview and full-res export diverge. See RenderScale.
enum AdjustmentNode: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    /// Exposure in stops. CIExposureAdjust, key kCIInputEVKey. Identity ev == 0.
    case exposure(ev: Double)
    /// CIColorControls. Identity: brightness 0, contrast 1, saturation 1.
    case colorControls(brightness: Double, contrast: Double, saturation: Double)
    /// CIHighlightShadowAdjust. Identity: highlights 1, shadows 0.
    case highlightShadow(highlights: Double, shadows: Double)
    /// Creative white balance. CITemperatureAndTint. Identity neutral = (6500, 0).
    case temperatureTint(temp: Double, tint: Double)
    /// CIVibrance. Identity amount == 0.
    case vibrance(amount: Double)

    /// Applies this stage to a lazy CIImage and returns a lazy CIImage.
    /// Nothing rasterizes here — Core Image defers until the engine renders.
    func apply(to image: CIImage) -> CIImage {
        switch self {
        case let .exposure(ev):
            return image.applyingFilter("CIExposureAdjust",
                parameters: [kCIInputEVKey: ev])
        case let .colorControls(b, c, s):
            return image.applyingFilter("CIColorControls",
                parameters: [kCIInputBrightnessKey: b,
                             kCIInputContrastKey: c,
                             kCIInputSaturationKey: s])
        case let .highlightShadow(h, s):
            return image.applyingFilter("CIHighlightShadowAdjust",
                parameters: ["inputHighlightAmount": h,
                             "inputShadowAmount": s])
        case let .temperatureTint(t, tint):
            // CITemperatureAndTint maps inputNeutral -> inputTargetNeutral as
            // (temp, tint) CIVectors. Source neutral fixed at D65 (6500,0).
            return image.applyingFilter("CITemperatureAndTint",
                parameters: ["inputNeutral": CIVector(x: 6500, y: 0),
                             "inputTargetNeutral": CIVector(x: t, y: tint)])
        case let .vibrance(a):
            return image.applyingFilter("CIVibrance",
                parameters: ["inputAmount": a])
        }
    }

    /// True if this node, with its current parameters, is a no-op. Used so an
    /// "added but untouched" slider doesn't perturb the image (and so the
    /// engine can skip the filter entirely — see ordering rules).
    var isIdentity: Bool {
        switch self {
        case let .exposure(ev):                 return ev == 0
        case let .colorControls(b, c, s):       return b == 0 && c == 1 && s == 1
        case let .highlightShadow(h, s):        return h == 1 && s == 0
        case let .temperatureTint(t, tint):     return t == 6500 && tint == 0
        case let .vibrance(a):                  return a == 0
        }
    }
}

// MARK: - LUTSettings + LUTID

/// LUT referenced by stable ID, never by the non-Codable CubeLUT (CubeLUT holds
/// a non-Codable float `tableData` blob — embedding it would break Codable and
/// bloat undo snapshots). The ID resolves through RenderEngine's registry
/// (and LUTLibrary for file-backed LUTs).
struct LUTSettings: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    var lutID: LUTID? = nil
    var intensity: Double = 1.0   // clamped 0...1 by the UI; pipeline clamps too
    static let none = LUTSettings()

    /// Pure pass-through when there is no LUT OR intensity is 0.
    var isIdentity: Bool { lutID == nil || intensity <= 0 }
}

/// Stable identity for a LUT (file-backed or derived-in-memory). UUID-backed
/// so derived LUTs and re-scanned file LUTs both get a durable handle.
struct LUTID: Codable, Sendable, Equatable, Hashable {
    let raw: UUID
    init(_ raw: UUID = UUID()) { self.raw = raw }
}
```

### File 2 — NEW `LUTzy/Models/RenderTypes.swift`

```swift
import CoreGraphics
import Foundation

// MARK: - WorkingSpace — the SINGLE color-space seam

/// Collapses the hardcoded `CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!` sites the
/// brief calls out — CubeLUT.makeFilter (CubeLUT.swift:135), ImageProcessor.export
/// (ImageProcessor.swift:180), and the IMPLICIT preview-raster site
/// (ImageProcessor.swift:109, where createCGImage passes NO colorSpace) — into
/// ONE value. The LUT interpolation space and the output encoding space now read
/// the SAME `WorkingSpace`, so a future P3 switch moves them atomically
/// (structurally enforced, not by discipline).
///
/// `current` defaults to .sRGB — satisfies "future-proof for P3 without
/// committing to P3 now". Flipping to P3 is this one line PLUS the 8-bit-gamut
/// and derive-build-space open questions.
///
/// NOT routed into RecipeExtractor's own sRGB literal (RecipeExtractor.swift:81):
/// a derived .cube is FIT in sRGB and must be APPLIED in the same space it was
/// fit in; keeping derive on sRGB while apply==sRGB is correct today. See derive note.
enum WorkingSpace: String, Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    case sRGB
    case displayP3

    var cgColorSpace: CGColorSpace {
        switch self {
        case .sRGB:      return CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!
        case .displayP3: return CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.displayP3)!
        }
    }

    /// One line to flip app-wide later.
    static let current: WorkingSpace = .sRGB
}

// MARK: - ImageSource — how to (RE)produce the source CIImage

/// A URL + classification, NOT a live CIImage. Keeping a URL (Sendable) instead
/// of a CIImage means RAW can be re-developed per render (CIRAWFilter must be
/// rebuilt from the URL with develop properties set BEFORE outputImage — you
/// cannot chain develop onto an already-developed CIImage), and nothing
/// non-Sendable lives in app state.
struct ImageSource: Sendable, Equatable {
    enum Kind: Sendable, Equatable { case raw, standard }
    let url: URL
    let kind: Kind
    /// Native pixel extent, cached at open time, used to compute the RAW
    /// preview scaleFactor (early downscale). For standard images it is the
    /// decoded extent.
    let nativeExtent: CGSize

    /// Classify by the EXISTING canonical RAW set (ImageProcessor.rawExtensions).
    /// Do NOT introduce a new extension list here.
    static func kind(for url: URL) -> Kind {
        ImageProcessor.rawExtensions.contains(url.pathExtension.lowercased()) ? .raw : .standard
    }
}
```

> `ImageProcessor.rawExtensions` is currently `private static`. Change it to `static` (drop `private`) so `ImageSource` can read it — this keeps a single source of truth and is a one-word edit. (The in-memory Photos-import path has no URL; that path is the ViewModel component's concern — it will build an `ImageSource`-less render or wrap the data in a temp file. Flagged in open questions.)

```swift
// MARK: - RenderScale — the ONLY difference between preview and export

/// Preview vs export differ ONLY in this value (and in the final rasterize call:
/// CGImage vs Data). Same EditDocument, same buildImage, same WorkingSpace.
enum RenderScale: Sendable, Equatable {
    /// Fit within maxSize (no upscaling). 1600x1200 today (was AppViewModel.maxPreview:249).
    case preview(maxSize: CGSize)
    case full
}

// MARK: - ImageError (relocated from ImageProcessor so it is Sendable & shared)

/// Moved out of ImageProcessor (ImageProcessor.swift:219) so the pure pipeline
/// and the actor can both throw it across isolation boundaries.
enum ImageError: LocalizedError, Sendable {
    case cannotLoad(String)
    case processingFailed
    case exportFailed
    var errorDescription: String? {
        switch self {
        case .cannotLoad(let name): return "Cannot load \(name)"
        case .processingFailed:     return "Image processing failed"
        case .exportFailed:         return "Export failed"
        }
    }
}
```

### File 3 — NEW `LUTzy/Models/RenderPipeline.swift` (the ONE render function)

```swift
import CoreImage
import CoreImage.CIFilterBuiltins
import CoreGraphics

/// Stateless. The ONLY place an EditDocument becomes a CIImage. Builds ONE lazy
/// graph: source → RAW develop → ordered adjustments → LUT(intensity) → output.
/// Nothing rasterizes between stages — Core Image evaluates only when the engine
/// calls createCGImage / *Representation. Used by BOTH preview and export, which
/// makes preview/export parity a STRUCTURAL property, not a convention.
enum RenderPipeline {

    /// - Parameters:
    ///   - lut: already resolved from `document.lut.lutID` by the caller
    ///          (RenderEngine looks it up in its registry). Pure function stays
    ///          pure: it does not touch any registry.
    /// - Returns: a lazy, un-rendered CIImage, or nil if the source can't decode.
    static func buildImage(source: ImageSource,
                           document: EditDocument,
                           lut: CubeLUT?,
                           scale: RenderScale,
                           space: WorkingSpace) -> CIImage? {

        // STAGE 0+1 — SOURCE + RAW DEVELOP (rebuild CIRAWFilter from URL).
        var image: CIImage
        switch source.kind {
        case .raw:
            guard #available(macOS 12.0, *),
                  let f = CIRAWFilter(imageURL: source.url) else {
                // Fallback path: neutral develop only (no user knobs on <12).
                guard let ci = ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral(at: source.url) else { return nil }
                image = (scale.isPreview ? earlyDownscale(ci, maxSize: scale.maxSize!) : ci)
                break
            }
            document.rawDevelop.apply(to: f)               // user knobs on the EDIT path
            if case let .preview(maxSize) = scale {        // DOWNSCALE EARLY, RAW-native
                f.scaleFactor = previewScaleFactor(native: source.nativeExtent, maxSize: maxSize)
            }
            guard let out = f.outputImage else { return nil }
            image = out

        case .standard:
            guard let ci = CIImage(contentsOf: source.url) else { return nil }
            image = (scale.isPreview ? earlyDownscale(ci, maxSize: scale.maxSize!) : ci)
        }

        // STAGE 2 — ORDERED TONE/COLOR (lazy; resolution-independent).
        // Skip identity nodes so an untouched slider is a true no-op.
        for node in document.adjustments where !node.isIdentity {
            image = node.apply(to: image)
        }

        // STAGE 3 — LUT WITH INTENSITY (blend in working space; see CubeLUT below).
        if let lut, !document.lut.isIdentity {
            image = lut.applyWithIntensity(to: image,
                                           intensity: document.lut.intensity,
                                           colorSpace: space.cgColorSpace)
        }

        return image   // still lazy
    }

    // MARK: - Scale helpers

    /// scaleFactor for CIRAWFilter so the demosaiced output fits maxSize,
    /// clamped to ≤1 (never upscale). This replaces the post-hoc affine scale
    /// at ImageProcessor.swift:107 and means heavy nodes run at ~preview size.
    static func previewScaleFactor(native: CGSize, maxSize: CGSize) -> Float {
        guard native.width > 0, native.height > 0 else { return 1 }
        let s = min(maxSize.width / native.width, maxSize.height / native.height, 1.0)
        return Float(s)
    }

    /// Early Lanczos downscale for standard (non-RAW) images, applied right
    /// after decode and BEFORE any node, so nodes operate on preview-sized px.
    static func earlyDownscale(_ image: CIImage, maxSize: CGSize) -> CIImage {
        let e = image.extent
        guard e.width > 0, e.height > 0 else { return image }
        let s = min(maxSize.width / e.width, maxSize.height / e.height, 1.0)
        if s >= 1 { return image }
        let f = CIFilter.lanczosScaleTransform()
        f.inputImage = image
        f.scale = Float(s)
        f.aspectRatio = 1
        return f.outputImage ?? image.transformed(by: CGAffineTransform(scaleX: s, y: s))
    }
}

private extension RenderScale {
    var isPreview: Bool { if case .preview = self { return true }; return false }
    var maxSize: CGSize? { if case let .preview(m) = self { return m }; return nil }
}
```

### File 4 — MODIFIED `LUTzy/Models/CubeLUT.swift`

Three edits: add a stable `LUTID`, parameterize the color space (remove the hardcoded sRGB at line 135), add the intensity blend, and cache the cube filter (fixes per-apply reallocation at line 141).

```swift
// 1) Add a stable identity alongside the existing `id: String`.
//    `id` (file path) stays for Identifiable/Hashable; lutID is the registry key.
let lutID: LUTID = LUTID()           // initialized in BOTH inits

// 2) BEFORE (CubeLUT.swift:131-137):
//    func makeFilter() -> CIFilter? {
//        guard let filter = CIFilter(name: "CIColorCubeWithColorSpace") else { return nil }
//        filter.setValue(size, forKey: "inputCubeDimension")
//        filter.setValue(tableData as NSData, forKey: "inputCubeData")
//        filter.setValue(CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!, forKey: "inputColorSpace")  // HARDCODED
//        return filter
//    }
//
//    AFTER — color space injected; filter cached per color-space name so live
//    sliders don't reallocate a 33^3 (~35,937×4 float) filter every tick:
private final class FilterCache: @unchecked Sendable {   // boxed mutable cache; see note
    var bySpace: [String: CIFilter] = [:]
}
private let filterCache = FilterCache()

func makeFilter(colorSpace: CGColorSpace) -> CIFilter? {
    let key = colorSpace.name as String? ?? "unknown"
    if let cached = filterCache.bySpace[key] { return cached }
    guard let filter = CIFilter(name: "CIColorCubeWithColorSpace") else { return nil }
    filter.setValue(size, forKey: "inputCubeDimension")
    filter.setValue(tableData as NSData, forKey: "inputCubeData")
    filter.setValue(colorSpace, forKey: "inputColorSpace")
    filterCache.bySpace[key] = filter
    return filter
}

// 3) Intensity blend — NEW behavior (no prior to preserve). Blend pre-LUT vs
//    post-LUT in the WORKING space via CIDissolveTransition. Short-circuits at
//    the extremes so intensity 0/1 skip the blend filter entirely.
func applyWithIntensity(to input: CIImage,
                        intensity: Double,
                        colorSpace: CGColorSpace) -> CIImage {
    let t = max(0, min(1, intensity))
    if t <= 0 { return input }
    guard let f = makeFilter(colorSpace: colorSpace) else { return input }
    f.setValue(input, forKey: kCIInputImageKey)
    guard let graded = f.outputImage else { return input }
    if t >= 1 { return graded }
    // Dissolve from `input` (time 0) toward `graded` (time 1): time == intensity.
    let blended = graded.applyingFilter("CIDissolveTransition", parameters: [
        kCIInputTargetImageKey: input,
        kCIInputTimeKey: 1.0 - t
    ])
    // Dissolve can grow the extent; clamp back to the source rect.
    return blended.cropped(to: input.extent)
}
```

> **Cache concurrency note:** `CubeLUT` is a `struct` and the cube filter is now built lazily and cached. Because the engine is an `actor`, all `applyWithIntensity` calls happen on the actor's executor — there is no real concurrent access. The `@unchecked Sendable` box is the pragmatic seam so `CubeLUT` remains `Sendable` for the registry; document this. Alternative if you want zero `@unchecked`: drop the cache and rebuild the filter each call (simpler, slightly slower on big cubes) — acceptable for v1 if you prefer. The spec recommends the cache; either is correct.

> **`CubeLUT.apply(to:)` (line 140):** keep as a thin shim `apply(to:) { applyWithIntensity(to:$0, intensity:1, colorSpace: WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace) }` during migration, OR delete once `AppViewModel.applyLUT` and the engine no longer call it. The derive path (`RecipeExtractor`) does NOT use `apply` — it only fits the cube — so removing `apply` does not affect derive.

> **`makeFilter()` old signature (line 131):** no remaining caller after this change (it was only used by `apply`). Delete the no-arg version to force every call site through the color-space seam.

### File 6 — MODIFIED `LUTzy/Models/RecipeExtractor.swift`

Its private `sRGB` (line 81) and own `CIContext` (line 421) stay. Derive's sampling space is intentionally NOT `WorkingSpace.current` (a derived `.cube` is fit-in-sRGB and applied-in-sRGB; they must match — see derive note in openQuestions for the P3 follow-up).

---

### Ordering rules (precise, normative)
1. **Fixed stage order**: SOURCE → RAW develop → adjustments[0..n] → LUT(+intensity) → output. This is hard-coded in `buildImage`; only the *contents/order of `adjustments`* is data-driven.
2. **Within `adjustments`**: applied in array order, index 0 first. Reordering the array reorders the render. The UI owns reordering; the pipeline just folds.
3. **RAW develop is NOT in `adjustments`** — it is the separate `rawDevelop` field consumed only at the source stage, because `CIRAWFilter` must be configured before `outputImage` (you cannot re-develop an already-developed CIImage).
4. **Intensity is NOT a free-floating node** — it lives in `LUTSettings` and is applied at the LUT stage, because it blends specifically the pre-LUT vs post-LUT pair.
5. **Identity skipping**: nodes where `isIdentity == true` are skipped (no filter added); `lut.isIdentity` skips the LUT stage. This guarantees an untouched-but-present control is a true no-op and avoids needless filters in the graph.

### Working color space — where it lives
Exactly one place: `WorkingSpace` (`RenderTypes.swift`). It is threaded as the `space:` parameter of `buildImage`, which forwards it to `CubeLUT.makeFilter(colorSpace:)` (interpolation space) and to `CubeLUT.applyWithIntensity` (blend space). The **engine** (separate component) reads `WorkingSpace.current` and passes the *same* value to both `createCGImage(...colorSpace:)` (preview raster — fixing the implicit-default site at ImageProcessor.swift:109) and the export encoders (ImageProcessor.swift:180). Because one value feeds interpolation + blend + raster + encode, they cannot diverge. `WorkingSpace` is **not** stored in `EditDocument` (it is output config, not the look).

### How an empty pipeline is identity (exact semantics)
`EditDocument.identity` (== `EditDocument()`): `rawDevelop == .neutral`, `adjustments == []`, `lut == .none`. Threading it through `buildImage`:
- RAW source: `RAWDevelopSettings.neutral.apply` sets **no** CIRAWFilter properties → `outputImage` is identical to today's `developRAWNeutral` output. (For `.full` scale, `scaleFactor` is left untouched = 1, so a full-res identity render equals the current neutral RAW decode.)
- Standard source: `CIImage(contentsOf:)` unchanged; `earlyDownscale` only runs for `.preview` and only when fit < 1 (pure downscale, no color change).
- adjustments loop: empty → skipped.
- LUT stage: `lut.isIdentity` (no `lutID`) → skipped.

Result: the output CIImage equals the decoded source with at most a downscale for preview. `EditDocument.isIdentity` lets the engine/VM short-circuit to "show the source preview" exactly like today's `applyLUT` does when `selectedLUT == nil` (AppViewModel.swift:230-234). **Parity guarantee**: identity render at `.full` == current export of an un-LUT'd image (modulo the now-explicit color tag), and identity at `.preview` == current original preview.

### Before/after at the call site (for the consuming components — shown for orientation only)
```swift
// BEFORE — AppViewModel.applyLUT (AppViewModel.swift:236-244), runs on @MainActor:
previewTask = Task {
    guard let result = lut.apply(to: source) else { ... }   // full-extent graph on main thread
    self.processedImage = result
    self.updatePreview(result)                              // separate renderPreview path
}
// export reuses processedImage (line 283) — a DIFFERENT path that merely agrees.

// AFTER — one definition, two renders (engine is a separate component):
let cg = await RenderEngine.shared.renderPreview(
    source: source, document: document,
    maxSize: maxPreview, space: .current)                  // off-actor, scaled
self.previewNSImage = cg.map { NSImage(cgImage: $0, size: ...) }
// export: try await RenderEngine.shared.export(source:, document:, to:, format:, space: .current)
//   → calls the SAME RenderPipeline.buildImage with .full.
```

NEW `Tests/LUTzyTests/PipelineCoreTests.swift`. Pure/deterministic first, then tiny GPU renders (software CIContext is fine):

PURE (no GPU):

- previewScaleFactor(6000x4000, 1600x1200) ≈ 0.2667 and ==1.0 for 800x600; earlyDownscale returns identity when already fitting.
