GPU (tiny buffers, exact byte asserts):
- Identity render == source: render buildImage(.identity, lut:nil, .full, .sRGB) of a small PNG fixture and the plain CIImage(contentsOf:) to RGBA8; assert byte-equal (proves identity & color-tag parity).
- AdjustmentNode.apply extent preservation: constant-color CIImage 16x16 through each node; assert result.extent == input.extent (no extent growth).
- Intensity boundaries: in-memory size-2 INVERT CubeLUT; mid-gray 8x8; applyWithIntensity(0) == input, (1) == invert, (0.5) strictly between (within tolerance of working-space midpoint). Confirms dissolve + 0/1 short-circuits.

DERIVE NON-REGRESSION (integration; run only if a (RAW,JPG) fixture pair exists, else mark manual): RecipeExtractor.derive returns non-empty cube of expected size + report.sampleCount>0. Decoupling from develop is a COMPILE-TIME guarantee (RecipeExtractor does not import EditDocument) — assert via a source-level note, not runtime.

MANUAL/VISUAL (not unit-testable): preview/export color parity on a real RAW now that both pass WorkingSpace.current explicitly; 50%-intensity blend look sign-off; RAW develop sliders change preview and survive full-res export.

**Reviewer-flagged issues:**
- BLOCKER (compile error, SDK + swiftc verified): RAWDevelopSettings.apply(to:) is the EDR block `if f.isEnableEDRModeAvailable { f.isEDRModeEnabled = on }`. Neither member exists on CIRAWFilter. I typechecked it against the macOS SDK: `error: value of type 'CIRAWFilter' has no member 'isEnableEDRModeAvailable'` and `... no member 'isEDRModeEnabled'`. The SDK header (CIRAWFilter.h:156-161) exposes ONLY `extendedDynamicRangeAmount` (float, 0...2) for EDR — there is NO boolean enable property and NO availability gate for it at all. The spec's enableEDR Bool field, the gate, and the whole `if ... { isEDRModeEnabled = on }` block must be deleted; keep only `if let v = extendedDynamicRangeAmount { f.extendedDynamicRangeAmount = Float(v) }` (ungated).
- BLOCKER (false availability annotation will not compile under its own guard): apply(to:) is annotated `@available(macOS 12.0, *)` but writes properties that are NOT in the macOS-12 CIRAWFilter. The header marks the CLASS as available 12.0, but contrastAmount/detailAmount/localToneMapAmount/extendedDynamicRangeAmount and their support gates are part of the later (macOS 14/15-era) API surface; the class-level NS_CLASS_AVAILABLE(12_0,...) does NOT make these members 12.0-callable. Since Package.swift targets `.macOS(.v14)`, the correct fix is to REMOVE the `@available(macOS 12.0, *)` on apply(to:) entirely. As written it is both wrong and, combined with the macOS-12 fallback branch the spec adds in buildImage, signals confusion about the deployment floor.
- MAJOR (color correctness — the spec's central intensity claim is factually FALSE, runtime-verified): The spec asserts repeatedly (intensityModel, colorSeam, openQuestions, risks) that LUT intensity blends 'in the WORKING space ... not in linear light' via CIDissolveTransition, and treats that as a deliberate, documented, sign-off-needed behavior. I ran CIDissolveTransition at time=0.5 on pixel-backed solid red(255,0,0)/blue(0,0,255): the midpoint is R=188,B=188 (and the full ramp is 255→225→188→137ₒ0). 188 ≈ linearToSRGB(0.5*linear) — i.e. the dissolve mixes in LINEAR light, NOT the working/sRGB space (sRGB-space average would be ~128). So (a) the documented behavior is the opposite of reality, (b) the `colorSpace:` argument threaded into applyWithIntensity is used ONLY by the cube filter and has ZERO effect on the blend, so the 'blend space moves in lockstep with WorkingSpace' lockstep claim is false for the blend, and (c) the entire 'visual sign-off / versioned look change for the working-space blend' open question is moot. Mechanism is otherwise sound: time direction is correct and the 0/1 short-circuits match (t0=red, t1=blue verified).
- MAJOR (missing support gates inconsistent with the spec's own discipline, SDK-verified): The header gives every macOS-14 creative knob a support gate that apply(to:) ignores: contrastAmount→isContrastSupported (h:129), detailAmount→isDetailSupported (h:137), localToneMapAmount→isLocalToneMapSupported (h:153), luminanceNoiseReductionAmount→isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported (h:105), colorNoiseReductionAmount→isColorNoiseReductionSupported (h:113). The spec gates sharpness and moire but not these, despite the SDK documenting that the *Supported flag 'is false if the current image doesn't support this adjustment.' Setting an unsupported knob is undefined/no-op; gate them all or document the asymmetry. (Also: the spec's doc comment says moire range is 0...1 — header says detail and moire are 0...3 for detail and the moire comment text is inconsistent; verify ranges when surfacing in UI.)
- MAJOR (LUTID stable-identity design is self-contradicting): The spec adds `let lutID: LUTID = LUTID()` defaulting to a random UUID, and claims it gives 'a durable handle' so 'derived AND re-scanned file LUTs both get a durable handle' and EditDocuments resolve via the registry. But a fresh random UUID is minted on every parse, so a file-backed LUT re-scanned after relaunch gets a DIFFERENT lutID, and any persisted EditDocument.lut.lutID will fail to resolve — breaking the Codable/registry spine for the exact persistence/undo use case LUTID was introduced for. File-backed LUTID must be derived deterministically from the source path/url; only truly in-memory derived LUTs should get a random UUID.
- MINOR (false 'correction' to the brief #1): The spec claims 'ImageCollection.isImageFile (ImageCollection.swift:77) is still a SEPARATE extension list'. There is no `isImageFile` symbol in ImageCollection.swift at all; the folder-import filter at ImageCollection.swift:44 already calls `ImageProcessor.supportedExtensions.contains(ext)`. There is NO duplicate extension list to consolidate — the spec's (and architecture's) open question about this is moot, and the cited line/symbol do not exist.
- MINOR (false 'correction' to the brief #2): The spec asserts in passing (via the architecture) that 'lutIntensity exists nowhere'. The real AppViewModel has no lutIntensity field today, so this is consistent for THIS file — but note applyLUT applies the LUT full-strength via `lut.apply(to:source)` (AppViewModel:237) with NO intensity, confirming intensity is genuinely new. (Flagging because the architecture doc's stronger wording about prior intensity behavior is the thing being relied upon; the component spec's own statement here is fine.)
- MINOR (CubeLUT init claim is wrong): Spec says initialize lutID 'in BOTH inits' of CubeLUT. CubeLUT has two initializers (init(cube:size:name:...) at line 23 and init(url:category:) at line 50) — so 'both' is numerically right — BUT both are explicit memberwise-style inits that set every stored property, and `tableData` is `private let`. Adding `let lutID` with a default is fine, however the spec elsewhere says 'changes the memberwise-init signature and every call site' — that is FALSE here because CubeLUT does not use the synthesized memberwise init (it has explicit inits); the only edit needed is to set lutID inside each explicit init. The spec conflates the two.
- MINOR (ImageError relocation citation wrong but harmless): Spec says 'REMOVE ImageError (ImageProcessor.swift:219-231)'. ImageError IS at lines 219-231 and IS top-level (not nested), so the move is source-compatible. However the spec ALSO describes it in RenderTypes as 'Moved out of ImageProcessor (ImageProcessor.swift:219) so the pure pipeline and the actor can both throw it' — fine — but adds case `cannotLoad(String)` etc. matching exactly. Verify no code uses `ImageProcessor.ImageError` qualification (none found); relocation is safe.
- MINOR (line-number drift on the load-bearing color sites): The three sRGB sites the seam targets are real but mostly mis-cited. Actual: CubeLUT.makeFilter sRGB at CubeLUT.swift:135 (spec also says :114 — wrong); ImageProcessor.export sRGB at :180 (spec body says :163 in one place, :180 in another — inconsistent); renderPreview createCGImage-with-no-colorSpace at :109 (spec says :92 and :109 — inconsistent). All three sites DO exist and the seam logic is sound, but the inconsistent citations should be reconciled to avoid editing the wrong lines.
- MINOR (overstated 'implicit fourth color site' severity): The spec calls renderPreview's missing explicit colorSpace 'a real latent preview/export color MISMATCH' and 'a correctness requirement.' Runtime check: for an sRGB-tagged source, `createCGImage(from:)` (no colorSpace, today's path) vs `createCGImage(..., colorSpace: sRGB)` produced BYTE-IDENTICAL output (diff=0 over the test buffer). Threading WorkingSpace explicitly is the right fix and matters once wide-gamut (P3/AdobeRGB) sources or P3 output are in play, but the claimed present-day sRGB mismatch does not occur — the framing overstates current breakage.

**Open questions:**
- Photos-import path has no URL (AppViewModel.openImage(data:name:), line 116). ImageSource carries a URL, so this path needs a decision: (a) write imported Data to a temp file and build an ImageSource(url:) — simplest, keeps one render path; or (b) add an ImageSource.Kind/case backed by Data. Recommendation: temp-file (a) so buildImage stays URL-only. This is a ViewModel-component decision but constrains ImageSource here — flag now.
- ImageCollection.isImageFile (ImageCollection.swift:77) is still a SEPARATE extension list from ImageProcessor.supportedExtensions. ImageSource.kind reuses ImageProcessor.rawExtensions; folding ImageCollection onto ImageProcessor.supportedExtensions is a small cleanup so 'what is an image' has one definition. Out of scope for the render fold but should be tracked.
- CubeLUT filter cache uses an @unchecked Sendable box to keep CubeLUT Sendable while caching the CIFilter. Acceptable because all calls run on the RenderEngine actor, but confirm we are comfortable with the one @unchecked in the codebase; the zero-@unchecked alternative is to rebuild the filter each call (no cache). Decide before merge.
- CIContext working space: this component does NOT set kCIContextWorkingColorSpace/kCIContextWorkingFormat (that lives on the engine's CIContext). If the engine later adopts extendedLinear + RGBAh for higher-precision intermediates, the working-space primaries and 8-bit-export gamut behavior change for EVERY render — needs a soft-clip before the 8-bit JPEG/PNG encoders. Deferred; sRGB-default v1 is unaffected.
- Display P3 + derive build-space stamp: WorkingSpace.current is sRGB for v1, so LUT-interpolation==output==derive-sampling all == sRGB and are consistent. When P3 is enabled, a derived .cube (fit in sRGB) must either be re-fit in P3 OR record the space it was built in so CubeLUT.makeFilter applies it in the matching space. Concrete follow-up before P3 ships; do NOT blindly thread WorkingSpace.current into RecipeExtractor's sampler (its neutral RAW baseline is itself an sRGB-default render).
- LUT-intensity blend space is the WORKING space (CIDissolveTransition), not linear light. New behavior with no prior; needs a quick visual sign-off and, if changed later, is a versioned look change for every sub-100% LUT.
- RAW preview fidelity: CIRAWFilter.scaleFactor (early downscale) can demosaic slightly differently from the full-quality .full decode, so a draft preview is not byte-perfect proof of export. v1 accepts the small interaction-time delta; a two-phase 'draft while dragging, settle full-quality on idle' preview is a known enhancement owned by the ViewModel component.
- CIRAWFilter gate names (isSharpnessSupported / isMoireReductionSupported / isEnableEDRModeAvailable etc.) vary by property; verify each against the installed SDK header when implementing RAWDevelopSettings.apply — a wrong name is a compile error, not a silent bug, but get them right to avoid churn. The RAW-develop UI component owns the full availability matrix.
- EditDocument.version starts at 1 with all-defaulted fields; confirm we want explicit migration scaffolding now vs. relying on defaulted decoding. Recommendation: keep the field, add migration only when the first breaking field lands.

**Risks:**
- PREVIEW/EXPORT PARITY hinges on RenderScale being the ONLY difference. If any AdjustmentNode (now or later) uses pixel-sized units, the early-downscaled preview and full-res export diverge silently. Mitigation: the AdjustmentNode.isIdentity/normalized-units INVARIANT is documented and the current cases are all scale-invariant; enforce it in code review for any new case.
- WORKING-SPACE LOCKSTEP: the LUT interpolation space (CubeLUT.makeFilter) and export/raster space must move together. This component makes that structural by threading ONE WorkingSpace value, but the ENGINE component must actually pass WorkingSpace.current to BOTH createCGImage and the export encoders (today renderPreview passes none at ImageProcessor.swift:109 while export forces sRGB at :180). If the engine wiring misses the preview raster, the latent mismatch persists. Flagged as a hard requirement for the engine component.
- DERIVE BUILD/APPLY SPACE: derived .cube is fit in sRGB (RecipeExtractor.swift:81) and applied via makeFilter in WorkingSpace.current. v1 sRGB-everywhere keeps them equal. A future P3 flip that changes makeFilter's space WITHOUT re-fitting derive (or stamping build space on the LUT) silently mis-maps derived LUTs. Captured in open questions; do not flip P3 without addressing it.
- CIDissolveTransition extent growth: the blend can enlarge extent; applyWithIntensity crops back to input.extent. If that crop is dropped, downstream raster/export could pick up transparent borders. Covered by the extent-preservation test.
- CubeLUT filter cache + Sendable: the @unchecked Sendable box assumes actor-serialized access. If a future caller touches a CubeLUT off the actor concurrently, the cache dictionary is a data race. Safe under the current actor design; revisit if CubeLUT is ever used outside RenderEngine. Zero-@unchecked fallback (no cache) is available.
- ImageError relocation: moving ImageError out of ImageProcessor into RenderTypes is source-compatible only if no code qualifies it as ImageProcessor.ImageError (it is referenced unqualified everywhere checked). Verify after the move; it is a same-name type, so a stray qualified reference would be the only breakage.
- Photos-import (data, no URL) does not fit ImageSource(url:) as written. Until the temp-file decision lands, that path cannot use buildImage and would have to keep a legacy render — a parity gap for imported photos. Flagged; pick the temp-file approach to avoid a second render path.
- RAW preview draft fidelity: CIRAWFilter.scaleFactor demosaic differs subtly from full decode, so the scaled preview is not a byte-perfect proof of the full-res export for RAW. Accepted for v1; could surprise pixel-peeping power users until the optional full-quality idle-settle is added.

---

### RAW develop controls

**Verdict:** major-issues (concurrency OK · color FLAG · preview/export OK)

**Summary.** Adds a Sendable/Codable/Equatable RAWDevelopSettings value type whose fields are all Optional (nil == CIRAWFilter decoder default == neutral), an apply(to: CIRAWFilter) that sets ONLY user-touched keys using verified macOS-14 properties, and a RAWCapabilities probe so the UI only enables knobs the current file/decoder actually supports. RAW becomes the SOURCE stage of the render graph via a single pure factory ImageDecode.developRAW(url:settings:scaleFactor:) -> CIImage? that rebuilds CIRAWFilter from the URL, applies settings, and sets scaleFactor for early preview downscale; the same factory called with .neutral and scaleFactor 1 is byte-identical to today's loadRAW. Reset-to-neutral is assigning .neutral (all fields nil) — never zeros, because temperature/tint/baselineExposure/boost are metadata-derived. Derive stays independent by physical decoupling: RecipeExtractor never imports the edit model and keeps developing RAW at defaults; a debug assertion pins .neutral as the contract. CRITICAL CORRECTION to the binding sketch: isSharpnessSupported and isMoireReductionSupported DO NOT EXIST in this SDK and isGamutMappingEnabled is readonly here — only isBaselineExposureAvailable, isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported, isEnableEDRModeAvailable, isLensCorrectionSupported are real gates.

**Files to change:**
- NEW /Users/tim/Documents/Claude/Projects/LUTzy/LUTzy-macOS/LUTzy/Models/RAWDevelopSettings.swift — the value type, apply(to:), .neutral, isNeutral, RAWCapabilities probe.
- NEW /Users/tim/Documents/Claude/Projects/LUTzy/LUTzy-macOS/LUTzy/Models/ImageSource.swift — ImageSource (origin url/data, kind raw/standard, nativeExtent) + the pure ImageDecode.developRAW / loadStandard factory functions (the SOURCE stage). RenderScale is owned by the render-pipeline component; referenced here, not redefined.
- NEW /Users/tim/Documents/Claude/Projects/LUTzy/LUTzy-macOS/LUTzy/Models/SupportedImageTypes.swift — single source of truth for RAW vs standard extension sets (consolidates the two divergent literals).
- MODIFIED /Users/tim/Documents/Claude/Projects/LUTzy/LUTzy-macOS/LUTzy/Models/ImageProcessor.swift — loadRAW(at:settings:) gains a settings param defaulting to .neutral and delegates to ImageDecode.developRAW; rawTypes literal removed in favor of SupportedImageTypes; add rawCapabilities(for:) thin wrapper. (Per render component, ImageProcessor's GPU duties later move into actor RenderEngine; the RAW develop logic deliberately lives as pure free/static functions so BOTH the actor and the main actor can call it.)

NO import of the edit model.

**New types:** RAWDevelopSettings (struct, Codable+Sendable+Equatable; all fields Double?/Bool? Optional; .neutral, isNeutral, apply(to: CIRAWFilter)), RAWCapabilities (struct, Sendable+Equatable; isRAW, baselineExposureAvailable, luminanceNoiseReductionSupported, edrModeAvailable, lensCorrectionSupported, plus suggested temperature/tint nominal defaults read from metadata) + init(probing url:), ImageSource (struct, Sendable+Equatable) with nested ImageSource.Origin (enum: url(URL) / data(Data, identifierHint: String)) and ImageSource.Kind (enum: raw / standard), ImageDecode (caseless enum namespace) with static developRAW(...) and loadStandard(...) — the SOURCE stage factory (pure, no singleton, no CIContext), SupportedImageTypes (caseless enum namespace): rawExtensions, standardExtensions, all, isRAW(_:), isImage(_:)

**Implementation:**

## Component: RAW develop controls

This component owns: (1) the `RAWDevelopSettings` value type + its exact CIRAWFilter mapping, (2) turning a RAW URL into the **source stage** of the render graph, (3) reset-to-neutral semantics, (4) keeping derive's neutral baseline independent. It plugs into the binding architecture as the `rawDevelop` field of `EditDocument` and as STAGE 0+1 of `RenderPipeline.buildImage`. The task calls this the "RAWSettings value type"; per the BINDING decision the type is named **`RAWDevelopSettings`** — same thing.

> **SDK CORRECTION (load-bearing).** I verified against `/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/SDKs/MacOSX.sdk/.../CIRAWFilter.h`. The binding decision's `apply(to:)` sketch calls `f.isSharpnessSupported` and `f.isMoireReductionSupported` — **neither exists in this SDK** and would not compile. The only real per-image capability flags are: `isBaselineExposureAvailable` (readonly, macOS 13+), `isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported` (macOS 13+), `isEnableEDRModeAvailable` (readonly, macOS 14+), `isLensCorrectionSupported` (macOS 13+). Also `isGamutMappingEnabled` is declared **readonly** in this SDK (Apple's published docs mark it settable) — so it is **excluded** from the v1 settable set. `sharpnessAmount` and `moireReductionAmount` have **no** support flag and are set unconditionally. App min-deployment is macOS 14, so every property used below (12/13/14) is available at compile time with **no `#available` guards**; only the four runtime capability flags above gate behavior per file.

---

### 1. `RAWDevelopSettings.swift` (NEW)

Design rule: **every field is Optional; `nil` means "do not touch the CIRAWFilter property" = decoder/metadata default = neutral.** This is the entire reason reset works (see §4). Do **not** model defaults as `0` — `neutralTemperature`/`neutralTint`/`baselineExposure`/`boostAmount`/`boostShadowAmount` are *metadata-derived per shot*; forcing them to 0 would white-balance to ~0 K and destroy the image.

```swift
import CoreImage

/// User-tunable RAW develop knobs. The `rawDevelop` field of EditDocument.
/// nil field == leave CIRAWFilter at its decoder/metadata default. `.neutral` == all nil
/// == byte-identical to today's CIRAWFilter(imageURL:).outputImage.
/// Stored as Double? for clean Codable; cast to Float at apply time (CIRAWFilter is Float).
struct RAWDevelopSettings: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {

    // --- Tone / exposure ---
    var exposure: Double?           // CIRAWFilter.exposure        stops; TRUE default 0.0 (NOT metadata). UI -2.0...2.0 (no hard clamp).
    var boostAmount: Double?        // .boostAmount                0...1; default ~1.0 (full boost). nil = keep default.
    var boostShadowAmount: Double?  // .boostShadowAmount          metadata-derived, can be negative. UI -1.0...1.0. nil = keep.
    var shadowBias: Double?         // .shadowBias                 shadow deepening; range decoder-dependent (see open Qs). UI -1.0...1.0. nil = keep.
    var contrastAmount: Double?     // .contrastAmount             0...1, ~0.35 nominal default. nil = keep.
    var localToneMapAmount: Double? // .localToneMapAmount  (14+)  0...1. nil = keep.

    // --- White balance (creative; temperature/tint pair) ---
    var neutralTemperature: Double? // .neutralTemperature         Kelvin, METADATA default per shot. UI 2000...10000. nil = as-shot.
    var neutralTint: Double?        // .neutralTint                METADATA default. UI -150...150. nil = as-shot.

    // --- Detail / noise ---
    var sharpnessAmount: Double?           // .sharpnessAmount               0...1. No support flag. nil = keep.
    var detailAmount: Double?              // .detailAmount                  0...3. nil = keep.
    var colorNoiseReductionAmount: Double?     // .colorNoiseReductionAmount     0...1. nil = keep.
    var luminanceNoiseReductionAmount: Double? // .luminanceNoiseReductionAmount 0...1. GATED by isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported.
    var moireReductionAmount: Double?      // .moireReductionAmount          0...1. No support flag. nil = keep.

    // --- Dynamic range (macOS 14+, gated) ---
    var enableEDR: Double?                 // stored as 0/1 -> .isEDRModeEnabled. GATED by isEnableEDRModeAvailable. (Double? to keep Codable uniform; see note.)
    var extendedDynamicRangeAmount: Double? // .extendedDynamicRangeAmount    0...2. GATED by isEnableEDRModeAvailable.

    // Forward-compat: bump when fields are added so EditDocument migration is explicit.
    // (version lives on EditDocument, not here.)

    static let neutral = RAWDevelopSettings()   // all nil
    var isNeutral: Bool { self == .neutral }

    /// Sets ONLY user-touched keys. Consults the four REAL capability flags. macOS-14 min => no #available.
    func apply(to f: CIRAWFilter) {
        if let v = exposure                  { f.exposure = Float(v) }
        if let v = boostAmount               { f.boostAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = boostShadowAmount         { f.boostShadowAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = shadowBias                { f.shadowBias = Float(v) }
        if let v = contrastAmount            { f.contrastAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = localToneMapAmount        { f.localToneMapAmount = Float(v) }

        if let v = neutralTemperature        { f.neutralTemperature = Float(v) }
        if let v = neutralTint               { f.neutralTint = Float(v) }

        if let v = sharpnessAmount           { f.sharpnessAmount = Float(v) }            // no support flag
        if let v = detailAmount              { f.detailAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = colorNoiseReductionAmount { f.colorNoiseReductionAmount = Float(v) }
        if let v = luminanceNoiseReductionAmount, f.isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported {  // REAL gate (13+)
            f.luminanceNoiseReductionAmount = Float(v)
        }
        if let v = moireReductionAmount      { f.moireReductionAmount = Float(v) }       // no support flag

        if f.isEnableEDRModeAvailable {                                                  // REAL gate (14+)
            if let on = enableEDR            { f.isEDRModeEnabled = (on != 0) }
            if let v = extendedDynamicRangeAmount { f.extendedDynamicRangeAmount = Float(v) }
        }
        // NOTE: baselineExposure intentionally NOT user-set in v1 (metadata offset; see open Qs).
        //       isGamutMappingEnabled is readonly in this SDK -> excluded.
        //       neutralChromaticityX/Y and neutralLocation deliberately NOT exposed (mutually
        //       exclusive WB representation vs temperature/tint).
    }
}
```

> `enableEDR` is modeled as `Double?` (0/1) only to keep one uniform numeric Codable shape and one slider/toggle path; if you prefer, make it `Bool?` — both are `Codable/Sendable/Equatable`. Pick one and be consistent. The toggle reads `enableEDR != 0`.

#### `RAWCapabilities` — per-image gating probe (same file)

The UI must not show knobs the file/decoder ignores (availability-gated risk in the maps). Probe once at open by building a throwaway `CIRAWFilter` for the URL and reading its readonly flags. This is cheap (no render) and read-only.

```swift
/// What the *currently loaded* RAW supports. Drives enable/disable of inspector controls.
struct RAWCapabilities: Sendable, Equatable {
    var isRAW: Bool                          // false => RAW inspector hidden entirely
    var baselineExposureAvailable: Bool
    var luminanceNoiseReductionSupported: Bool
    var edrModeAvailable: Bool
    var lensCorrectionSupported: Bool
    var asShotTemperature: Double?           // f.neutralTemperature read-back = slider's "home" value
    var asShotTint: Double?                   // f.neutralTint read-back

    static let none = RAWCapabilities(isRAW: false, baselineExposureAvailable: false,
        luminanceNoiseReductionSupported: false, edrModeAvailable: false,
        lensCorrectionSupported: false, asShotTemperature: nil, asShotTint: nil)

    init(isRAW: Bool, baselineExposureAvailable: Bool, luminanceNoiseReductionSupported: Bool,
         edrModeAvailable: Bool, lensCorrectionSupported: Bool,
         asShotTemperature: Double?, asShotTint: Double?) {
        self.isRAW = isRAW; self.baselineExposureAvailable = baselineExposureAvailable
        self.luminanceNoiseReductionSupported = luminanceNoiseReductionSupported
        self.edrModeAvailable = edrModeAvailable; self.lensCorrectionSupported = lensCorrectionSupported
        self.asShotTemperature = asShotTemperature; self.asShotTint = asShotTint
    }

    init(probingRAWAt url: URL) {
        guard let f = CIRAWFilter(imageURL: url) else { self = .none; return }
        self.init(isRAW: true,
                  baselineExposureAvailable: f.isBaselineExposureAvailable,
                  luminanceNoiseReductionSupported: f.isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported,
                  edrModeAvailable: f.isEnableEDRModeAvailable,
                  lensCorrectionSupported: f.isLensCorrectionSupported,
                  asShotTemperature: Double(f.neutralTemperature),
                  asShotTint: Double(f.neutralTint))
    }
}
```

The `asShot*` read-backs give the WB sliders their "as-shot home" tick (so a relative slider knows where neutral sits even though the stored value is `nil`).

---

### 2. RAW as the SOURCE stage — `ImageSource.swift` (NEW)

The look must be re-developable per render (you cannot chain develop onto an already-developed `CIImage`), so app state holds a **URL/Data**, not a live `CIImage`. The develop happens inside the pure factory below, called by BOTH `RenderPipeline.buildImage` (render component) and `RecipeExtractor`. Keeping it as **free/static functions with no singleton and no CIContext** is what makes it usable from the future `actor RenderEngine` and from the main actor without crossing a non-Sendable boundary.

```swift
import CoreImage
import Foundation

struct ImageSource: Sendable, Equatable {
    enum Origin: Sendable, Equatable {
        case url(URL)
        case data(Data, identifierHint: String)   // Photos picker / openImage(data:name:)
    }
    enum Kind: Sendable, Equatable { case raw, standard }

    let origin: Origin
    let kind: Kind
    let nativeExtent: CGSize   // probed at open; used to compute preview scaleFactor

    var url: URL? { if case let .url(u) = origin { return u } else { return nil } }
}

/// Caseless-enum namespace. PURE: no singleton, no CIContext, no @MainActor.
enum ImageDecode {

    /// STAGE 0+1 of the graph for RAW. Rebuilds CIRAWFilter from the source, applies develop,
    /// sets scaleFactor BEFORE outputImage so demosaic happens at preview size (early downscale).
    /// scaleFactor 1.0 => full-res export. settings .neutral + scaleFactor 1 == today's loadRAW.
    static func developRAW(_ origin: ImageSource.Origin,
                           settings: RAWDevelopSettings,
                           scaleFactor: Float) -> CIImage? {
        let filter: CIRAWFilter?
        switch origin {
        case let .url(u):                       filter = CIRAWFilter(imageURL: u)
        case let .data(d, hint):                filter = CIRAWFilter(imageData: d, identifierHint: hint)
        }
        guard let f = filter else { return nil }
        if scaleFactor < 1.0 { f.scaleFactor = scaleFactor }   // only ever downscale
        settings.apply(to: f)                                   // user knobs (or none for .neutral)
        return f.outputImage
    }

    /// STAGE 0 for non-RAW. Orientation applied (parity with current loadImage).
    static func loadStandard(_ origin: ImageSource.Origin) -> CIImage? {
        let opt: [CIImageOption: Any] = [.applyOrientationProperty: true]
        switch origin {
        case let .url(u):        return CIImage(contentsOf: u, options: opt)
        case let .data(d, _):    return CIImage(data: d, options: opt)
        }
    }

    /// Native extent for scaleFactor math, without a full develop.
    static func probeExtent(_ origin: ImageSource.Origin, kind: ImageSource.Kind) -> CGSize {
        switch kind {
        case .raw:
            // outputImage at scaleFactor 1 gives true extent; cheap relative to a render.
            return developRAW(origin, settings: .neutral, scaleFactor: 1.0)?.extent.size ?? .zero
        case .standard:
            return loadStandard(origin)?.extent.size ?? .zero
        }
    }
}
```

**How the render pipeline consumes it** (render component owns `RenderScale`; shown here for the seam):

```swift
// Inside RenderPipeline.buildImage(source:document:lut:scale:space:)  -- STAGE 0+1:
var image: CIImage
switch source.kind {
case .raw:
    let sf: Float = {
        if case let .preview(maxSize) = scale {
            return previewScaleFactor(native: source.nativeExtent, max: maxSize) // <= 1.0
        }
        return 1.0   // .full
    }()
    guard let out = ImageDecode.developRAW(source.origin, settings: document.rawDevelop, scaleFactor: sf)
    else { return nil }
    image = out
case .standard:
    guard let ci = ImageDecode.loadStandard(source.origin) else { return nil }
    image = (scaleIsPreview(scale)) ? earlyDownscale(ci, max: previewMax(scale)) : ci
}
// ...STAGE 2 adjustments, STAGE 3 LUT(intensity) follow (other components)

func previewScaleFactor(native: CGSize, max: CGSize) -> Float {
    guard native.width > 0, native.height > 0 else { return 1.0 }
    return Float(min(max.width / native.width, max.height / native.height, 1.0))
}
```

This replaces today's post-hoc affine downscale (`ImageProcessor.renderPreview`, `transformed(by:)` at ImageProcessor.swift:52) with RAW-native draft demosaic, so adjustment + LUT nodes run on ~1600x1200 px instead of full extent.

---

### 3. `SupportedImageTypes.swift` (NEW) — one extension source of truth

Today two divergent literals exist: `ImageProcessor.rawTypes` (ImageProcessor.swift:13-15) and `ImageCollection.imageExtensions` (ImageCollection.swift:9-12). `ImageSource.Kind` must be derived from ONE list or RAW vs standard routing drifts across open/collection/derive.

```swift
enum SupportedImageTypes {
    static let rawExtensions: Set<String> =
        ["dng","cr2","cr3","nef","arw","raf","rw2","orf","pef","srw"]
    static let standardExtensions: Set<String> =
        ["jpg","jpeg","png","tiff","tif","heic","heif"]
    static var all: Set<String> { rawExtensions.union(standardExtensions) }
    static func isRAW(_ url: URL) -> Bool { rawExtensions.contains(url.pathExtension.lowercased()) }
    static func isImage(_ url: URL) -> Bool { all.contains(url.pathExtension.lowercased()) }
    static func kind(for url: URL) -> ImageSource.Kind { isRAW(url) ? .raw : .standard }
}
```

---

### 4. Reset-to-neutral

Reset = assign `.neutral` (or set an individual field to `nil`). Because `nil` means "don't touch the property", the next render builds a `CIRAWFilter` with no overrides → decoder/metadata defaults. **No property is ever written to a literal "neutral value"** — that's the whole point and the bug-class guard.

```swift
// AppViewModel (rawDevelop lives on document, per render component):
func resetRAWDevelop() {
    document.rawDevelop = .neutral          // Equatable change -> debounced re-render
}

// Per-field reset (UI "reset this slider" affordance), generic over KeyPath:
func resetRAWField(_ kp: WritableKeyPath<RAWDevelopSettings, Double?>) {
    document.rawDevelop[keyPath: kp] = nil
}
```

UI sliders bind to the Optional via a non-optional proxy that writes `nil` when returned to home, e.g.:

```swift
// In the inspector view; `home` is e.g. capabilities.asShotTemperature for WB, or 0 for exposure.
private func proxy(_ kp: WritableKeyPath<RAWDevelopSettings, Double?>, home: Double) -> Binding<Double> {
    Binding(get: { vm.document.rawDevelop[keyPath: kp] ?? home },
            set: { newVal in
                vm.document.rawDevelop[keyPath: kp] = (abs(newVal - home) < .ulpOfOne) ? nil : newVal
            })
}
```

A global "Reset RAW" button calls `resetRAWDevelop()`. `isNeutral` drives that button's disabled state.

---

### 5. Derive keeps a neutral baseline — independent of these settings

Guaranteed by **physical decoupling**: `RecipeExtractor` (a value struct) must NOT import `EditDocument`/`AppViewModel` and must NOT accept a `RAWDevelopSettings` from the user. It keeps developing RAW at defaults. Two acceptable implementations; both pin the contract:

**Option A (preferred — dedupe + assertion).** Route derive's baseline through the same factory with `.neutral`:

```swift
// RecipeExtractor — neutral baseline. BEFORE: CIRAWFilter(imageURL:).outputImage (RecipeExtractor.swift:86-89)
private func neutralRAWBaseline(rawURL: URL) -> CIImage? {
    // CONTRACT: the derive baseline is the .neutral develop, INDEPENDENT of any user settings.
    assert(RAWDevelopSettings.neutral.isNeutral)   // tripwire if someone makes .neutral non-empty
    return ImageDecode.developRAW(.url(rawURL), settings: .neutral, scaleFactor: 1.0)
}
```

**Option B (keep its own call site, just document it).** Leave the inline `CIRAWFilter(imageURL: rawURL).outputImage` exactly as-is and add the comment+assert above it. Either way RecipeExtractor: keeps its **own** `CIContext` (RecipeExtractor.swift:419) and its **own** `sRGB` sampling space (RecipeExtractor.swift:81) — do NOT thread `WorkingSpace.current` into the derive sampler (a derived `.cube` is fit-from-sRGB-baseline and must be applied in the same space; that's the color-seam component's concern). The only structural rule for THIS component: derive's RAW develop is `.neutral`, full and unconditional.

The strongest guarantee is the *absence* of a code path: there is no parameter by which user develop could reach `neutralRAWBaseline`. A grep test (see notes) enforces "RecipeExtractor never references `document.rawDevelop`".

---

### 6. Modified call sites (before/after)

**`ImageProcessor.loadRAW` (ImageProcessor.swift:40-43 / map :53)** — keep the symbol, make it settings-aware with a neutral default so all existing callers are byte-identical:

```swift
// AFTER
func loadRAW(at url: URL, settings: RAWDevelopSettings = .neutral) -> CIImage? {
    ImageDecode.developRAW(.url(url), settings: settings, scaleFactor: 1.0)
}
func rawCapabilities(for url: URL) -> RAWCapabilities { RAWCapabilities(probingRAWAt: url) }
// rawTypes literal (lines 13-15) DELETED; loadImage's branch uses SupportedImageTypes.isRAW(url).
```

**`AppViewModel.openImage` (AppViewModel.swift:72 / map :27-47)** — capture an `ImageSource` + capabilities instead of only a baked `sourceImage`:

```swift
// AFTER (sketch)
let kind = SupportedImageTypes.kind(for: url)
let origin = ImageSource.Origin.url(url)
let extent = ImageDecode.probeExtent(origin, kind: kind)
self.imageSource = ImageSource(origin: origin, kind: kind, nativeExtent: extent)
self.rawCapabilities = (kind == .raw) ? RAWCapabilities(probingRAWAt: url) : .none
self.document.rawDevelop = .neutral          // fresh image starts neutral
// trigger the render component's debounced renderPreview(source:document:...)
```

(`sourceImage: CIImage?` is retired by the render component; this component supplies `imageSource`. The data path `openImage(data:name:)` builds `.data(d, identifierHint:)` and sets `kind` by sniffing — for RAW data use `CIRAWFilter(imageData:identifierHint:)`, already handled in `developRAW`.)

---

### 7. UI binding contract (cross-ref — full inspector is adjacent scope)

Controls bind to `vm.document.rawDevelop` (so changes are Equatable-diffed and debounced by the render component) and are enabled per `vm.rawCapabilities`. Minimal sketch:

```swift
struct RAWDevelopInspector: View {
    @ObservedObject var vm: AppViewModel
    var body: some View {
        if vm.rawCapabilities.isRAW {
            Section("Tone") {
                LabeledSlider("Exposure", proxy(\.exposure, home: 0), -2...2)
                LabeledSlider("Contrast", proxy(\.contrastAmount, home: 0.35), 0...1)
                LabeledSlider("Boost",    proxy(\.boostAmount, home: 1.0), 0...1)
            }
            Section("White Balance") {
                LabeledSlider("Temp", proxy(\.neutralTemperature, home: vm.rawCapabilities.asShotTemperature ?? 5500), 2000...10000)
                LabeledSlider("Tint", proxy(\.neutralTint, home: vm.rawCapabilities.asShotTint ?? 0), -150...150)
            }
            Section("Detail / Noise") {
                LabeledSlider("Sharpness", proxy(\.sharpnessAmount, home: 0), 0...1)
                LabeledSlider("Luma NR", proxy(\.luminanceNoiseReductionAmount, home: 0), 0...1)
                    .disabled(!vm.rawCapabilities.luminanceNoiseReductionSupported)
            }
            if vm.rawCapabilities.edrModeAvailable {
                Section("Dynamic Range") {
                    LabeledSlider("EDR Amount", proxy(\.extendedDynamicRangeAmount, home: 0), 0...2)
                }
            }
            Button("Reset RAW") { vm.resetRAWDevelop() }.disabled(vm.document.rawDevelop.isNeutral)
        }
    }
}
```

> KeyMonitor caveat (maps): the global `NSEvent` monitor swallows `v/[/]/space`; numeric text entry in this inspector will be hijacked. Either gate the monitor when a control has focus or make the inspector a popover. Tracked in the State/View component, flagged here.

**Testing:** No XCTest target exists today; add a unit-test target (or a lightweight debug harness) — these tests need only CoreImage and a sample .DNG/.RAW + matching .JPG fixture. Priority tests:

1. NEUTRAL EQUIVALENCE (the reset invariant). Render ImageDecode.developRAW(.url(raw), settings: .neutral, scaleFactor: 1) and a hand-built CIRAWFilter(imageURL: raw).outputImage to RGBA8 via one CIContext+sRGB; assert the two byte buffers are identical (or equal SHA256). This proves "reset == decoder default" and that .neutral touches nothing.

2. APPLY TOUCHES ONLY SET KEYS. Build filterA = fresh CIRAWFilter(imageURL:). Build filterB = fresh, then RAWDevelopSettings(exposure: 1.0).apply(to: filterB). Assert filterB.exposure == 1.0 AND filterB.neutralTemperature == filterA.neutralTemperature (metadata default untouched) AND filterB.contrastAmount == filterA.contrastAmount. Repeat with one field at a time.

3. CODABLE ROUND-TRIP + FORWARD COMPAT. Encode a populated RAWDevelopSettings to JSON, decode, assert ==. Then decode a JSON literal that OMITS several keys (simulating an older document) and assert the missing fields are nil and the value isNeutral for the omitted ones. Confirms additive schema growth is safe.

4. EQUATABLE / isNeutral. RAWDevelopSettings() == .neutral; .neutral.isNeutral == true; after setting any one field non-nil, isNeutral == false and != .neutral.

5. DERIVE INDEPENDENCE (headline regression for the hard constraint). Derive a LUT via RecipeExtractor.derive(raw, jpg) -> cubeA. Then set vm.document.rawDevelop = RAWDevelopSettings(exposure: 3.0, neutralTemperature: 9000) (an extreme), derive AGAIN -> cubeB. Assert cubeA.tableData == cubeB.tableData. If they differ, user develop leaked into the baseline. ALSO add a source-level guard test: assert RecipeExtractor.swift contains no reference to `rawDevelop`/`EditDocument`/`AppViewModel` (string grep in a test) so future edits can't reintroduce coupling.

6. CAPABILITIES GATING. For a RAW lacking baseline-exposure, assert RAWCapabilities(probingRAWAt:).baselineExposureAvailable == false. Construct settings with luminanceNoiseReductionAmount set and apply to a filter whose isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported is false (use such a fixture) — assert the property was NOT changed from the fresh-filter value (the gate held).

7. PREVIEW/EXPORT RAW PARITY (tolerance, not exact, due to draft demosaic). With the SAME non-neutral RAWDevelopSettings: render preview (scaleFactor from previewScaleFactor) and full export; bilinearly sample ~9 normalized coordinates in both and assert per-channel delta < a small tolerance (e.g. 3/255). Document that scaleFactor/draft can introduce a sub-threshold delta; if a test demands exactness, it must compare two FULL renders (scaleFactor 1) instead.

8. EXTENSION CONSOLIDATION. Assert SupportedImageTypes.rawExtensions and .standardExtensions are disjoint; SupportedImageTypes.kind(for:) returns .raw for a .dng URL and .standard for a .jpg URL; ImageCollection.isImageFile and the old behavior still accept the same set (no regressions in filmstrip).

9. COMPILE GUARD. A trivial test that calls RAWDevelopSettings.neutral.apply(to: CIRAWFilter(imageURL: fixture)!) — its mere compilation proves no non-existent API (isSharpnessSupported/isMoireReductionSupported/settable isGamutMappingEnabled) crept back in from the binding sketch, under the macOS 14 deployment target with no #available.

Manual smoke: open a RAW, drag Exposure/Temp/Tint — preview updates live; press Reset RAW — returns to as-shot; open a non-RAW JPG — RAW inspector hidden (capabilities.isRAW == false); derive a LUT while develop is heavily adjusted — derived look matches deriving at neutral.

**Reviewer-flagged issues:**
- COMPILE ERROR (load-bearing): apply(to:) and RAWCapabilities reference EDR APIs that DO NOT EXIST in this SDK. I read the real header end-to-end (CoreImage.framework/Headers/CIRAWFilter.h, 199 lines; byte-identical between Xcode MacOSX26.5 SDK and CommandLineTools). The ONLY EDR symbol is `extendedDynamicRangeAmount` (line 161, @property(readwrite) float, range 0...2, default 0, NO support flag). There is NO isEDRModeEnabled, NO isEnableEDRModeEnabled, NO isEnableEDRModeAvailable, NO EDR-enable BOOL of any name (grep 'EDR' = exactly one line). So `f.isEDRModeEnabled = (on != 0)`, the `if f.isEnableEDRModeAvailable {...}` gate, and `RAWCapabilities.edrModeAvailable = f.isEnableEDRModeAvailable` ALL fail to compile; the `enableEDR` field has no backing property. Fix: delete enableEDR; set extendedDynamicRangeAmount unconditionally (0...2).
- THE BOLDED 'SDK CORRECTION' IS BACKWARDS AND IS ITSELF THE BUG. The spec insists (bold, 'load-bearing') that isSharpnessSupported and isMoireReductionSupported 'DO NOT EXIST in this SDK and would not compile', and that isGamutMappingEnabled is readonly. The header says the OPPOSITE: isSharpnessSupported EXISTS (121), isMoireReductionSupported EXISTS (145), and gamut mapping is `@property(readwrite, getter=isGamutMappingEnabled) BOOL gamutMappingEnabled` (92) = SETTABLE. The binding sketch's original gates were CORRECT; the spec 'corrects' working code into a downgrade (removes real support gates, excludes a real settable property) on a misread header. The spec's apply() compiles but is worse than what it replaced, and its justifying narrative is false.
- 'Only 4 real capability gates' is FALSE in both directions. Spec claims the only flags are isBaselineExposureAvailable, isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported, isEnableEDRModeAvailable, isLensCorrectionSupported. Real readonly support flags in the header: isLensCorrectionSupported(97), isLuminanceNoiseReductionSupported(105), isColorNoiseReductionSupported(113), isSharpnessSupported(121), isContrastSupported(129), isDetailSupported(137), isMoireReductionSupported(145), isLocalToneMapSupported(153), and isHighlightRecoverySupported(87, NS_AVAILABLE(16_0)). isEnableEDRModeAvailable does NOT exist; isBaselineExposureAvailable does NOT exist (baselineExposure is a plain readwrite float, 69, no flag). So the spec INVENTS two flags and OMITS eight-to-nine real ones. RAWCapabilities therefore cannot disable the genuinely-unsupported knobs (sharpness/contrast/detail/moire/colorNR/localToneMap), defeating the spec's own UX rule.
- MISSED RAW KNOB EXPLICITLY REQUIRED BY THE GOAL. Phase-2 goal (B) names 'highlights/shadows recovery'. The header has highlightRecoveryEnabled (88, readwrite BOOL) gated by isHighlightRecoverySupported (87), both NS_AVAILABLE(16_0,19_0); and boostShadowAmount (83, range 0...2, default 1) is the shadow lift/recovery knob. The spec models neither highlight recovery (omitted entirely) nor boostShadow correctly (it uses -1...1 instead of 0...2 centered at 1). So a headline goal-(B) control is absent and a present one is mis-ranged.
- FABRICATED 'two divergent extension lists' premise; the proposed replacement DROPS supported formats. ImageProcessor already defines ONE source of truth -- rawExtensions + standardExtensions + unioned supportedExtensions (ImageProcessor.swift:19-32) -- and ImageCollection.loadFromFolder ALREADY consults ImageProcessor.supportedExtensions (ImageCollection.swift:44). There is no second literal; the consolidation is already done. Worse, the spec's SupportedImageTypes OMITS `x3f` and `raw` from raw (real raw set = 12 incl. both) and OMITS `bmp` from standard (real standard = jpg,jpeg,png,tiff,tif,bmp,heic). Adopting it silently demotes .x3f/.raw and drops .bmp; the spec's own test 8 ('same set accepted, no filmstrip regressions') would FAIL.
- SPEC IGNORES THE EXISTING SHARED NEUTRAL HELPER -- the exact anti-drift anchor the constraint wants. Both render and derive already funnel RAW-at-defaults through the static ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral(at:) (defined ImageProcessor.swift:74-81; called by loadRAW :85 and RecipeExtractor.derive :89), whose doc comment already states the derive-independence contract. The spec proposes a brand-new ImageDecode.developRAW(.url,.neutral,1.0) + a fresh RAWDevelopSettings.neutral assertion as if no anchor exists. Adding ImageDecode WITHOUT retiring/delegating developRAWNeutral creates TWO competing neutral definitions -- precisely the drift the hard constraint forbids. Must reconcile.
- AVAILABILITY ASSUMPTION RESTS ON THE WRONG FILE BUT THE FLOOR HAPPENS TO HOLD -- with caveats. The spec says 'macOS-14 min => no #available guards'. The project is SwiftPM, not an xcodeproj: Package.swift sets swift-tools-version:5.9 and platforms:[.macOS(.v14)] (confirmed). So the 14.0 floor is real. BUT: CIRAWFilter is NS_CLASS_AVAILABLE(12_0,...) and the in-tree developRAWNeutral STILL wraps it in `if #available(macOS 12.0,*)` with a legacy CIFilter(imageURL:) fallback (ImageProcessor.swift:75-79) that is now dead code under a 14 floor; and highlightRecovery is NS_AVAILABLE(16_0), which DOES need an #available guard. So 'no #available guards' is wrong for any macOS-16 property (highlight recovery), and the existing 12.0 guard should be cleaned up -- the spec addresses neither.
- DERIVE COLOR-SEAM FACTS WRONG (carried from the binding). Spec: RecipeExtractor 'keeps its own CIContext (RecipeExtractor.swift:419)' with 'its own sRGB sampling space (line 81)'. Real: context built by makeContext() at RecipeExtractor.swift:421 as CIContext(mtlDevice:options:[.useSoftwareRenderer:false]) -- NO working-space pin; sRGB is only the raster colorSpace constant at line 81, consumed by render() (451-472, called 129-137). Line :419 is wrong and 'sRGB sampling space' is imprecise. The decoupling conclusion holds, but the P3 follow-up depends on stating working=CI-default vs output=sRGB.
- WHITE-BALANCE / BOOST RANGES MIS-STATED. Header line 165: neutralTemperature 2000..50000K, neutralTint -150..150. Spec caps Temp at 10000 (UI choice ok) but understates the SDK range. WB alternatives neutralChromaticity (168) and neutralLocation (169) are CGPoint, not separate x/y floats. boostAmount 0...1 default 1 (74-78); boostShadowAmount 0...2 default 1 (80-83); extendedDynamicRangeAmount 0...2 default 0 (156-161). The spec's boostShadow -1...1 and EDR field comments do not match the header.
- MINOR/CORRECTED: CubeLUT is `Identifiable, Hashable` only -- NOT Codable, NOT Sendable (CubeLUT.swift:5) -- with a `private let tableData: Data` blob (line 11). The binding's 'embedding makes EditDocument non-Codable' is true in spirit (CubeLUT isn't Codable) though it cites the wrong reason ([Float] vs Data); the field is genuinely named `tableData` and is Data, so the spec's `tableData` references are CORRECT. The real LUT-by-ID justification is identity + snapshot size. Also note CubeLUT==/hash are by `id` only, so any registry keyed by content must not rely on Hashable semantics. (Concurrency posture is otherwise sound: Package.swift sets no swiftLanguageMode/StrictConcurrency, so the actor+value-state readiness argument is valid.)

**Open questions:**
- Undocumented numeric ranges: Apple does not publish hard clamps/defaults for shadowBias and boostShadowAmount (and exposure has no documented hard clamp). Proposed UI ranges above (-1...1, -1...1, -2...2) are conservative; confirm empirically per representative cameras before shipping, or these sliders feel mis-scaled.
- baselineExposure exposure-as-UI: it is a metadata-derived absolute baseline, not a relative offset, and is gated by isBaselineExposureAvailable. Recommend NOT exposing it as a user slider in v1 (left out of apply()); decide whether to surface it read-only in the report/inspector or omit entirely.
- White-balance representation: temperature/tint vs neutralChromaticityX/Y vs neutralLocation (eyedropper pick) are alternative, partly mutually-exclusive WB controls. v1 exposes temperature+tint only. Decide if an as-shot eyedropper (neutralLocation) is wanted later, and define precedence if more than one is set.
- isGamutMappingEnabled: this local SDK declares it READONLY while Apple's published docs mark it settable. Excluded from v1 to guarantee compilation. Verify against the shipping Xcode SDK on the build machine; if settable there, add it behind a capability note.
- enableEDR typing: modeled as Double? (0/1) for a uniform numeric Codable shape; confirm whether you'd rather it be Bool? (cleaner semantics, still Codable). Pick one before the inspector is written.
- CIRAWFilter rebuild cost vs caching: developRAW constructs a fresh CIRAWFilter every render tick (correct, because unset Optionals must revert to decoder defaults). Caching a CIRAWFilter per URL and mutating it is a TRAP: a field returning from non-nil to nil (reset) would NOT revert on a reused filter unless you re-set the default, which you don't know. Recommend fresh-per-render + debounce; revisit only if profiling shows the per-tick CIRAWFilter init dominates.
- Photos/data RAW path: openImage(data:name:) — confirm we can reliably detect RAW-in-Data and supply a correct identifierHint for CIRAWFilter(imageData:identifierHint:); otherwise route data RAW to a temp file. v1 sketch supports .data origin but the RAW-vs-standard sniff for in-memory data needs a decision (UTType from the picker).
- Derive build-space stamp (cross-component, but interacts here): if WorkingSpace ever flips to Display P3, derive must re-fit in P3 or stamp the build space on the CubeLUT. This component fixes derive's RAW develop to .neutral; it does NOT solve the build/apply color-space lockstep — confirm that stays with the color-seam component.

**Risks:**
- Binding sketch is not compilable as written: it calls isSharpnessSupported and isMoireReductionSupported (absent in this SDK) and sets isGamutMappingEnabled (readonly here). Copying the binding's apply() verbatim breaks the build. This spec's apply() is the corrected, verified version — use it, not the binding sketch.
- Metadata-default vs zero-default confusion: neutralTemperature/neutralTint/boostAmount/boostShadowAmount default from shot metadata, while exposure truly defaults to 0. Modeling any metadata-derived field's reset as a literal (0 or 5500) instead of nil silently re-white-balances/re-tones every reset. The Optional=nil-means-untouched rule must be preserved everywhere, including the slider proxy.
- Derive-baseline drift: if any future refactor adds a parameter that lets user RAWDevelopSettings reach RecipeExtractor.neutralRAWBaseline, every derived LUT silently changes meaning (violates the hard constraint). Mitigated by physical decoupling + the .neutral assertion + the grep test, but it is the highest-severity regression surface.
- RAW preview fidelity: scaleFactor/draft demosaic is not byte-identical to a full decode, so a scaled preview is not a pixel-perfect proof of export (parity is tolerance-based for RAW). Acceptable for v1 interaction; flag to users/tests. A two-phase 'settle at full quality on idle' is the known enhancement.
- Per-tick CIRAWFilter construction: building CIRAWFilter from the URL on every slider tick is heavier than non-RAW filter ops. Without the render component's debounce + early scaleFactor, dragging develop sliders will stutter on large RAWs. Caching the filter is unsafe (see open Qs reset trap), so debounce is mandatory, not optional.
- Capabilities probe builds an extra CIRAWFilter at open: cheap but non-zero; ensure it runs off the main actor if openImage is on the hot path, and cache the result in @Published rawCapabilities rather than re-probing per render.
- Extension-set consolidation touches routing for open/collection/derive simultaneously; a mistake here mis-routes RAW as standard (no develop, wrong baseline) or vice versa. Cover with the disjoint/kind tests before relying on it.
- Swift 6 concurrency: ImageDecode is intentionally free/static (no singleton, no CIContext) so it crosses the actor boundary cleanly; RAWDevelopSettings/ImageSource/RAWCapabilities are all Sendable value types. Do NOT add a CIContext or @MainActor to ImageDecode or you reintroduce the non-Sendable-capture hazard the render component is trying to remove.
- KeyMonitor focus collision: the global NSEvent monitor swallows letter/space keys; an always-visible RAW inspector with numeric text fields will lose keystrokes (typing in a field triggers shortcuts). Must be resolved (focus-gating or popover) for the controls to be usable, even though the monitor lives in the State/View component.

---

### LUT node + intensity + de-hardcoded color

**Verdict:** minor-issues (concurrency OK · color FLAG · preview/export FLAG)

**Summary.** Refactor CubeLUT so the 3D-cube color op (a) interpolates in an INJECTED CGColorSpace instead of hardcoded sRGB, and (b) supports a 0...1 intensity that blends the pre-LUT input toward the graded output in that same working space — realized as the final stage of the render pipeline, NOT baked into the cube filter. Parse/write (the two inits + cubeFileContents/write/LUTError) stay byte-for-byte untouched.

Concretely: add an `enum WorkingSpace` seam type (single source of truth for the cube space), change `makeFilter()` -> `makeFilter(colorSpace:)` with a small per-color-space CIFilter cache, add `applyWithIntensity(to:intensity:colorSpace:)` (short-circuits at 0 and 1, otherwise CIDissolveTransition), and KEEP a thin full-strength `apply(to:colorSpace:)` (old `apply(to:)` semantics minus the hardcoded space). The zero-arg `makeFilter()`/`apply(to:)` are deleted and all callers updated.

Edits land in CubeLUT.swift (+ one new ~25-line WorkingSpace.swift) plus call-site updates in AppViewModel.applyLUT(), ImageProcessor.export(), and RecipeExtractor (funnel its existing sRGB literal through the seam WITHOUT changing the neutral-baseline contract — pinned to .sRGB, not .current).

This is the LUT-and-color slice of the larger Phase-2 pipeline; it deliberately does NOT build EditDocument / RenderEngine (sibling components) — it exposes the exact signatures those will call.

SDK-VERIFIED against CIFilterBuiltins.h (MacOSX26.5.sdk): `CIColorCubeWithColorSpace` setValue keys inputCubeDimension/inputCubeData/inputColorSpace are correct (the typed-protocol spellings are cubeDimension/cubeData/colorSpace, same filter); `CIDissolveTransition` conforms to CITransitionFilter so kCIInputImageKey/kCIInputTargetImageKey/kCIInputTimeKey are the right keys; and `CIColorCubeWithColorSpace` also exposes an `extrapolate` Bool (macOS 13+) relevant to the future extended-range/P3 path.

**Files to change:**
- MODIFIED: /Users/tim/Documents/Claude/Projects/LUTzy/LUTzy-macOS/LUTzy/Models/CubeLUT.swift — change makeFilter() -> makeFilter(colorSpace:); add a per-colorSpace CIFilter cache (reference box); add applyWithIntensity(to:intensity:colorSpace:); replace apply(to:) with apply(to:colorSpace:). Add `import CoreImage.CIFilterBuiltins` if using the typed dissolve builder (optional). Keep BOTH parse inits (lines 23-126), cubeFileContents/write (150-181), == / hash (15-16), and enum LUTError (186-194) EXACTLY as-is — only the `// MARK: - Core Image Filter` block (128-144) and two added stored members change.
- NEW: /Users/tim/Documents/Claude/Projects/LUTzy/LUTzy-macOS/LUTzy/Models/WorkingSpace.swift — `enum WorkingSpace { case sRGB, displayP3 }` with `var cgColorSpace: CGColorSpace` and `static let current = .sRGB`. ~25 lines. MUST be added to the LUTzy app target in the .xcodeproj (same group as CubeLUT.swift) or it won't compile into the app.
- MODIFIED: /Users/tim/Documents/Claude/Projects/LUTzy/LUTzy-macOS/LUTzy/ViewModels/AppViewModel.swift — applyLUT() (224-245) calls lut.applyWithIntensity(to: source, intensity: lutIntensity, colorSpace: WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace) instead of lut.apply(to: source); add `@Published var lutIntensity: Double = 1.0` and re-run applyLUT() when it changes. applyWithIntensity is NON-optional, so the `guard let result = ... else { statusMessage = "LUT application failed" }` branch (237-240) is removed.
- MODIFIED: /Users/tim/Documents/Claude/Projects/LUTzy/LUTzy-macOS/LUTzy/Models/ImageProcessor.swift — export() (174) gains a `colorSpace: CGColorSpace = WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace` parameter and uses it for all three encoders; delete the hardcoded `let colorSpace = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!` at line 180. (Output-encoding half of the seam.)
- MODIFIED: /Users/tim/Documents/Claude/Projects/LUTzy/LUTzy-macOS/LUTzy/Models/RecipeExtractor.swift — ONE line: change `let sRGB = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!` (line 81) to `let sRGB = WorkingSpace.sRGB.cgColorSpace`. DO NOT use WorkingSpace.current here and DO NOT touch developRAWNeutral or the sampling math — derive must stay pinned to sRGB + neutral RAW regardless of any future P3 flip (documented invariant).

**New types:** WorkingSpace (enum, new file WorkingSpace.swift): `enum WorkingSpace: String, Codable, Sendable, Equatable { case sRGB, displayP3 }`. Members: `var cgColorSpace: CGColorSpace` (CGColorSpace(name: .sRGB)! / CGColorSpace(name: .displayP3)!) and `static let current: WorkingSpace = .sRGB`. The SINGLE seam the brief asks for; default sRGB = zero behavior change, one-line flip to P3. Both system names are guaranteed-resolvable so the force-unwrap is safe and now localized to ONE place (replaces the two force-unwraps the brief flags at ImageProcessor.export and RecipeExtractor). Sendable/Codable so it can ride into the sibling RenderEngine actor / EditDocument later without friction., NO protocol for the LUT node. Per the binding architecture, adjustment nodes are a CLOSED enum owned by the sibling 'pipeline' component and the LUT is intentionally NOT an AdjustmentNode case (intensity is intrinsically coupled to the pre/post-LUT pair, so it lives with the LUT). This component therefore adds methods to the existing CubeLUT struct, not a new node type., FilterCache (private final class inside CubeLUT): `private final class FilterCache { var byColorSpaceName: [String: CIFilter] = [:] }`, held as `private let filterCache = FilterCache()`. Needed because CubeLUT is a value type with `let` storage and its methods are non-mutating; the reference box lets makeFilter(colorSpace:) memoize the (size^3 x 4-float) cube filter without making the struct mutable. == / hash are hand-written on `id` so equality is unaffected. ALTERNATIVE (call out, pick one): omit this and let the sibling RenderEngine actor own a [LUTID: [String: CIFilter]] cache; then makeFilter(colorSpace:) is a pure rebuild-each-call builder.

**Implementation:**

## Ground truth (real file, not the brief's minified line numbers)

`/Users/tim/.../LUTzy/Models/CubeLUT.swift` is 194 lines (readable). Real anchors:
- `func makeFilter() -> CIFilter?` lines **131-137**, hardcodes sRGB at **135**, returns **optional** (`CIFilter(name:)` can fail).
- `func apply(to image: CIImage) -> CIImage?` lines **140-144**.
- `private let tableData: Data` line **11**. The two inits (**23-126**), `cubeFileContents`/`write` (**150-181**), `==`/`hash` (**15-16**), `enum LUTError` (**186-194**) are OUT OF SCOPE.
- `struct CubeLUT: Identifiable, Hashable` with existing `id: String`, `size: Int`.

SDK-verified (CIFilterBuiltins.h, MacOSX26.5):
- `CIColorCubeWithColorSpace` properties: `inputImage`, `cubeDimension` (float), `cubeData` (NSData), `extrapolate` (bool, macOS 13+), `colorSpace` (CGColorSpaceRef). The code's `setValue(_:forKey:)` names `inputCubeDimension`/`inputCubeData`/`inputColorSpace` are the correct KVC keys for the same filter — keep them.
- `CIDissolveTransition: CITransitionFilter` ⇒ keys `kCIInputImageKey`, `kCIInputTargetImageKey`, `kCIInputTimeKey` (time 0→from, 1→to). Verified.

## 1. New file `WorkingSpace.swift`

```swift
import CoreGraphics

/// Single source of truth for (a) the color space the LUT cube interpolates in
/// and (b) the space images are encoded to on export. Replaces the hardcoded
/// `CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!` literals in CubeLUT.makeFilter and
/// ImageProcessor.export. Default `.sRGB` preserves today's behavior exactly;
/// `.displayP3` is reserved (NOT wired to UI yet).
///
/// Deliberately NOT part of any saved edit state — it is output configuration,
/// not "the look". RecipeExtractor does NOT read `.current` (its sampling stays
/// pinned to `.sRGB`; see the RecipeExtractor change).
enum WorkingSpace: String, Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    case sRGB
    case displayP3

    var cgColorSpace: CGColorSpace {
        switch self {
        case .sRGB:      return CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!
        case .displayP3: return CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.displayP3)!
        }
    }

    /// The one line to flip when P3 ships (after the gamut open-questions resolve).
    static let current: WorkingSpace = .sRGB
}
```
Both names are guaranteed-resolvable system constants ⇒ the `!` is safe and now lives in exactly one place.

---

## 2. CubeLUT.swift — rewrite ONLY the `// MARK: - Core Image Filter` block (128-144) + add 2 members

### 2a. Add a private filter cache near the stored props (after line 11)
Rationale: `makeFilter` currently rebuilds the cube filter (a 33³×4-float upload) on EVERY apply — under a live intensity slider that runs per tick.
```swift
/// Memoizes the cube CIFilter per working color space so the table
/// (size^3 x 4 floats) isn't re-uploaded every render tick. Reference box
/// because CubeLUT is a value type with `let` storage and these methods
/// stay non-mutating. Excluded from == / hash (those key on `id` only).
private let filterCache = FilterCache()

private final class FilterCache {
    var byColorSpaceName: [String: CIFilter] = [:]
}
```

### 2b. `makeFilter()` → `makeFilter(colorSpace:)`
BEFORE (131-137):
```swift
func makeFilter() -> CIFilter? {
    guard let filter = CIFilter(name: "CIColorCubeWithColorSpace") else { return nil }
    filter.setValue(size, forKey: "inputCubeDimension")
    filter.setValue(tableData as NSData, forKey: "inputCubeData")
    filter.setValue(CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!, forKey: "inputColorSpace")
    return filter
}
```
AFTER:
```swift
/// Builds (and caches) the CIColorCubeWithColorSpace filter that interpolates
/// this LUT in `colorSpace`. Caller sets kCIInputImageKey before reading outputImage.
func makeFilter(colorSpace: CGColorSpace) -> CIFilter? {
    let key = (colorSpace.name as String?) ?? "unknown"
    if let cached = filterCache.byColorSpaceName[key] { return cached }
    guard let filter = CIFilter(name: "CIColorCubeWithColorSpace") else { return nil }
    filter.setValue(size,                forKey: "inputCubeDimension")
    filter.setValue(tableData as NSData, forKey: "inputCubeData")
    filter.setValue(colorSpace,          forKey: "inputColorSpace")
    // (Do NOT set "inputExtrapolate" here — leave default false to preserve
    //  current [0,1]-clamped behavior. It becomes relevant only on the P3 /
    //  extended-range path; see open questions.)
    filterCache.byColorSpaceName[key] = filter
    return filter
}
```
Keys unchanged & exact; only the space source changes.

### 2c. `apply(to:)` → `apply(to:colorSpace:)` (full-strength, == old behavior minus hardcoded space)
BEFORE (139-144):
```swift
func apply(to image: CIImage) -> CIImage? {
    guard let filter = makeFilter() else { return nil }
    filter.setValue(image, forKey: kCIInputImageKey)
    return filter.outputImage
}
```
AFTER:
```swift
func apply(to image: CIImage, colorSpace: CGColorSpace) -> CIImage? {
    guard let filter = makeFilter(colorSpace: colorSpace) else { return nil }
    filter.setValue(image, forKey: kCIInputImageKey)
    return filter.outputImage
}
```

### 2d. NEW `applyWithIntensity(to:intensity:colorSpace:)` — the node entry point
NON-optional: a failed LUT must never blank the preview; it falls back to the input. intensity clamped to [0,1].
```swift
/// Apply this LUT at `intensity` (0...1) blending the ORIGINAL input toward the
/// graded output. Blend happens in `colorSpace` (same space the cube interpolates
/// in) — a deliberate, documented choice (NOT linear-light). intensity>=1 -> fully
/// graded; intensity<=0 -> untouched input. Never returns nil.
func applyWithIntensity(to image: CIImage,
                        intensity: Double,
                        colorSpace: CGColorSpace) -> CIImage {
    let t = min(1.0, max(0.0, intensity))
    if t <= 0 { return image }
    guard let graded = apply(to: image, colorSpace: colorSpace) else { return image }
    if t >= 1 { return graded }

    // CIDissolveTransition(time:) returns inputImage at 0 and inputTargetImage at 1,
    // so time == t gives the t% graded mix.
    guard let dissolve = CIFilter(name: "CIDissolveTransition") else { return graded }
    dissolve.setValue(image,  forKey: kCIInputImageKey)        // from
    dissolve.setValue(graded, forKey: kCIInputTargetImageKey)  // to
    dissolve.setValue(t,      forKey: kCIInputTimeKey)         // 0...1 (NSNumber from Double is fine)
    guard let mixed = dissolve.outputImage else { return graded }
    return mixed.cropped(to: image.extent)   // transitions output a union extent; pin back
}
```
Implementer notes:
- Keys are exact and SDK-verified. Typed alternative: `let d = CIFilter.dissolveTransition(); d.inputImage = image; d.targetImage = graded; d.time = Float(t)` (needs `import CoreImage.CIFilterBuiltins`). Either is fine; the string form avoids the Float cast.
- `graded` and `image` are both working-space CIImages ⇒ the dissolve linearly interpolates pre vs post in `colorSpace`. Documented behavior; a future linear-light mix is a *versioned look change* (open question), not this component.
- `.cropped(to: image.extent)` is mandatory — `CIDissolveTransition` is a transition filter whose output extent is the union of inputs; the crop makes export size bullet-proof.

## 3. Call-site updates

### 3a. AppViewModel — intensity state + applyLUT()
Add near 18-19:
```swift
@Published var selectedLUT: CubeLUT?
@Published var processedImage: CIImage?
@Published var lutIntensity: Double = 1.0 { didSet { applyLUT() } }   // 0...1, UI shows 0–100%
```
(The full debounce/EditDocument coalescing is the sibling state/concurrency component — here just wire the trigger. The cube-filter cache in 2a blunts per-tick cost meanwhile.)

BEFORE (Task body, 236-244):
```swift
previewTask = Task {
    guard let result = lut.apply(to: source) else {
        self.statusMessage = "LUT application failed"
        return
    }
    guard !Task.isCancelled else { return }
    self.processedImage = result
    self.updatePreview(result)
}
```
AFTER:
```swift
let space = WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace
let intensity = lutIntensity
previewTask = Task {
    let result = lut.applyWithIntensity(to: source, intensity: intensity, colorSpace: space)
    guard !Task.isCancelled else { return }
    self.processedImage = result
    self.updatePreview(result)
}
```
The nil/"LUT application failed" branch is dropped (the call can't return nil). A broken cube now shows the original — safer UX. Export parity holds because exportDialog reuses `processedImage` (283); but see 3c for the export space.

### 3b. Delete zero-arg APIs, fix callers
`grep` `.apply(to:` and `.makeFilter()`. Only production caller is applyLUT (237), handled above. RecipeExtractor builds cubes via `CubeLUT(cube:size:...)` and never calls apply/makeFilter. After edits the zero-arg forms don't exist — a clean build catches any #Preview/test caller.

### 3c. ImageProcessor.export() — output-encoding half of the seam
BEFORE (174 + 180):
```swift
func export(_ ciImage: CIImage, to url: URL, format: ExportFormat, quality: CGFloat = 0.95) throws {
    ...
    let colorSpace = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!
```
AFTER:
```swift
func export(_ ciImage: CIImage, to url: URL, format: ExportFormat,
            colorSpace: CGColorSpace = WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace,
            quality: CGFloat = 0.95) throws {
    ...
    // delete the local sRGB line; the encoders at 188/196/207 already use `colorSpace`.
```
exportDialog (AppViewModel:299) needs no change (uses default). CRITICAL invariant: cube space (2b) and export space MUST stay equal — both now read `WorkingSpace.current`, so they move in lockstep by construction. Never change one without the other.

### 3d. RecipeExtractor (line 81) — funnel the literal, keep the contract
BEFORE: `let sRGB = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!`
AFTER:  `let sRGB = WorkingSpace.sRGB.cgColorSpace`
That is the ONLY change. NOT `.current`; do not touch `ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral`. A derived `.cube` encodes neutral-RAW-in-sRGB → JPEG and is later applied by makeFilter in `WorkingSpace.current`; v1 keeps both sRGB so fit-space == apply-space trivially. Pinning derive to `.sRGB` makes the decoupling explicit and crash-proof (one construction site) without coupling derive to the user/output space.
