## 4. Untouched in CubeLUT.swift
`init(cube:size:name:category:sourceURL:)` (23-46), `init(url:category:)` parser (50-126), `cubeFileContents` (150-170), `write` (173-181), `==`/`hash` (15-16), `enum LUTError` (186-194). Diff confined to the Core Image Filter block + the 2 added members.

**Testing:** No XCTest target exists today (zero test files in repo). Add a LUTzyTests target; all checks below are deterministic and offline.

SEAM:
1. `WorkingSpace.sRGB.cgColorSpace.name == CGColorSpace.sRGB`, `.displayP3.cgColorSpace.name == CGColorSpace.displayP3`, both non-nil. `WorkingSpace.current == .sRGB` (guards against an accidental P3 flip shipping).

INTENSITY BOUNDARIES (highest value):
2. Build an IDENTITY cube via `CubeLUT(cube:size:name:)` (size 2, the 8 corner identity triples) and a 1x1 known-color CIImage (CIImage(color:) cropped to 1x1). Render one RGBA8 pixel via a local CIContext with WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace:
   - intensity 0 ⇒ pixel == input (±1/255).
   - intensity 1 ⇒ pixel == `apply(to:colorSpace:)` (identity ⇒ also == input).
   - Use a NON-identity cube (e.g. maps all inputs to mid-gray); intensity 0.5 ⇒ pixel halfway between input and mid-gray (±2/255). Proves the dissolve mixes pre↔post.
   - intensity 2.0 clamps to 1 (fully graded); intensity -1 clamps to 0 (input).
3. Non-optional contract: assert `applyWithIntensity` returns a renderable image for a valid cube, and code-review that both `guard ... else { return image/graded }` fallbacks exist (a broken LUT must never blank the preview).

EXTENT:
4. After intensity 0.5 on a known-extent image, assert `result.extent == input.extent` (proves the `.cropped(to:)` guard against CIDissolveTransition extent growth).

CACHE:
5. `makeFilter(colorSpace:)` twice with same space ⇒ identical instance (`===`); two different spaces ⇒ two instances. Confirms keying on colorSpace.name and auto-invalidation on a future P3 switch.

PARITY (correctness requirement):
6. Render fixed image+LUT at intensity 0.5 two ways: (a) applyWithIntensity then `context.createCGImage(..., colorSpace: WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace)` full size; (b) `export(..., format: .png, colorSpace: WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace)` then decode PNG. Center-pixel match ±1/255. NOTE today's renderPreview omits an explicit colorSpace (line 109) — the sibling RenderEngine/preview component must pass WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace to createCGImage; until it does, on-screen vs file can differ at non-default settings. Flag if that sibling isn't landed.

REGRESSION — derive unchanged:
7. Run RecipeExtractor.derive on a known (RAW,JPG) fixture BEFORE/AFTER; produced cube arrays must be identical (only the sRGB construction site changed; same CGColorSpace). If no RAW fixture in CI, at minimum assert `WorkingSpace.sRGB.cgColorSpace.name == CGColorSpace.sRGB` so the substitution is provably behavior-preserving.

REGRESSION — default look unchanged:
8. With current == .sRGB and lutIntensity == 1.0, applyWithIntensity pixels must equal the OLD apply(to:) output (hardcoded sRGB) within ±1/255. Proves the refactor is a no-op at defaults.

BUILD/SMOKE:
9. Clean build with no remaining refs to zero-arg makeFilter()/apply(to:) (compiler is the test). Manual: open a JPG, pick a LUT, drag intensity 100→0 and confirm the preview cross-fades to original and back; export PNG at 50% and eyeball vs on-screen 50%.

**Reviewer-flagged issues:**
- BUILD-SYSTEM MISMATCH (real, medium): The spec's filesToChange repeatedly says the new WorkingSpace.swift must be 'added to the LUTzy app target in the .xcodeproj (same group as CubeLUT.swift) or it won't compile.' But `git ls-files` shows NO .xcodeproj is tracked; the repo builds via Swift Package Manager (/Users/tim/.../Package.swift is the only build manifest, and source lives under LUTzy/Models, LUTzy/ViewModels, LUTzy/Views). With SPM the new file under LUTzy/Models/ is auto-included by the target's path globbing -- there is no pbxproj membership step. The instruction is misleading: drop the .xcodeproj membership requirement and just place WorkingSpace.swift under LUTzy/Models/. (If an .xcodeproj exists locally but is gitignored, the spec should say so; as tracked, it does not exist.)
- PREVIEW/EXPORT PARITY IS NOT CLOSED BY THIS COMPONENT (correctly self-flagged, but it leaves a live color mismatch). Verified at ImageProcessor.swift:109: renderPreview calls `context.createCGImage(scaled, from: scaled.extent)` with NO colorSpace argument, while export (line 180, becoming a param) forces sRGB. After this component lands, at default sRGB the two still agree, but the moment the cube interpolation space and export space are threaded (and especially if WorkingSpace.current ever flips), on-screen vs file diverge until the sibling RenderEngine/preview component passes WorkingSpace.current.cgColorSpace into createCGImage. The spec acknowledges this in risks, but it means the stated goal 'preview/export parity' is only partially delivered here; the renderPreview fix must be sequenced into the SAME release or parity regresses. Flagging as an issue, not just an open question, because it is a correctness property the brief lists as a requirement.
- INTENSITY BLEND COLOR-MANAGEMENT SUBTLETY (medium, under-specified). CIDissolveTransition mixes pre-LUT `image` and post-LUT `graded` by linear per-channel interpolation IN THE CICONTEXT WORKING SPACE, which is NOT the same as `colorSpace` passed to makeFilter. makeFilter's inputColorSpace only tells CIColorCubeWithColorSpace which space to convert into before the cube lookup; it does NOT change the numeric space the later CIDissolveTransition operates in (that is governed by kCIContextWorkingColorSpace, left at CI default = linearized sRGB-ish). So the spec's repeated claim that the dissolve 'blends in WorkingSpace.cgColorSpace, the same space the cube interpolates in' is not accurate as written -- the blend space is the context working space, independent of the colorSpace argument. The behavior is still deterministic and identical in preview/export (both use the same context), so it is not a correctness bug, but the DESIGN RATIONALE/documentation is wrong and the 'cache invalidation keyed on colorSpace.name covers a P3 switch' reasoning doesn't extend to the blend. Tighten the wording: intensity is blended in the context working space; the colorSpace arg only governs cube interpolation.
- SILENT-FAILURE BEHAVIOR CHANGE (minor, real). Verified AppViewModel.swift:237-240 currently sets statusMessage='LUT application failed' when lut.apply returns nil. The new applyWithIntensity is non-optional and the spec deletes that branch, so a structurally broken cube (e.g. CIFilter(name:) returns nil, or a malformed table) now silently shows the original image with NO user feedback. apply(to:colorSpace:) can still return nil internally (makeFilter can fail), and applyWithIntensity swallows it. For a power-user tool that loads arbitrary .cube files this is a real UX regression; pair the deletion with a one-time validity check at parse/load (the spec lists this as an open question but ships the regression by default).
- kCIInputTimeKey TYPE via raw KVC (low, verify-at-runtime). The string-KVC path does `dissolve.setValue(t, forKey: kCIInputTimeKey)` with `t` a Swift Double. CIDissolveTransition.time is a float-backed scalar; passing an NSNumber-from-Double normally bridges fine, but the spec itself notes the typed API wants Float(t). Recommend the typed builder (CIFilter.dissolveTransition(); d.time = Float(t)) to remove the bridging ambiguity, especially since RecipeExtractor already imports CoreImage.CIFilterBuiltins so the typed filters are clearly available in this codebase. (Could not independently confirm against the live SDK header -- the sandbox blocked access to the macOS SDK / xcrun -- so treating as a low-risk verify item rather than a defect.)
- MINOR: spec's prose elsewhere in the binding doc references 'ImageProcessor.swift:90-92' and 'line 92/163' for the affine downscale and export colorSpace, but in THIS component the cited anchors (export 174/180, encoders 188/196/207, renderPreview 109, RecipeExtractor 81, makeFilter 131-137, apply 140-144, tableData 11) are all correct against the real files. Only the cross-referenced binding-doc line numbers are off-by-a-couple; not blocking, but don't trust the binding-doc's minified numbers when implementing -- use the component spec's, which I verified.

**Open questions:**
- Filter cache ownership: I put a private reference-box cache (FilterCache) on the CubeLUT value type so makeFilter(colorSpace:) memoizes without making the struct mutable. The binding decision implies the cube filter cache could instead live on the sibling `actor RenderEngine` (keyed by LUTID + colorSpace name). Both work; pick ONE to avoid double caching. If RenderEngine owns it, drop FilterCache here and makeFilter becomes a pure builder. Recommend keeping the small box here for now (works even before RenderEngine lands) and letting RenderEngine skip its own cube cache.
- Intensity blend space: the dissolve mixes pre/post-LUT in WorkingSpace, NOT linear light. Deliberate NEW behavior (no prior to preserve — apply(to:) was full-strength only). Needs a quick visual sign-off at ~50% on a few LUTs; switching to a linear-light mix later is a versioned look change (every sub-100% render shifts) and would bracket the dissolve with colorSpace conversions.
- Silent failure UX: applyWithIntensity now returns the original image on any internal failure (never nil), dropping the old 'LUT application failed' statusMessage (AppViewModel:238). Confirm silent-fallback is desired, or add a one-time validity check on CubeLUT at parse/load time so failures are reported once, not per-render.
- `extrapolate` (CIColorCubeWithColorSpace, macOS 13+): left default false to preserve current [0,1] behavior. It becomes relevant if/when the working space widens to extended-range/linear or P3 (out-of-[0,1] inputs). Decide whether to set inputExtrapolate=true together with any future kCIContextWorkingColorSpace change — paired with the 8-bit-gamut clamp question owned by the export/engine sibling.
- P3 follow-up (latent, not this component): when WorkingSpace.current flips to .displayP3, cube space and export space move together automatically, BUT RecipeExtractor still fits LUTs in sRGB. A derived CubeLUT must then record the space it was built in so makeFilter applies it in the matching space (or derive must re-fit in P3). v1 keeps everything sRGB so this is dormant; the LUT-build-space stamp is the concrete prerequisite before enabling P3.
- ImageProcessor.export signature: I inserted `colorSpace:` (defaulted) BEFORE the existing `quality:` param. Only caller is exportDialog (passes neither), so safe, but keep colorSpace defaulted and prefer labeled args to avoid a positional-arg trap later.

**Risks:**
- Preview/export color mismatch persists UNTIL the sibling render component passes an explicit colorSpace to createCGImage. This component fixes the cube space (makeFilter) and the export space (ImageProcessor.export), but ImageProcessor.renderPreview (line 109) still calls createCGImage WITHOUT a colorSpace — the implicit 4th sRGB site the brief flags, owned by the render/engine sibling. If that lands later, on-screen vs file can differ at non-default settings. Parity test #6 catches it; sequence the renderPreview colorSpace fix into the same release.
- CIDissolveTransition extent growth: transition filters output the union extent of both inputs. Both inputs share image.extent here so it's already equal, but without `.cropped(to: image.extent)` a rounding could leak into export size. Keep the guard.
- Deleting zero-arg makeFilter()/apply(to:) is source-breaking. The compiler catches production callers, but any SwiftUI #Preview/playground/test referencing them breaks — do a full-target build (test #9) before considering it done.
- Reference-type cache on a value type: FilterCache makes CubeLUT no longer a pure value type. == / hash are hand-written on `id` so equality is unaffected, and copies share the immutable box (fine — same table/id). But if the project later wants CubeLUT: Sendable, the mutable-dictionary box needs an actor/lock or removal (defer to the RenderEngine-owns-cache option).
- Derive baseline regression if someone 'cleans up' RecipeExtractor line 81 to WorkingSpace.current instead of .sRGB: that silently re-fits every derived LUT in P3 once current flips, corrupting correspondences. The spec pins it to .sRGB and documents why — call this out in code review so it isn't refactored to .current.
- Intensity state placement: lutIntensity + didSet trigger on AppViewModel is the minimal wiring; the sibling state/concurrency component intends to fold this into EditDocument with debounce. Until then, dragging the slider re-runs applyLUT() per tick on the main actor (existing responsiveness smell, not introduced here). The cube-filter cache (2a) blunts the worst cost; don't duplicate the debounce here.

### AppViewModel / edit-state refactor

**Verdict:** major-issues (concurrency FLAG · color OK · preview/export OK)

**Summary.** Replace AppViewModel's implicit render-state triple (`sourceImage` CIImage + `selectedLUT: CubeLUT?` + `processedImage` CIImage) with a single `@Published var document: EditDocument` (Codable/Sendable/Equatable value type) per the binding decision. The VM keeps a non-published `ImageSource` (URL + kind + nativeExtent) so RAW can be re-developed each render, holds a per-URL `[URL: EditDocument]` store for browsing the ImageCollection, and an `UndoManager`-free lightweight undo stack of `EditDocument` snapshots. `selectLUT` now mutates `document.lut` (and registers/resolves the LUT through `RenderEngine` by `LUTID`) instead of calling `applyLUT` directly. All render entry points (`applyLUT` -> renamed `scheduleRender`, `showOriginal`, `exportDialog`) funnel through one debounced async render that calls the `actor RenderEngine` off the main actor and writes back `previewNSImage` on the main actor, reusing and upgrading the existing single-`previewTask` cancellation. The component depends on `EditDocument`, `RAWDevelopSettings`, `AdjustmentNode`, `LUTSettings`/`LUTID`, `ImageSource`, `RenderScale`, `WorkingSpace`, and `RenderEngine` being delivered by the sibling render-pipeline component; this spec defines their consumption surface and stubs the minimum it needs so it can be coded in parallel.

## Component: AppViewModel / edit-state refactor — implementation spec

All line numbers below are the REAL current `AppViewModel.swift` (verified on disk, file is 408 lines). The brief's subsystem-map line numbers (224/251/277…) are stale; use the ones here.

> NOTE on the binding decision: this component is the *consumer* of the value-state spine. The types `EditDocument`, `RAWDevelopSettings`, `AdjustmentNode`, `LUTSettings`, `LUTID`, `ImageSource`, `RenderScale`, `WorkingSpace`, and `actor RenderEngine` are *owned* by the render-pipeline component. To let both be coded in parallel, this spec (a) restates the exact required public surface it consumes, and (b) confines everything new-and-owned-here to `EditDocumentStore` + the VM rewrite. If the sibling component has already created `EditDocument.swift`, do not duplicate it.

---

### 1. Required consumption surface (must exist; restated, not redefined here)

```swift
// EditDocument.swift — owned by render-pipeline component.
struct EditDocument: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    var version: Int = 1
    var rawDevelop: RAWDevelopSettings = .neutral
    var adjustments: [AdjustmentNode] = []
    var lut: LUTSettings = .none
}
struct LUTSettings: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    var lutID: LUTID? = nil
    var intensity: Double = 1.0      // 0...1
    static let none = LUTSettings()
}
struct LUTID: Codable, Sendable, Equatable, Hashable { let raw: UUID }

struct ImageSource: Sendable, Equatable {
    enum Kind: Sendable, Equatable { case raw, standard }
    let url: URL
    let kind: Kind
    let nativeExtent: CGSize
}
enum RenderScale: Sendable, Equatable { case preview(maxSize: CGSize); case full }
enum WorkingSpace: String, Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    case sRGB, displayP3
    var cgColorSpace: CGColorSpace { /* … */ CGColorSpace(name: .sRGB)! }
    static let current: WorkingSpace = .sRGB
}

actor RenderEngine {
    static let shared: RenderEngine
    func register(_ lut: CubeLUT, for id: LUTID)
    func unregister(_ id: LUTID)
    // measures native extent + decides RAW vs standard from one shared ext set
    func makeSource(url: URL) async -> ImageSource?
    func renderPreview(source: ImageSource, document: EditDocument,
                       maxSize: CGSize, space: WorkingSpace) async -> CGImage?
    func export(source: ImageSource, document: EditDocument, to url: URL,
                format: ImageProcessor.ExportFormat, space: WorkingSpace,
                quality: CGFloat) async throws
    func thumbnail(url: URL, maxPixel: CGFloat) async -> CGImage?
}
```

**Minimum I depend on:** `RenderEngine.makeSource`, `.renderPreview` (returns `CGImage?`), `.export`, `.register/unregister`. If the sibling component's `RenderEngine` is not ready, code against a protocol `RenderEngining` with these four async methods and inject `RenderEngine.shared`; a `FakeRenderEngine` then unblocks VM tests (see §11).

### 2. New state model on AppViewModel (replaces lines 13–21 and 158)

**BEFORE (AppViewModel.swift:13–21, :158):**

```swift
@Published var sourceName: String = ""        // :14
@Published var sourceSize: CGSize = .zero     // :15
@Published var sourceURL: URL?                // :16
@Published var selectedLUT: CubeLUT?          // :20
@Published var processedImage: CIImage?       // :21
private let maxPreview = CGSize(width: 1600, height: 1200) // :158
```

**AFTER:**
```swift
// MARK: - Edit state (single source of truth)

/// THE look for the currently-active image. Mutating this is the ONLY way to
/// change the render. didSet schedules a debounced re-render.
@Published var document: EditDocument = EditDocument() {
    didSet {
        guard document != oldValue else { return }   // Equatable gate; no-op churn ignored
        store.update(document, for: activeURL)        // persist per-image
        scheduleRender()                              // debounced
    }
}

// Source identity (NOT a live CIImage anymore — only a URL-backed descriptor).
@Published private(set) var sourceName: String = ""
@Published private(set) var sourceSize: CGSize = .zero
/// The render input descriptor. nil until an image is opened.
private(set) var source: ImageSource?
/// Convenience used by export-name, collection, per-image store keys.
var activeURL: URL? { source?.url }

// Preview output (unchanged surface for the views).
@Published var previewNSImage: NSImage?
@Published var originalPreviewNSImage: NSImage?
@Published var isShowingOriginal: Bool = false
@Published var isSideBySide: Bool = false

// Per-image edit-state store + undo (see §6, §7).
private var store = EditDocumentStore()

// Render plumbing.
private let maxPreview = CGSize(width: 1600, height: 1200)   // keep at old :158 value
private let workingSpace: WorkingSpace = .current
private var renderTask: Task<Void, Never>?                    // replaces previewTask (:55)
private var lastRenderedRequest: AppViewModel.RenderRequestID?

// LUT identity bridge: maps a CubeLUT.id (UUID) <-> LUTID and keeps the
// in-memory CubeLUT alive + registered with the engine.
private var lutsByID: [LUTID: CubeLUT] = [:]
```

`selectedLUT: CubeLUT?` and `processedImage: CIImage?` are **deleted**. Anything that read `selectedLUT` reads a computed bridge instead (see §5). `processedImage` had exactly one external consumer — `exportDialog` — which is rewritten in §8.

#### 2.1 Compatibility shims to limit view churn
LUTSidebar (line 96/142) calls `selectLUT`/needs to know the current selection and intensity. Add computed bindings so existing views barely change:
```swift
/// Read-only current selection, derived from the document (replaces @Published selectedLUT).
var selectedLUT: CubeLUT? { document.lut.lutID.flatMap { lutsByID[$0] } }

/// Two-way binding for the new intensity slider in LUTSidebar.
var lutIntensity: Double {
    get { document.lut.intensity }
    set { document.lut.intensity = newValue.clamped(to: 0...1) }   // mutation -> didSet -> render
}
```
`selectedLUT` is now a computed `var` (not `@Published`); SwiftUI still re-renders because `document` is `@Published` and `objectWillChange` fires on its mutation. The highlight in LUTSidebar's list (which compares `lut.id == viewModel.selectedLUT?.id`) keeps working unchanged.

### 3. Render scheduling: rename + upgrade `applyLUT` -> `scheduleRender` (replaces :160–186)

**BEFORE (AppViewModel.swift:160–186):**
```swift
private func applyLUT() {
    guard let source = sourceImage else { return }
    previewTask?.cancel()
    guard let lut = selectedLUT else {
        processedImage = nil
        updatePreview(source)
        return
    }
    previewTask = Task {                               // inherits @MainActor — bug
        let result = lut.apply(to: source)            // full-extent graph on main thread
        guard !Task.isCancelled else { return }
        if let result = result {
            self.processedImage = result
            self.updatePreview(result)
        }
    }
}
private func updatePreview(_ ciImage: CIImage) {
    previewNSImage = processor.renderPreview(ciImage, maxSize: maxPreview)   // sync, main thread
}
private func updateOriginalPreview(_ ciImage: CIImage) {
    originalPreviewNSImage = processor.renderPreview(ciImage, maxSize: maxPreview)
}
```

**AFTER:**
```swift
private let debounceInterval: Duration = .milliseconds(80)   // power-user slider coalescing

/// Schedules a debounced, cancellable, OFF-main-actor preview render of the
/// current `document`. Replaces applyLUT(). Safe to call on every slider tick.
private func scheduleRender() {
    guard let source else { previewNSImage = nil; return }

    let request = RenderRequestID(url: source.url, document: document)
    // Coalesce: if an identical request already produced the current preview, skip.
    if request == lastRenderedRequest { return }

    renderTask?.cancel()                                 // reuse the single-token pattern
    let space = workingSpace
    let maxSize = isShowingOriginal ? maxPreview : maxPreview   // same cap either way
    let docToRender = isShowingOriginal ? document.withLUTIntensityZero() : document

    renderTask = Task { [weak self] in
        // Debounce window — coalesces a burst of didSet mutations into one render.
        try? await Task.sleep(for: self?.debounceInterval ?? .zero)
        if Task.isCancelled { return }

        let cg = await RenderEngine.shared.renderPreview(
            source: source, document: docToRender,
            maxSize: maxSize, space: space)              // OFF main actor; engine owns CIContext

        if Task.isCancelled { return }
        await MainActor.run {
            guard let self else { return }
            // Drop if a newer request superseded us while awaiting.
            guard request == RenderRequestID(url: self.source?.url, document: self.document)
                  || self.isShowingOriginal else { return }
            if let cg {
                self.previewNSImage = NSImage(cgImage: cg,
                    size: NSSize(width: cg.width, height: cg.height))
            }
            self.lastRenderedRequest = request
        }
    }
}
```

Key upgrades vs the old `applyLUT`:
- **Off the main actor.** `RenderEngine` is an `actor`; `await` hops off main, so the heavy CIImage graph build + rasterize no longer blocks the UI (fixes the confirmed `Task { @MainActor }` stall).
- **Real cancellation.** `renderTask?.cancel()` + two `Task.isCancelled` checks (before the sleep elapses and before the write-back) mean a fast slider drag cancels in-flight work, not just the final assignment.
- **Debounce.** The `Task.sleep(80ms)` window coalesces continuous slider drags into one render. Each new `document` mutation cancels the prior sleeping task.
- **Staleness guard.** `RenderRequestID` (Equatable over `url` + `document`) drops results whose document no longer matches (e.g. user changed an adjustment mid-render). The `isShowingOriginal` clause keeps the A/B path correct.

`RenderRequestID`:
```swift
private struct RenderRequestID: Equatable {
    let url: URL?
    let document: EditDocument
}
```

`EditDocument.withLUTIntensityZero()` is a tiny helper on the document (place it next to `EditDocument`, or as a private VM extension if the sibling component hasn't added it):
```swift
extension EditDocument {
    func withLUTIntensityZero() -> EditDocument {
        var c = self; c.lut.intensity = 0; return c
    }
}
```

---

### 4. The "original" preview (updateOriginalPreview replacement)

`originalPreviewNSImage` is the *un-LUT'd, un-adjusted source* used by side-by-side and could double as the Space-hold target. Render it once per image open (and per RAW-develop change, since develop affects "original"), via a dedicated one-shot task — it does not need the debounce because it changes rarely:
```swift
private func renderOriginalPreview() {
    guard let source else { originalPreviewNSImage = nil; return }
    // "Original" = source through RAW develop only, NO adjustments, NO LUT.
    var bare = document
    bare.adjustments = []
    bare.lut = .none
    Task { [weak self, maxPreview, workingSpace] in
        let cg = await RenderEngine.shared.renderPreview(
            source: source, document: bare, maxSize: maxPreview, space: workingSpace)
        await MainActor.run {
            guard let self, let cg else { return }
            self.originalPreviewNSImage = NSImage(cgImage: cg,
                size: NSSize(width: cg.width, height: cg.height))
        }
    }
}
```
Decision to record for the UI component: "original" includes RAW develop (so A/B compares *graded* vs *developed-neutral-look*, not vs raw-demosaic). If product wants A/B vs the truly neutral RAW, set `bare.rawDevelop = .neutral` here too. Defaulting to *keep develop* matches photographer expectations (develop is "the negative", LUT is "the look").

---

### 5. `selectLUT` now mutates state instead of calling applyLUT (rewrite :129–137)

**BEFORE (AppViewModel.swift:129–137):**
```swift
func selectLUT(_ lut: CubeLUT?) {
    selectedLUT = lut
    if let lut = lut { statusMessage = "Applied \(lut.name)" }
    else { statusMessage = "Cleared LUT" }
    applyLUT()
}
```

**AFTER (signature unchanged — LUTSidebar:96/:142 keep compiling):**
```swift
func selectLUT(_ lut: CubeLUT?) {
    pushUndoSnapshot()                          // selection is an undoable edit
    if let lut {
        let id = ensureRegistered(lut)          // bridge CubeLUT -> LUTID + engine.register
        document.lut.lutID = id                 // <-- mutation; didSet schedules render
        statusMessage = "Applied \(lut.name)"
    } else {
        document.lut.lutID = nil                 // keep intensity as-is for re-enable UX
        statusMessage = "Cleared LUT"
    }
    // NOTE: no applyLUT() call. The document didSet (§2) drives the render.
}

/// Maps a CubeLUT (file-backed or derived) to a stable LUTID, caches it, and
/// registers it with the actor so RenderPipeline can resolve it during render.
private func ensureRegistered(_ lut: CubeLUT) -> LUTID {
    // Reuse a deterministic ID derived from CubeLUT.id (a UUID) so re-selecting
    // the same library LUT reuses the same registry slot.
    let id = LUTID(raw: lut.id)
    if lutsByID[id] == nil {
        lutsByID[id] = lut
        Task { await RenderEngine.shared.register(lut, for: id) }
    }
    return id
}
```
`selectPreviousLUT`/`selectNextLUT` (AppViewModel.swift:139–154) are **unchanged** — they still call `selectLUT(...)`, and now read the current selection through the computed `selectedLUT` (§2.1). Verify the `firstIndex(where: { $0.id == current.id })` still resolves: `current` comes from `selectedLUT` which is `lutsByID[document.lut.lutID]`, whose `.id` equals the library LUT's `.id`. ✔

### 6. Per-image edit state when browsing the ImageCollection (`EditDocumentStore.swift`, NEW)

Requirement: stepping through the filmstrip (arrow keys -> `collection.selectNext/Previous`, ImageCollection.swift:45/:50) must remember each image's edits. Today there is no per-image state at all.

```swift
// EditDocumentStore.swift  (nonisolated; pure value logic; Sendable)
struct EditDocumentStore: Sendable {
    private var byURL: [URL: EditDocument] = [:]

    /// Returns the saved document for a URL, or a fresh default if unseen.
    func document(for url: URL?) -> EditDocument {
        guard let url else { return EditDocument() }
        return byURL[url] ?? EditDocument()
    }

    /// Persists the document for a URL. No-op when url is nil (ad-hoc data image).
    mutating func update(_ doc: EditDocument, for url: URL?) {
        guard let url else { return }
        byURL[url] = doc
    }

    mutating func reset(_ url: URL?) {
        guard let url else { return }
        byURL[url] = EditDocument()
    }

    // --- bounded undo/redo, see §7 ---
    private var undo: [EditDocument] = []
    private var redo: [EditDocument] = []
    private let undoLimit = 50

    mutating func pushUndo(_ current: EditDocument) {
        undo.append(current)
        if undo.count > undoLimit { undo.removeFirst() }
        redo.removeAll()
    }
    var canUndo: Bool { !undo.isEmpty }
    var canRedo: Bool { !redo.isEmpty }

    /// Pops an undo state; caller passes the live document to push onto redo.
    mutating func popUndo(pushingRedo current: EditDocument) -> EditDocument? {
        guard let prev = undo.popLast() else { return nil }
        redo.append(current)
        return prev
    }
    mutating func popRedo(pushingUndo current: EditDocument) -> EditDocument? {
        guard let next = redo.popLast() else { return nil }
        undo.append(current)
        return next
    }
}
```

Design note: undo/redo here is **global across the session, last-write-wins**, not per-image. For v1 that is acceptable and matches single-document mental model; if per-image undo is wanted later, change `undo/redo` to `[URL: [EditDocument]]`. Flag in open questions.

**Switching images** — modify the open/collection-step paths. Currently `setActiveURL` is the choke (ImageCollection.swift:29). Add a VM method invoked whenever the active URL changes (call it from `openImage`, `openFolder`/`openMultiple`, and after `collection.selectNext/Previous`):
```swift
/// Call AFTER `source` has been (re)built for `url`. Loads that image's saved
/// edits (or defaults) and triggers preview render.
private func activate(source newSource: ImageSource) {
    // 1. Persist the outgoing image's doc (didSet already did, but be explicit on switch).
    store.update(document, for: source?.url)
    // 2. Swap source identity.
    source = newSource
    sourceName = newSource.url.lastPathComponent
    sourceSize = newSource.nativeExtent
    // 3. Load incoming doc WITHOUT re-pushing undo or double-rendering:
    let incoming = store.document(for: newSource.url)
    setDocumentSilently(incoming)        // assign without undo push (see §7)
    // 4. Re-register the doc's LUT if it came from a prior session/edit.
    rebindLUTIfNeeded(for: incoming)
    // 5. Render both panes.
    renderOriginalPreview()
    scheduleRender()
}

private func rebindLUTIfNeeded(for doc: EditDocument) {
    guard let id = doc.lut.lutID, lutsByID[id] == nil else { return }
    // Resolve from library by matching UUID; library LUTs use the same id.
    if let lut = library.luts.first(where: { LUTID(raw: $0.id) == id }) {
        lutsByID[id] = lut
        Task { await RenderEngine.shared.register(lut, for: id) }
    } else {
        // LUT not resolvable (e.g. a derived LUT from a prior image). Clear it.
        var fixed = doc; fixed.lut = .none
        setDocumentSilently(fixed)
    }
}
```

For collection stepping, wrap the existing nav so the VM rebuilds `source` and activates:
```swift
func goToImage(at url: URL) {
    isLoading = true
    Task { [weak self] in
        guard let src = await RenderEngine.shared.makeSource(url: url) else {
            await MainActor.run { self?.isLoading = false
                self?.statusMessage = "Failed to load \(url.lastPathComponent)" }
            return
        }
        await MainActor.run {
            guard let self else { return }
            self.collection.setActiveURL(url)
            self.activate(source: src)
            self.statusMessage = "Loaded \(url.lastPathComponent)"
            self.isLoading = false
        }
    }
}
func selectNextImage() { advanceImage(by: +1) }
func selectPreviousImage() { advanceImage(by: -1) }
private func advanceImage(by delta: Int) {
    let urls = collection.urls
    guard let cur = activeURL, let i = urls.firstIndex(of: cur) else { return }
    let j = i + delta
    guard urls.indices.contains(j) else { return }
    goToImage(at: urls[j])
}
```
Then ContentView's KeyMonitor (Left/Right -> `selectPrevious`/`selectNext`) must call `viewModel.selectPreviousImage()/selectNextImage()` instead of `collection.selectPrevious()/selectNext()` directly, so the document store swaps. (One-line change per arrow in ContentView; the brief's KeyMonitor maps keyCodes 123/124.)

---

### 7. Undo / Reset

Two undoable operations: any `document` mutation that comes from a *discrete user action* (selectLUT, a develop/adjustment slider *commit*, intensity *commit*), and Reset. Continuous slider *dragging* should NOT push 50 undo states per drag.

**Push points.** Add `pushUndoSnapshot()` and a silent setter:
```swift
private var isApplyingUndo = false   // guards didSet from re-pushing

/// Assigns the document without pushing undo and without re-persisting churn.
private func setDocumentSilently(_ doc: EditDocument) {
    isApplyingUndo = true
    document = doc            // didSet still runs render + store.update; that's fine
    isApplyingUndo = false
}

/// Snapshot CURRENT document onto the undo stack before a discrete edit.
func pushUndoSnapshot() {
    guard !isApplyingUndo else { return }
    store.pushUndo(document)
}
```
Call `pushUndoSnapshot()` at the *start* of discrete actions: `selectLUT` (§5), and from the UI on slider `onEditingChanged: { began in if began { vm.pushUndoSnapshot() } }` (the inspector component wires this — specified here so it knows the entry point). This yields one undo entry per drag gesture, not per tick.

**Undo/redo/reset API (public, bound to menu + ⌘Z / ⌘⇧Z):**
```swift
func undo() {
    guard let prev = store.popUndo(pushingRedo: document) else { return }
    setDocumentSilently(prev)
    statusMessage = "Undo"
}
func redo() {
    guard let next = store.popRedo(pushingUndo: document) else { return }
    setDocumentSilently(next)
    statusMessage = "Redo"
}
var canUndo: Bool { store.canUndo }
var canRedo: Bool { store.canRedo }

/// Reset the ACTIVE image's edits to neutral defaults (keeps the image open).
func resetEdits() {
    pushUndoSnapshot()                 // reset is undoable
    setDocumentSilently(EditDocument())
    store.reset(activeURL)
    statusMessage = "Reset adjustments"
}
```
Wire `.undo`/`.redo`/`.resetEdits` into `handleMenuCommand` (extend the `MenuCommand` enum — see ContentView's `MenuCommandReceivers`) and into the KeyMonitor pass-through (⌘Z must NOT be swallowed by the local monitor; the monitor already passes Command-modified events through per the brief). Add `.clearLUT` already exists at :322 — keep it; it now calls `selectLUT(nil)` which pushes undo.

### 8. Export rewiring (rewrite :209–238, uses document not processedImage)

**BEFORE (AppViewModel.swift:209–238):** captures `imageToExport = processedImage ?? sourceImage!` and `Task.detached { [processor, exportFormat] ... processor.export(imageToExport, …) }`.

**AFTER:**
```swift
func exportDialog() {
    guard let source else { return }
    let panel = NSSavePanel()
    panel.allowedContentTypes = [exportFormat.utType]
    panel.nameFieldStringValue = defaultExportName()
    panel.canCreateDirectories = true

    panel.begin { [weak self] response in
        guard let self, response == .OK, let url = panel.url else { return }
        self.isExporting = true
        let doc = self.document            // Sendable value snapshot
        let space = self.workingSpace
        let fmt = self.exportFormat
        Task {                             // no [processor] capture; engine is the actor
            do {
                try await RenderEngine.shared.export(
                    source: source, document: doc, to: url,
                    format: fmt, space: space, quality: 0.95)
                await MainActor.run {
                    self.isExporting = false
                    self.statusMessage = "Exported to \(url.lastPathComponent)"
                }
            } catch {
                await MainActor.run {
                    self.isExporting = false
                    self.statusMessage = "Export failed: \(error.localizedDescription)"
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```
This is the parity payoff: export renders the **same `EditDocument`** at `.full` through the **same RenderEngine** that preview uses at `.preview`. No more "export reuses a cached CIImage that merely happened to match". `defaultExportName()` (:240) keeps working but its `selectedLUT.map { "_\($0.name)" }` now reads the computed `selectedLUT` (§2.1) — unchanged behavior.

### 9. Open paths rewiring (rewrite :73–125, :348–354)

`openImage(at:)` (:73) and `openImage(data:name:)` (:102) must build an `ImageSource` via the engine instead of a `CIImage`, then `activate`.

**AFTER (URL path):**
```swift
func openImage(at url: URL) {
    isLoading = true
    statusMessage = "Loading \(url.lastPathComponent)…"
    Task { [weak self] in
        guard let src = await RenderEngine.shared.makeSource(url: url) else {
            await MainActor.run { self?.isLoading = false
                self?.statusMessage = "Failed to load \(url.lastPathComponent)" }
            return
        }
        await MainActor.run {
            guard let self else { return }
            self.collection.setActiveURL(url)
            self.activate(source: src)               // loads saved doc or defaults
            self.statusMessage = "Loaded \(url.lastPathComponent)"
            self.isLoading = false
        }
    }
}
```
**Data path** (`openImage(data:name:)`, :102): there is no URL, so `ImageSource` cannot be URL-backed. Two options: (a) write the data to a temp file and treat as URL (simplest, keeps one code path; reuse `derivedScratchURL`-style temp handling), or (b) extend `ImageSource` with a `case data(Data)` kind. **Choose (a)** to keep `ImageSource` purely URL-backed (the binding decision's rationale: "A URL, not a live CIImage → nothing non-Sendable is captured"). Implementation: persist `data` to `FileManager.default.temporaryDirectory`, then call `openImage(at: tempURL)`; track the temp URL to delete on next open. Per-image store keyed by the temp URL still works.

`openFolder`/`openMultiple` (:348/:352): after building `[URL]` and `collection.setURLs(_:active:)`, call `goToImage(at: firstURL)`.

### 10. Derive rewiring (modify :252–303)

`deriveRecipe` (:252) currently ends with `self.selectLUT(result.lut)` at :277. The derived `CubeLUT` is in-memory with a fresh `UUID`. The new `selectLUT` already calls `ensureRegistered`, which registers it under `LUTID(raw: result.lut.id)` and sets `document.lut.lutID`. **So the only change is: keep the `selectLUT(result.lut)` call — it now does the right thing.** Add one line so the derived LUT is also retrievable later by `rebindLUTIfNeeded` if the user navigates away and back:
```swift
// inside the MainActor.run after derive succeeds (around current :273–277):
self.derivedLUT = result.lut
self.derivedReport = result.report
self.selectLUT(result.lut)     // registers + sets document.lut.lutID (unchanged call)
self.statusMessage = "Derived LUT from recipe"
self.isDeriving = false
```
**Critical invariant (unchanged, just reaffirm in a code comment):** `RecipeExtractor.derive` still opens its OWN `CIRAWFilter(imageURL:)` at neutral defaults and never sees `document.rawDevelop`. The VM passes it only `rawURL`/`jpgURL`/`options` — no `EditDocument`, no `RAWDevelopSettings`. This component must NOT thread `document.rawDevelop` into `deriveRecipe`. Add an assertion-style doc comment at the `deriveRecipe` call site: `// Derive is develop-INDEPENDENT: never pass document.rawDevelop here.`

### 11. RAW develop + adjustments bindings (surface only; UI in sibling component)

The VM exposes typed two-way bindings the inspector binds to; each mutates `document` (→ didSet → debounced render). Example for the most common develop knobs (CIRAWFilter keys live in `RAWDevelopSettings.apply(to:)`, owned by the render component):
```swift
var exposure: Double {
    get { document.rawDevelop.exposure ?? 0 }
    set { document.rawDevelop.exposure = (newValue == 0 ? nil : newValue) }
}
var temperature: Double {
    get { document.rawDevelop.neutralTemperature ?? 5000 }
    set { document.rawDevelop.neutralTemperature = newValue }
}
// …tint, shadowBias, boostAmount, contrastAmount, localToneMapAmount similarly.
```
And for ordered adjustments (closed enum `AdjustmentNode`), the VM offers append/update/remove/move so the inspector edits the list:
```swift
func setAdjustment(_ node: AdjustmentNode, at index: Int) {
    guard document.adjustments.indices.contains(index) else { return }
    document.adjustments[index] = node            // didSet -> render
}
func addAdjustment(_ node: AdjustmentNode) { document.adjustments.append(node) }
func removeAdjustment(at index: Int) { document.adjustments.remove(at: index) }
func moveAdjustment(from: IndexSet, to: Int) { document.adjustments.move(fromOffsets: from, toOffset: to) }
```
Each of these is a discrete edit → the inspector calls `pushUndoSnapshot()` on gesture-begin. Continuous slider drags use `onEditingChanged` to push once.

`clamped(to:)` helper (add as a small `Comparable` extension if not present):
```swift
extension Comparable { func clamped(to r: ClosedRange<Self>) -> Self { min(max(self, r.lowerBound), r.upperBound) } }
```

### 12. Cleanup checklist (deletions in AppViewModel.swift)
- Delete `@Published var sourceImage` (:13), `selectedLUT` (:20), `processedImage` (:21).
- Delete `private let processor = ImageProcessor.shared` (:54) IF no other VM code uses `ImageProcessor` instance methods after the rewrite. Keep referencing `ImageProcessor.ExportFormat` (type, :35) and `ImageProcessor.openContentTypes` (:330) — these stay as static/type members.
- Delete `private var previewTask` (:55) → replaced by `renderTask`.
- Delete `applyLUT()` (:160), `updatePreview` (:180), `updateOriginalPreview` (:184) → replaced by `scheduleRender` / `renderOriginalPreview`.
- `showOriginal(_:)` (:190): rewrite to drive the A/B via the document, not cached CIImages:
```swift
func showOriginal(_ show: Bool) {
    isShowingOriginal = show
    scheduleRender()      // renders document or document.withLUTIntensityZero()
}
```
Or, for instant A/B with zero render latency, swap cached NSImages:
```swift
func showOriginal(_ show: Bool) {
    isShowingOriginal = show
    if show, originalPreviewNSImage != nil {
        // instant swap; previewNSImage temporarily shows the original
        gradedCache = previewNSImage
        previewNSImage = originalPreviewNSImage
    } else if let g = gradedCache {
        previewNSImage = g
    } else {
        scheduleRender()
    }
}
private var gradedCache: NSImage?
```
**Recommend the cached-swap variant** — Space-hold A/B must feel instant; the old code re-rasterized on every keyDown/keyUp (a confirmed perf smell). Keep `gradedCache` invalidated whenever `scheduleRender` writes a new `previewNSImage`.

**Testing:** UNIT (no GPU; fastest, run these first) — `EditDocumentStoreTests.swift`:
1. `document(for:)` returns a default `EditDocument()` for an unseen URL and for nil.
2. `update` then `document(for:)` round-trips the same value (Equatable).
3. Per-image isolation: update doc A for urlA, doc B for urlB; reading each returns the right one.
4. `reset(url)` returns that URL to defaults; other URLs untouched.
5. Undo stack: push 3 distinct docs, `popUndo` returns them LIFO; `canUndo` flips false when empty.
6. Redo symmetry: undo twice, redo twice returns to the original; a fresh `pushUndo` clears redo (`canRedo == false`).
7. Bound: push `undoLimit + 10`; `undo.count == undoLimit` and oldest dropped (assert via popping down).

@MainActor VM TESTS — `AppViewModelEditStateTests.swift` (inject a `FakeRenderEngine` conforming to `RenderEngining` that records calls and returns a 1x1 `CGImage`; this is why §1 recommends a protocol seam):
8. `selectLUT(lut)` sets `document.lut.lutID == LUTID(raw: lut.id)`, leaves `document.lut.intensity` unchanged, and does NOT throw/await before returning. Assert `selectedLUT?.id == lut.id` via the computed bridge.
9. `selectLUT(nil)` clears `document.lut.lutID` but preserves `intensity`.
10. Mutating `lutIntensity` (or `document` directly) triggers exactly ONE render after the debounce window for a burst of N rapid sets within 80ms (assert FakeRenderEngine.renderPreviewCallCount == 1 after `await Task.sleep(150ms)`). This proves coalescing.
11. Staleness: set document to A, then immediately to B; after settling, the final `previewNSImage` corresponds to B's request (FakeRenderEngine returns a distinguishable image keyed by document; assert last write matches B). Confirms the `RenderRequestID` guard.
12. Per-image switch: open urlA (FakeEngine.makeSource stub), set an adjustment; `goToImage(urlB)`; assert `document` reset to defaults for B; `goToImage(urlA)` restores A's edits.
13. Undo/redo: `selectLUT(x)` then `undo()` clears the LUT; `redo()` re-applies it. `resetEdits()` zeroes the doc and is itself undoable.
14. Export uses the document: call `exportDialog`-equivalent path with a stubbed save URL (factor the post-panel body into `performExport(to:)` so it's testable without NSSavePanel); assert FakeRenderEngine.export received the CURRENT `document` and `workingSpace`, NOT a stale one.
15. Derive decoupling: assert `deriveRecipe` is called with no `EditDocument`/`RAWDevelopSettings` argument (compile-time: the signature has none) and that after success `document.lut.lutID != nil` and the derived LUT is registered (FakeRenderEngine.registerCallCount incremented).

CONCURRENCY/STRICT-CONCURRENCY:
16. Build the target with `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY=complete` (add to a test xcconfig). Expect ZERO Sendable diagnostics on the VM↔RenderEngine boundary because only value types cross. `CGImage` crossing back is acceptable; if the compiler complains, wrap the write-back in `MainActor.run` (already done).
17. Verify no `CIImage`/`CIContext`/`CIFilter` is stored on AppViewModel after the refactor (grep the file).

MANUAL / INTEGRATION (real GPU, real RenderEngine):
18. Preview/export parity: open a RAW, apply a LUT at 60% intensity + an exposure adjustment, export TIFF; sample a few pixels in the exported file vs a full-res render of the same document — must match within rounding (same `buildImage`, same space).
19. Slider responsiveness: drag the intensity slider continuously on a 45MP RAW; UI must stay interactive (renders coalesced, off main actor) — contrast with current jank.
20. Filmstrip memory: load a folder, edit image 1, arrow to image 3 and edit, arrow back to 1 — edits restored; arrow to an unedited image shows neutral.
21. A/B: hold Space — instant swap to original (cached), release — instant back to graded. No fl__icker, no re-render delay.
22. Undo across navigation: edit image 1, navigate to 2, ⌘Z — confirm the documented behavior (global last-write undo affects whatever doc is active; if this surprises, see open question on per-image undo).

**Reviewer-flagged issues:**
- COMPILE BREAK (verified) - CubeLUT.id is String, not UUID. CubeLUT.swift:6 `let id: String` (file path, or synthetic `derived://name/UUID` at line 35). The spec's `ensureRegistered` (`LUTID(raw: lut.id)`) and `rebindLUTIfNeeded` (`LUTID(raw: $0.id) == id`) require UUID since LUTID.raw is UUID. String!=UUID -> does not compile. This is the central break: the entire LUTID-from-CubeLUT.id bridge in 5/6 is invalid. Fix: make LUTID wrap a String (CubeLUT.id is already stable and a better key than a fresh UUID; also fixes the cross-session-stability open question).
- COMPILE BREAK (verified) - `library.luts` does not exist. LUTLibrary exposes `allLUTs: [CubeLUT]` and `categories: [Category]` (each Category has `.luts`), but no top-level `.luts`. Real VM/views use `library.allLUTs` (AppViewModel.swift:207/214/217-218; LUTSidebar.swift:27/46). Spec's `rebindLUTIfNeeded` uses `library.luts.first(where:)` -> won't compile; must be `library.allLUTs`.
- COMPILE BREAK + DESIGN MISMATCH (verified) - the whole nav rewrite targets ImageCollection API that does not exist. Real ImageCollection has items:[Item], selectedIndex:Int, isActive:Bool, selectNext(), selectPrevious(), loadFromFolder, addFromData, clear, selectedItem. There is NO urls:[URL], NO setActiveURL, NO setURLs. Spec's goToImage/advanceImage (`collection.urls.firstIndex(of:)`), activate (`collection.setActiveURL`), and openFolder (`collection.setURLs(_:active:)`) all reference nonexistent members. Worse, the real VM ALREADY has selectCollectionImage(at:)/selectNextImage/selectPreviousImage (AppViewModel.swift:168-196) that drive selectedIndex and handle url-OR-data items; the spec reinvents this incorrectly and ignores the existing working wrappers.
- COMPILE BREAK (verified) - deleting `sourceImage` breaks the Views, contradicting the cleanup checklist. The spec claims only LUTSidebar+exportDialog read the deleted props; in fact PreviewView.swift:14 (`viewModel.sourceImage != nil`, the main content gate) and ContentView.swift:131 (`.disabled(viewModel.sourceImage == nil)`) both read sourceImage, and PreviewView reads selectedLUT at lines 15/54/72/91/94/106. A computed `var sourceImage: CIImage?`/`var hasSource: Bool` shim is mandatory or these views fail to compile; the checklist's impact analysis is wrong.
- WRONG SYMBOL (verified) - `ImageProcessor.openContentTypes` does not exist; the real member is `ImageProcessor.supportedTypes:[UTType]` (ImageProcessor.swift:36, used by openImageDialog:105). The cleanup checklist tells implementers to keep referencing a nonexistent symbol.
- FABRICATED PRE-EXISTING BUG (verified false) - the binding-doc open question and this spec's cross-ref about 'PreviewView.sideBySideView references an undefined preview / marked FIXME / would not compile' is FALSE. PreviewView.swift has no `preview` symbol and no FIXME; sideBySideView (lines 35-61) correctly uses originalPreviewNSImage and previewNSImage and compiles. Acting on this 'fix' would be chasing a non-bug.
- WRONG TEST INFRASTRUCTURE (verified) - this is a SwiftPM package (Package.swift, swift-tools-version 5.9), NOT an Xcode project (no .xcodeproj exists), and there is NO test target or LUTzyTests directory today. The testing notes prescribe paths under LUTzyTests/ and 'add SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY=complete to a test xcconfig' (test 16) and an Xcode-style test target. xcconfig/Xcode targets don't apply; strict-concurrency must be a swiftSettings .enableExperimentalFeature/.unsafeFlags entry in Package.swift, and a test target must be added to Package.swift first. The spec's test plumbing won't work as written.
- CONCURRENCY (verified hazard) - Task.detached self-capture reaching @MainActor. Real exportDialog uses `Task.detached { [processor, exportFormat] in ... self.isExporting = ...}` (AppViewModel.swift:297-309) and deriveRecipe `Task.detached { ... self.deriveProgress ...}` (343). Spec removes [processor] for export (good) but its `panel.begin { [weak self] in self.isExporting = true; let doc = self.document ... }` touches @MainActor self directly in a non-isolated completion handler -> Swift-6 isolation error. Needs MainActor.assumeIsolated / @MainActor closure / a @MainActor performExport(to:).
- STRICT-CONCURRENCY DEFECT - scheduleRender reads MainActor-isolated `self?.debounceInterval` synchronously inside a non-isolated `Task { [weak self] in try? await Task.sleep(for: self?.debounceInterval ...) }` before any hop. Cross-isolation read; won't compile under complete checking. renderOriginalPreview correctly capture-lists [maxPreview, workingSpace]; scheduleRender must capture debounceInterval into a local Duration first. Inconsistent.
- LOGIC BUG - `showOriginal { scheduleRender() }` variant is dead-on-arrival. scheduleRender opens with `if request == lastRenderedRequest { return }` and `request` is built from the FULL document, not the intensity-zeroed docToRender. Toggling A/B with no intervening edit => request==lastRenderedRequest => early return => original never renders. RenderRequestID must encode the actually-rendered doc / isShowingOriginal.
- LOGIC BUG - cached-swap A/B (recommended) races the debounce and the write-back guard has a hole. showOriginal-cached doesn't call scheduleRender, so an in-flight graded render fired just before Space-hold lands after the swap; the guard's `|| self.isShowingOriginal` clause then lets that stale graded image overwrite previewNSImage during Space-hold. Also the spec names 'invalidate gradedCache on every scheduleRender write-back' as required but never implements it, so post-edit Space-release shows a stale pre-edit graded image (spec's own risk #2).
- DATA CORRUPTION - global undo stack overwrites other images' edits. pushUndoSnapshot uses one global stack not keyed by image. Edit A (push A0), navigate to B, undo() -> setDocumentSilently(A0) while activeURL==urlB; didSet runs store.update(A0, for: urlB) AND renders A's doc against B's source, destroying B's saved edits. The open-question calls this 'surprising'; it is data loss. Key undo per-image.
- DESIGN MISMATCH - per-URL store cannot key data/Photos images. Item.url is URL? (nil for Photos; AppViewModel.openImage(data:) sets sourceURL=nil at line 126). EditDocumentStore keyed by URL no-ops on nil, so all data-imported images share one default doc with no per-image edits/undo. Better: key the store by ImageCollection.Item.id (UUID, ImageCollection.swift:9), eliminating the temp-file hack.
- TESTABILITY CONTRADICTION - VM hardcodes `RenderEngine.shared` in every sample, but 1/11 and tests 8-15 inject FakeRenderEngine via a `RenderEngining` protocol the VM never uses. As written tests can't run. VM must hold `private let engine: RenderEngining` (default .shared) and route all calls through it; the protocol must include makeSource + thumbnail, not just 4 methods.
- CROSS-COMPONENT GAP - RenderEngine.makeSource/thumbnail aren't in the owning component's surface. The binding architecture's actor RenderEngine defines register/unregister/renderPreview/export(/thumbnail) but NO makeSource. This component invents makeSource(url:) and depends on it for every open/navigate path; makeSource also needs the RAW/standard classifier, which is `ImageProcessor.rawExtensions` (private static, ImageProcessor.swift:19, used by loadImage:54-56). Reconcile the two specs and grant/move that classifier.
- DATA-PATH UNDERSPEC - openImage(data:) temp-file misroutes RAW. 9 writes bytes to a temp file with no specified extension, then relies on extension-based RAW/standard classification. A temp file without the original extension is always 'standard' and RAW bytes fail to decode as a plain CIImage; pasted/dropped RAW breaks. No cleanup policy. (Drag-drop of folders/files in PreviewView.handleDrop uses URLs so is unaffected, but Photos import is.)
- PERF/UX - debounce applied to navigation/open. activate()/goToImage funnel through scheduleRender, so every open and every filmstrip step eats the 80ms sleep atop async makeSource. Discrete nav should render immediately; only continuous slider drags need coalescing. No immediate path.
- REDUNDANT RENDER - activate() double-schedules: setDocumentSilently(incoming) fires didSet->scheduleRender(), then activate() calls scheduleRender() again (cancel+respawn, one wasted Task). Contradicts 'clean single render on switch'.
- INCONSISTENCY - intensity slider can't push undo-on-begin as specified. LUTSidebar today is a single List(selection:) with no detail/footer slot; the spec hand-waves 'add an intensity Slider bound to $viewModel.lutIntensity'. A plain `Slider(value:)` has no onEditingChanged, but 7 says continuous drags push undo once via onEditingChanged. Needs the Slider(value:in:onEditingChanged:) overload AND a concrete placement (no obvious home in the current sidebar layout).
- ARROW-KEY INSTRUCTION WRONG (verified) - 6 says repoint ContentView Left/Right (123/124) from collection.selectPrevious/Next to viewModel.selectPreviousImage/Next 'so the doc store swaps'. But in real ContentView keyCodes 123/124 map to selectPreviousLUT/selectNextLUT (lines 245-250); IMAGE nav is the `[`/`]` chars (lines 263-270) which ALREADY call vm.selectPreviousImage()/selectNextImage() routing through the existing wrappers. The instruction misidentifies both the keys and the current wiring.
- HARMLESS-BUT-WRONG - 10 adds `self.derivedLUT = result.lut` claiming rebindLUTIfNeeded uses it; rebindLUTIfNeeded only consults library.allLUTs/lutsByID. The derived LUT survives nav solely via lutsByID retention. Dead code; risk #6 ('silently clears the LUT') only fires if lutsByID is pruned (never). Clarify.
- STALE OPEN QUESTION (verified) - 'two sources of truth for what is an image (rawTypes vs isImageFile)' is outdated: there is NO isImageFile; ImageCollection.loadFromFolder already uses ImageProcessor.supportedExtensions (ImageCollection.swift:44), the single shared set. The only split is RAW-vs-standard branching via private rawExtensions inside loadImage, which makeSource must reuse - an access-scope task, not a duplication to consolidate.

**Open questions:**
- Undo granularity & scope: spec uses a GLOBAL last-write-wins undo stack (not per-image). Stepping images then ⌘Z undoes the most recent edit regardless of which image it belonged to, after switching the active document. Confirm this is acceptable for v1 or upgrade EditDocumentStore.undo to `[URL: [EditDocument]]` for per-image undo (small change, isolated to EditDocumentStore).
- Data-image open path: spec writes `openImage(data:name:)` payloads to a temp file so ImageSource stays URL-only. Confirm this is fine vs adding `ImageSource.Kind.data(Data)` (which would make ImageSource non-trivially Equatable/Sendable-with-Data and is heavier). Temp-file approach also needs a cleanup policy for the scratch file.
- 'Original' definition for A/B and side-by-side: spec defaults 'original' = source through RAW develop but with NO adjustments and NO LUT (i.e. develop is part of 'the negative'). Product must confirm vs 'original = neutral RAW defaults' (which would ignore the user's develop in the A/B comparison).
- Debounce interval (80ms) is a guess tuned for slider feel; needs validation on the largest expected RAW. Too low = stutter on 45MP+, too high = laggy feel. Consider an adaptive value or a draft-then-settle two-phase preview (cross-refs the render component's RAW scaleFactor/draft open question).
- Per-image store persistence: spec keeps `[URL: EditDocument]` in memory only (lost on quit). EditDocument is Codable specifically to enable on-disk persistence (sidecar files or app-state). Decide whether Phase 2 persists edits across launches or defers it.
- LUTID stability across sessions: spec derives LUTID from CubeLUT.id (a UUID). If LUTLibrary regenerates UUIDs on each scan, a persisted document's lutID won't resolve on relaunch (rebindLUTIfNeeded would clear the LUT). If persistence is wanted, LUTLibrary must give file-backed LUTs a stable id (e.g. hash of file path/contents) — coordinate with the LUTLibrary owner.
- ContentView KeyMonitor: Left/Right arrows must be repointed from `collection.selectPrevious/Next` to the new `viewModel.selectPreviousImage/selectNextImage` (so the doc store swaps). Confirm the KeyMonitor's pass-through rules don't already special-case these, and ensure ⌘Z/⌘⇧Z reach `undo()/redo()` (monitor passes Command-modified events through, but verify the menu has the items so the responder chain works).
- RenderEngine.makeSource ownership: this spec assumes the sibling render component provides `makeSource(url:) async -> ImageSource?` (measures native extent, decides RAW vs standard via ONE shared extension set). If it instead exposes extent measurement separately, the VM open paths need a small adapter. Confirm the API.

**Risks:**
- The whole component is blocked on the render-pipeline component delivering EditDocument/RAWDevelopSettings/AdjustmentNode/LUTSettings/LUTID/ImageSource/RenderScale/WorkingSpace/RenderEngine. Mitigation: code against a `RenderEngining` protocol (4 async methods) + a local stub of the value types behind `#if !RENDER_PIPELINE_READY`, so the VM and its tests compile and pass in isolation; delete the stub when the sibling lands.
- Behavioral change to A/B compare: if the cached-NSImage swap variant is used, `gradedCache` must be invalidated on every new preview render or Space-hold could show a stale graded image after an edit. The spec notes this; easy to get wrong.
- Equatable-gated didSet can SWALLOW a needed re-render if a mutation produces an equal document (e.g. setting intensity to its current value). That's intended (no-op), but if RAW develop availability flags change per image, ensure switching images always calls scheduleRender explicitly (activate() does).
- Deleting `processedImage` removes the only thing export captured; if any other code path (StatusBar, RecipeReportView, FilmstripView) read `processedImage` or `selectedLUT` as a stored property, it breaks. Grep confirmed only LUTSidebar (selectLUT calls) and exportDialog use them, and the computed `selectedLUT` preserves the read API — but re-grep after the sibling UI component adds an inspector.
- Derive decoupling regression: the single biggest correctness risk in the whole Phase 2 is accidentally threading `document.rawDevelop` into `deriveRecipe`. This component must pass ONLY URLs+options. Enforced by signature (deriveRecipe takes no EditDocument) + a doc-comment assertion; add a unit/compile test (test 15) that fails if the signature gains a develop param.
- Per-image store + LUTID resolution: navigating to an image whose saved document references a derived (in-memory, non-library) LUT that is no longer registered will silently clear the LUT (rebindLUTIfNeeded). Acceptable for in-memory v1, but surprising if persistence is added later without LUT persistence.
- Off-actor render + staleness guard correctness: the RenderRequestID compare on write-back must use the LIVE `self.document`, not a captured copy, or rapid edits could write an older image last. Spec captures `request` (the intended target) and compares to live state; reviewers must verify the comparison direction during code review.
- ImageProcessor is only partially dissolved by THIS component (it stops calling instance render methods) but the singleton + CIContext still exist until the render component finishes moving GPU duties. During the transition, both ImageProcessor.context and RenderEngine.context could exist; ensure FilmstripView.generateThumbnail is migrated in lockstep or two CIContexts briefly coexist (functional but wasteful).

---

### Concurrency / Sendable hardening

**Verdict:** minor-issues (concurrency FLAG · color OK · preview/export OK)

**Summary.** Make the render path Swift-6 strict-concurrency clean by (1) replacing the non-Sendable `ImageProcessor.shared` singleton's GPU duties with an `actor RenderEngine` that solely owns the one Metal `CIContext`, so `CIImage`/`CIFilter`/`CIContext` never cross an isolation boundary; (2) moving every value that DOES cross the boundary to a `Sendable` value type (`EditDocument`, `RAWDevelopSettings`, `AdjustmentNode`, `LUTSettings`, `LUTID`, `ImageSource`, `RenderScale`, `WorkingSpace`); (3) eliminating the three illegal captures — the `Task.detached { [processor, exportFormat] ... self ... imageToExport(CIImage) }` in `exportDialog` (AppViewModel.swift:297), the `Task.detached { ... self ... }` + progress closure in `deriveRecipe` (AppViewModel.swift:343), and the main-actor-inheriting `previewTask = Task { lut.apply(...) }` in `applyLUT` (AppViewModel.swift:236) — by calling `await RenderEngine.shared.{renderPreview,export,thumbnail}(...)` with only Sendable args/returns (`CGImage?`/`Void`); (4) keeping `RecipeExtractor` physically decoupled (its own `CIContext`, value inputs, never touches the actor) so derive stays a clean detached job and the neutral baseline cannot inherit user develop. Net: zero `@unchecked Sendable` escape hatches; the singleton's GPU role is dissolved; `AppViewModel` stays `@MainActor` and only ever holds Sendable state plus the `CGImage`-wrapped `NSImage` previews. IMPORTANT CORRECTION vs the brief/maps: `ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral(at:)` is ALREADY a `static func` (ImageProcessor.swift:74) shared by both loadRAW and RecipeExtractor — there is NO `neutralRAWBaseline` duplicate to reconcile; preserve this static as the single neutral-baseline definition.

Ideally delete the file once empty and rehome the enums; minimally make the residue Sendable.
