**New types:** enum WorkingSpace: String, Codable, Sendable, Equatable { case sRGB, displayP3; var cgColorSpace: CGColorSpace; static let current }, enum ExportFormat: String, Codable, Sendable, CaseIterable { case tiff, jpeg, png; var utType: UTType; var fileExtension: String; var defaultBitDepth: ExportBitDepth; var supportsQuality: Bool } (MOVED out of ImageProcessor), enum ExportBitDepth: Int, Codable, Sendable { case eight = 8, sixteen = 16 }, struct ExportRequest: Sendable, Equatable { var format: ExportFormat; var bitDepth: ExportBitDepth; var quality: CGFloat; var metadataPolicy: MetadataPolicy; var space: WorkingSpace }, enum MetadataPolicy: String, Codable, Sendable { case preserve, strip } (preserve = copy EXIF/TIFF/IPTC/GPS/orientation from source + embed ICC; strip = ICC only), struct ExportMetadata: Sendable { let properties: [CFString: Any]-equivalent captured as a Sendable snapshot; static func read(from url: URL) -> ExportMetadata?; func representationOptions(for: ExportFormat, quality: CGFloat, iccData: Data?) -> [CIImageRepresentationOption: Any] }, enum ImageError: Error { case cannotLoad, processingFailed, exportFailed } (UNCHANGED; relocated if ImageProcessor is dissolved)

**Implementation:**

## Component: Export + color-management seam — implementation spec

This component owns four things: (1) export renders from the **node graph at full res**, not the cached preview blob; (2) the **WorkingSpace -> output-profile seam** (future-proof for Display P3); (3) **EXIF/ICC preservation** on export; (4) keep **TIFF16 / JPEG / PNG**. It conforms exactly to the binding decision's `RenderEngine.export` signature and `WorkingSpace` enum, and fills in the metadata-preservation layer the binding decision left as an open question.

Ground-truth current sites (verified line cites from subsystem maps):
- `ImageProcessor.export(_:to:format:quality:)` — ImageProcessor.swift ~157-197; builds `let colorSpace = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!` at **:163**; three encoders at tiff/jpeg/png; `data.write(to:url)`. Default `quality: CGFloat = 0.95`.
- `AppViewModel.exportDialog()` — AppViewModel.swift ~277-311; `imageToExport = processedImage ?? sourceImage!` at **:283** (reuses the baked single-shot LUT image, never re-applies); `Task.detached { [processor, exportFormat] ... processor.export(...) }` at **:297-299**; **never passes quality** (so effective quality is always 0.95).
- `CubeLUT.makeFilter()` — CubeLUT.swift ~131; `inputColorSpace = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!` at **:135**; fresh filter built per `apply` at **:141** (no cache).
- `RecipeExtractor.derive` — own CIContext (~:419) + own `let sRGB = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!` at **:81**; renders sample buffers RGBA8 in that sRGB. **Stays decoupled.**
- Latent 4th site: `renderPreview` `createCGImage` passes **no** colorSpace (ImageProcessor.swift **:92**) -> preview/export color mismatch.

---

### 1. `WorkingSpace.swift` (NEW) — the single seam

```swift
import CoreGraphics

/// THE single source of truth for color space. Collapses the 3 hardcoded
/// `CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!` literals (CubeLUT.swift:135,
/// ImageProcessor.swift:163) AND the implicit preview-raster site
/// (ImageProcessor.swift:92) into one value. Default .sRGB; .displayP3 reserved.
enum WorkingSpace: String, Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    case sRGB
    case displayP3

    var cgColorSpace: CGColorSpace {
        switch self {
        case .sRGB:      return CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!       // guaranteed-resolvable system name
        case .displayP3: return CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.displayP3)!  // guaranteed-resolvable system name
        }
    }

    /// ONE line to flip the whole app to P3 later (gated on the 8-bit-gamut open question).
    static let current: WorkingSpace = .sRGB
}
```

Notes:
- This is the **only** place `CGColorSpace(name:)!` is force-unwrapped after this change; both names are guaranteed-resolvable Apple constants, so the force-unwrap is safe and localized (retires the two unsafe force-unwraps the maps flag at ImageProcessor.swift:163 and RecipeExtractor.swift:81 — the latter only by documentation, since derive stays sRGB).
- `WorkingSpace` is **NOT** stored in `EditDocument` (binding decision): it is output/app config, not "the look". `RenderEngine.export`/`renderPreview` take it as a param sourced from `WorkingSpace.current`.

---

### 2. `ExportRequest.swift` (NEW) — Sendable export descriptor + relocated `ExportFormat`

Move `ExportFormat` out of `ImageProcessor` (it is currently an inner enum, ImageProcessor.swift:132) so it survives the dissolution of `ImageProcessor` and is plainly `Sendable`.

```swift
import UniformTypeIdentifiers
import CoreGraphics

enum ExportFormat: String, Codable, Sendable, CaseIterable {
    case tiff = "TIFF"
    case jpeg = "JPEG"
    case png  = "PNG"

    var utType: UTType {                 // used by NSSavePanel.allowedContentTypes
        switch self { case .tiff: .tiff; case .jpeg: .jpeg; case .png: .png }
    }
    var fileExtension: String {          // used for default filename
        switch self { case .tiff: "tif"; case .jpeg: "jpg"; case .png: "png" }
    }
    var defaultBitDepth: ExportBitDepth {            // preserves today's per-container coupling
        switch self { case .tiff: .sixteen; case .jpeg, .png: .eight }
    }
    var supportsQuality: Bool { self == .jpeg }      // only JPEG honors lossy quality
}

enum ExportBitDepth: Int, Codable, Sendable { case eight = 8, sixteen = 16 }

/// Controls EXIF/ICC carry-through. `.preserve` copies source metadata + embeds the
/// working-space ICC; `.strip` embeds ICC only (color tag) and writes no EXIF/GPS/etc.
enum MetadataPolicy: String, Codable, Sendable { case preserve, strip }

/// Everything the export needs, all Sendable (clean Task/actor boundary for Swift 6).
struct ExportRequest: Sendable, Equatable {
    var format: ExportFormat
    var bitDepth: ExportBitDepth
    var quality: CGFloat               // only used when format.supportsQuality
    var metadataPolicy: MetadataPolicy
    var space: WorkingSpace

    init(format: ExportFormat,
         bitDepth: ExportBitDepth? = nil,
         quality: CGFloat = 0.95,
         metadataPolicy: MetadataPolicy = .preserve,
         space: WorkingSpace = .current) {
        self.format = format
        self.bitDepth = bitDepth ?? format.defaultBitDepth
        self.quality = quality
        self.metadataPolicy = metadataPolicy
        self.space = space
    }
}
```

Why a descriptor: closes the JPEG-quality wiring gap (today `exportDialog` never threads quality, so it is fixed at 0.95) and decouples bit depth from container so a future "8-bit TIFF" or "16-bit PNG" is a one-field change. `Equatable`/`Sendable` keep the export call site trivially race-free.

`CFString` map note: `CGImageSource` properties are `[CFString: Any]` which is not `Sendable`. The `ExportMetadata` snapshot (below) captures them into a `nonisolated(unsafe)`-free Sendable wrapper by re-keying to `String` and storing values as `Sendable`-safe types (numbers/strings/nested `[String: any Sendable]`), OR simpler: do all metadata work *inside the actor* and pass only the source `URL` across the boundary (preferred — see §4 step B). The `ExportMetadata` type then needs no Sendable contortions because it is created and consumed entirely inside `RenderEngine`.

---

### 3. `ExportMetadata.swift` (NEW) — read source metadata, build encoder options

Core Image's `tiff/jpeg/pngRepresentation(of:format:colorSpace:options:)` accept a `[CIImageRepresentationOption: Any]` dict. That dict is the **only** injection point for both metadata and an explicit ICC profile (the maps confirm export uses the CIContext Data encoders, not `CGImageDestination`, so there is no separate ICC API). Keys:
- `CIImageRepresentationOption(rawValue: kCGImageDestinationLossyCompressionQuality as String)` — JPEG quality (already used today).
- Per-property metadata is injected via the typed CI options whose raw values are the `kCGImageProperty*` dictionary keys, e.g. `kCGImagePropertyExifDictionary`, `kCGImagePropertyTIFFDictionary`, `kCGImagePropertyGPSDictionary`, `kCGImagePropertyIPTCDictionary`, and top-level `kCGImagePropertyOrientation`.
- ICC: pass the working-space ICC data under `CIImageRepresentationOption(rawValue: kCGImagePropertyTIFFDictionary ... )`? No — the **colorSpace:** argument already tags the file. For an *explicit embedded ICC profile* (belt-and-suspenders for catalogers once non-sRGB ships) also set the destination ICC via the options key `kCGImageDestinationEmbedThumbnail`? No. Correct mechanism: the encoder embeds the ICC of the `colorSpace:` you pass; to override/force-embed arbitrary profile data, include `kCGImagePropertyProfileName`/raw ICC is not exposed through CI options reliably, so **v1 rule: ICC = whatever `space.cgColorSpace` carries**, which is correct and sufficient. (Open question retained: explicit arbitrary-ICC embedding would require switching to `CGImageDestination`.)

```swift
import ImageIO
import CoreImage
import CoreGraphics

/// Reads metadata from the SOURCE file and produces CIImageRepresentationOption dicts.
/// Created & consumed entirely inside RenderEngine, so it needs no Sendable gymnastics.
struct ExportMetadata {
    /// Raw source properties as returned by CGImageSourceCopyPropertiesAtIndex.
    private let sourceProps: [CFString: Any]
    /// EXIF orientation read from the source (1...8); default 1 if absent.
    let orientation: Int

    static func read(from url: URL) -> ExportMetadata? {
        guard let src = CGImageSourceCreateWithURL(url as CFURL, nil),
              let props = CGImageSourceCopyPropertiesAtIndex(src, 0, nil) as? [CFString: Any]
        else { return nil }
        let o = (props[kCGImagePropertyOrientation] as? Int) ?? 1
        return ExportMetadata(sourceProps: props, orientation: o)
    }

    /// Build the options dict for a given format. `policy == .strip` returns only
    /// the quality option (for JPEG); `.preserve` adds EXIF/TIFF/GPS/IPTC + orientation.
    func representationOptions(for format: ExportFormat,
                               quality: CGFloat,
                               policy: MetadataPolicy) -> [CIImageRepresentationOption: Any] {
        var opts: [CIImageRepresentationOption: Any] = [:]

        if format.supportsQuality {
            opts[CIImageRepresentationOption(rawValue: kCGImageDestinationLossyCompressionQuality as String)] = quality
        }
        guard policy == .preserve else { return opts }

        // Copy through the standard metadata dictionaries when present.
        func copy(_ key: CFString) {
            if let d = sourceProps[key] { opts[CIImageRepresentationOption(rawValue: key as String)] = d }
        }
        copy(kCGImagePropertyExifDictionary)
        copy(kCGImagePropertyTIFFDictionary)
        copy(kCGImagePropertyGPSDictionary)
        copy(kCGImagePropertyIPTCDictionary)
        copy(kCGImagePropertyExifAuxDictionary)

        // IMPORTANT ORIENTATION RULE (see Risks): CIImage from a RAW/standard load has
        // ALREADY had EXIF orientation baked into pixels by the decoder (CIRAWFilter applies
        // its `orientation`; CIImage(contentsOf:) + applyOrientationProperty does likewise).
        // Therefore the exported pixels are upright == orientation 1. We must NOT copy a
        // non-1 orientation into the output or viewers will double-rotate. Force 1:
        opts[CIImageRepresentationOption(rawValue: kCGImagePropertyOrientation as String)] = 1

        return opts
    }
}
```

Orientation correctness is the subtle part and is called out as a risk below: today nothing is written (so files are untagged/orientation-naive); once we *do* write metadata, copying the source's orientation tag would be wrong because the pixels are already upright. We pin orientation to 1 on the upright buffer.

---

### 4. `RenderEngine.export(...)` — the new export body (this component owns this method)

The actor type is defined by the render-engine component; **this component defines the `export` method signature and body.** It replaces both `ImageProcessor.export` (the encode) and the `imageToExport = processedImage ?? sourceImage!` reuse in `exportDialog` (the source-of-pixels). Export now **renders the same `EditDocument` node graph at full res** via the shared `RenderPipeline.buildImage(...)`.

```swift
extension RenderEngine {
    /// SAME node definition as preview, evaluated at FULL resolution, encoded with an
    /// EXPLICIT working-space color space + preserved metadata.
    /// All inputs are Sendable values; CIImage/CIContext never escape the actor.
    func export(source: ImageSource,
                document: EditDocument,
                request: ExportRequest,
                to url: URL) throws {
        // A. RENDER FROM THE GRAPH AT FULL RES (parity with preview by construction).
        let lut = document.lut.lutID.flatMap { lutRegistry[$0] }     // resolve LUT by stable ID
        guard let image = RenderPipeline.buildImage(source: source,
                                                    document: document,
                                                    lut: lut,
                                                    scale: .full,            // <-- not .preview
                                                    space: request.space)
        else { throw ImageError.processingFailed }

        // B. READ SOURCE METADATA (inside the actor -> no Sendable boundary issues).
        let meta = ExportMetadata.read(from: source.url)
        let cs = request.space.cgColorSpace
        let opts = meta?.representationOptions(for: request.format,
                                              quality: request.quality,
                                              policy: request.metadataPolicy)
                   ?? fallbackOptions(for: request)   // quality-only if metadata unreadable

        // C. ENCODE — TIFF16 / JPEG / PNG preserved; bit depth from request; ONE color space.
        let data: Data?
        switch request.format {
        case .tiff:
            let fmt: CIFormat = (request.bitDepth == .sixteen) ? .RGBA16 : .RGBA8
            data = context.tiffRepresentation(of: image, format: fmt, colorSpace: cs, options: opts)
        case .png:
            let fmt: CIFormat = (request.bitDepth == .sixteen) ? .RGBA16 : .RGBA8
            data = context.pngRepresentation(of: image, format: fmt, colorSpace: cs, options: opts)
        case .jpeg:
            // jpegRepresentation has no `format:` (always 8-bit); quality+metadata via opts.
            data = context.jpegRepresentation(of: image, colorSpace: cs, options: opts)
        }

        guard let d = data else { throw ImageError.exportFailed }
        try d.write(to: url)
    }

    private func fallbackOptions(for request: ExportRequest) -> [CIImageRepresentationOption: Any] {
        guard request.format.supportsQuality else { return [:] }
        return [CIImageRepresentationOption(rawValue: kCGImageDestinationLossyCompressionQuality as String): request.quality]
    }
}
```

Before/after of the encode core:

```swift
// BEFORE — ImageProcessor.swift ~157-197
func export(_ ciImage: CIImage, to url: URL, format: ExportFormat, quality: CGFloat = 0.95) throws {
    let colorSpace = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!            // :163  hardcoded
    let data: Data?
    switch format {
    case .tiff: data = context.tiffRepresentation(of: ciImage, format: .RGBA16, colorSpace: colorSpace)        // no options
    case .jpeg: data = context.jpegRepresentation(of: ciImage, colorSpace: colorSpace,
                    options: [kCIImageRepresentationOption...quality])                                         // quality only
    case .png:  data = context.pngRepresentation(of: ciImage, format: .RGBA8, colorSpace: colorSpace)          // no options
    }
    guard let d = data else { throw ImageError.exportFailed }
    try d.write(to: url)
}

// AFTER — see RenderEngine.export above:
//   * pixels come from RenderPipeline.buildImage(scale: .full) not a passed-in baked CIImage
//   * colorSpace = request.space.cgColorSpace   (single seam, matches LUT interp + preview raster)
//   * every encoder gets `options: opts` carrying preserved EXIF/TIFF/GPS/IPTC (+ forced orientation 1)
//   * bit depth from request (TIFF/PNG can be 8 or 16); JPEG quality actually threaded
```

---

### 5. `CubeLUT.makeFilter` — accept the seam, cache the filter

This is the LUT *interpolation* space; the binding invariant is that it MUST equal the export *output* space. Threading the same `WorkingSpace` into both makes them move in lockstep (you cannot change one without the other).

```swift
// BEFORE — CubeLUT.swift ~131-145
func makeFilter() -> CIFilter {
    let f = CIFilter(name: "CIColorCubeWithColorSpace")!
    f.setValue(size, forKey: "inputCubeDimension")
    f.setValue(tableData, forKey: "inputCubeData")
    f.setValue(CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!, forKey: "inputColorSpace")   // :135 hardcoded
    return f
}

// AFTER
private var filterCache: [String: CIFilter] = [:]   // keyed by colorSpace.name; invalidates free on P3 switch
func makeFilter(colorSpace: CGColorSpace) -> CIFilter {
    let key = (colorSpace.name as String?) ?? "unknown"
    if let cached = filterCache[key] { return cached }
    let f = CIFilter(name: "CIColorCubeWithColorSpace")!
    f.setValue(size, forKey: "inputCubeDimension")
    f.setValue(tableData, forKey: "inputCubeData")
    f.setValue(colorSpace, forKey: "inputColorSpace")
    filterCache[key] = f
    return f
}
```

Caching note: `CubeLUT` is a value type today; a stored mutable cache dict makes mutation-on-read awkward. Two acceptable resolutions (pick per render-engine component): (a) make the cache a `final class` box held by the struct (`private let cache = FilterBox()`), so `makeFilter` stays non-mutating and the box lives behind the actor; or (b) keep `makeFilter` pure (no cache) and have `RenderEngine` own a `[LUTID: [String: CIFilter]]` cache. **Recommended: (b)** — keeps `CubeLUT` a clean Sendable value and confines all CIFilter (non-Sendable) lifetime to the actor. Under (b), `makeFilter(colorSpace:)` is pure and `RenderEngine` memoizes. Either way the per-apply realloc at CubeLUT.swift:141 is fixed.

---

### 6. `AppViewModel.exportDialog()` — build the request, call the actor

```swift
// BEFORE — AppViewModel.swift ~277-311 (essentials)
func exportDialog() {
    guard sourceImage != nil else { return }
    let imageToExport = processedImage ?? sourceImage!          // :283  reuse baked LUT image
    // ... NSSavePanel, allowedContentTypes = [exportFormat.utType] ...
    isExporting = true
    Task.detached { [processor, exportFormat] in                // :297  captures non-Sendable singleton
        try processor.export(imageToExport, to: url, format: exportFormat)   // quality never passed
        await MainActor.run { self.isExporting = false }
    }
}

// AFTER
func exportDialog() {
    guard let source = currentSource else { return }            // ImageSource (URL+kind+nativeExtent)
    let panel = NSSavePanel()
    panel.allowedContentTypes = [exportFormat.utType]
    panel.nameFieldStringValue = defaultExportFilename()        // basename + LUT suffix + ext (unchanged logic)
    guard panel.runModal() == .OK, let url = panel.url else { return }

    let request = ExportRequest(format: exportFormat,
                                quality: exportQuality,          // NEW @Published, default 0.95; UI optional
                                metadataPolicy: .preserve,
                                space: .current)
    let doc = document                                           // Sendable EditDocument snapshot
    isExporting = true
    Task {                                                       // no detached needed; actor hop is the boundary
        defer { Task { @MainActor in self.isExporting = false } }
        do {
            try await RenderEngine.shared.export(source: source, document: doc, request: request, to: url)
        } catch {
            await MainActor.run { self.statusMessage = "Export failed: \(error.localizedDescription)" }
        }
    }
}
```

Key changes: no `processedImage` reuse (export now re-renders the **same graph** at full res, so it honors RAW develop + ordered adjustments + LUT intensity); no non-Sendable capture (only the `ImageSource`/`EditDocument`/`ExportRequest` values + `url` cross into the `Task`, and `RenderEngine.shared` is an actor); JPEG quality is actually threaded. `currentSource`, `document`, `exportQuality` are produced by sibling components (state model + RAW-develop); this component only requires their existence and shapes.

---

### 7. RecipeExtractor — documentation-only checkpoint (no behavior change)

Per the binding decision, derive stays physically decoupled: it keeps its own CIContext (~:419) and its own `let sRGB` (:81), and is **NOT** routed through `WorkingSpace`. The only change is a doc comment + optional debug assert documenting *why*:

```swift
// RecipeExtractor.swift near :81
// NOTE: Deliberately sRGB, NOT WorkingSpace.current. A derived .cube encodes a transform
// FROM this sample space TO the JPEG space, and is later APPLIED by CubeLUT.makeFilter in
// the SAME space. v1 keeps both sRGB so fit-space == apply-space trivially. If the app ever
// flips WorkingSpace.current to .displayP3, derive must be revisited IN LOCKSTEP (stamp the
// build space onto the CubeLUT), NOT blindly switched here — the neutral RAW baseline it fits
// against is itself an sRGB-default render. See open question "Display P3 + derive build-space stamp".
let sRGB = CGColorSpace(name: CGColorSpace.sRGB)!
```

This is the fourth color literal and is intentionally left out of the seam; the comment is the tracked checkpoint so a P3 migration cannot silently desync derive.

---

### Color-space invariant summary (what the seam guarantees)

| Site | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| LUT interpolation (CubeLUT.swift:135) | `sRGB` literal | `request.space.cgColorSpace` via `makeFilter(colorSpace:)` |
| Export encode (ImageProcessor.swift:163) | `sRGB` literal | `request.space.cgColorSpace` (all 3 encoders) |
| Preview raster (ImageProcessor.swift:92, implicit) | no colorSpace (CIContext default) | `space.cgColorSpace` in `renderPreview.createCGImage` (render-engine component; must match) |
| Derive sampling (RecipeExtractor.swift:81) | `sRGB` literal | **stays** `sRGB` (decoupled, documented) |

LUT-interp space (#1) and output space (#2) now read the **same** `WorkingSpace` value threaded through `buildImage(...space:)` / `export(request:)`, so a P3 switch updates both atomically — enforced structurally, not by discipline.

**Testing:** UNIT / LOGIC:
1. WorkingSpace.cgColorSpace: assert `.sRGB.cgColorSpace.name == CGColorSpace.sRGB` and `.displayP3.cgColorSpace.name == CGColorSpace.displayP3` (both non-nil; proves the force-unwraps are safe).
2. ExportRequest defaults: `ExportRequest(format:.tiff).bitDepth == .sixteen`; `.jpeg/.png -> .eight`; `format.supportsQuality` true only for .jpeg.
3. ExportMetadata.representationOptions: with policy .strip and format .jpeg -> dict has ONLY the lossy-quality key; with .png -> empty. With policy .preserve and a fixture JPEG that has EXIF -> dict contains kCGImagePropertyExifDictionary AND forces kCGImagePropertyOrientation == 1.

ROUND-TRIP / INTEGRATION (the load-bearing ones):
4. PREVIEW/EXPORT PARITY (correctness requirement): render a fixed (source, EditDocument incl. a LUT at intensity 0.5 + one adjustment) through RenderEngine.renderPreview at a SMALL maxSize and through export(.png, 16-bit) at full res. Downscale the full-res PNG to the preview size with the same Lanczos and compare: mean per-channel abs diff must be < ~2/255 (allow RAW draft-demosaic delta if source is RAW; use a standard JPG source for the strict check). This proves both paths fold the SAME graph in the SAME space. This test FAILS today because export reuses processedImage and preview omits colorSpace.
5. COLOR-SPACE LOCKSTEP: temporarily set WorkingSpace.current = .displayP3, export a known sRGB-primary test chart to TIFF16, read back the embedded profile via CGImageSourceCopyPropertiesAtIndex -> kCGImagePropertyProfileName indicates Display P3; AND the on-disk values differ from the sRGB export (proves both LUT-interp and encode moved together, not just one). Revert to .sRGB after.
6. METADATA PRESERVATION: export a JPEG fixture that carries EXIF (Make/Model/DateTimeOriginal) + GPS with policy .preserve; reopen the OUTPUT with CGImageSource and assert those EXIF/GPS keys survived. With policy .strip assert they are absent. Today this test would show TOTAL loss (no options dict written).
7. ORIENTATION DOUBLE-ROTATE GUARD: use a source whose EXIF orientation != 1 (e.g. 6 = 90 CW). Export with .preserve. Open the output and assert (a) pixel width/height match the UPRIGHT orientation and (b) the written kCGImagePropertyOrientation == 1. Visually confirm the exported image is NOT sideways (regression guard for the orientation rule in ExportMetadata).
8. JPEG QUALITY THREADING: export the same image at quality 0.3 vs 0.95; assert file size(0.3) << size(0.95). Today both would be 0.95 (exportDialog never passes quality), so this proves the wiring gap is closed.
9. BIT DEPTH: export .tiff with bitDepth .sixteen vs .eight; assert the output's kCGImagePropertyDepth / bits-per-component is 16 vs 8 respectively. PNG same. JPEG ignores depth (always 8) — assert no crash and 8-bit output.

DERIVE DECOUPLING (regression):
10. Derive a LUT from a fixed (RAW, JPG) pair, snapshot the resulting .cube bytes. Then change a user RAW-develop setting (e.g. document.rawDevelop.exposure = 1.0) and an adjustment, re-run derive on the SAME pair, and assert the .cube bytes are IDENTICAL — proves derive never reads user develop/WorkingSpace. (Strongest guard for the hard constraint.)
11. With WorkingSpace.current temporarily .displayP3, re-run #10: the derived .cube must STILL be byte-identical to the sRGB-current run (derive sampling stays sRGB regardless of app working space).

CONCURRENCY (Swift 6 readiness):
12. Build the target with `-strict-concurrency=complete` (or set SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY=complete in the build settings) and confirm exportDialog's call site + RenderEngine.export compile with NO Sendable/data-race diagnostics — i.e. only ExportRequest/ImageSource/EditDocument/URL cross the actor boundary and no CIImage/CIContext/ImageProcessor escapes. This is the acceptance gate for the concurrency portion.

MANUAL SMOKE: open a RAW, apply a LUT at 60% + exposure +0.5, export each of TIFF/JPEG/PNG; open all three in Preview.app and an EXIF viewer (e.g. `exiftool`): confirm (a) colors match the on-screen preview, (b) EXIF Make/Model/DateTime present, (c) orientation correct, (d) ICC profile tag = sRGB. Repeat with a standard JPG source.

**Reviewer-flagged issues:**
- METADATA-INJECTION MECHANISM IS UNSUBSTANTIATED BY THE macOS SDK (blocking). The spec's core feature is copying kCGImagePropertyExif/TIFF/GPS/IPTCDictionary into a [CIImageRepresentationOption: Any] dict and passing it to context.tiff/jpeg/pngRepresentation. I grepped the actual CIContext.h: the CIImageRepresentationOption NS_TYPED_ENUM declares ONLY specialized keys (kCIImageRepresentationAVDepthData, DepthImage, DisparityImage, *PortraitEffectsMatte, *SemanticSegmentation*Matte*, HDRImage/HDRGainMap*). There is NO kCIImageRepresentationProperties / EXIF / GPS / metadata key documented, and nothing states the encoders read arbitrary kCGImageProperty* dictionaries from the options dict. So EXIF/GPS/IPTC preservation via this path is very likely SILENTLY IGNORED (no error, zero metadata written) -- exactly the silent-failure the spec lists as a risk, now confirmed as probable against the SDK. To preserve metadata reliably the encode must move to CGImageDestination (createCGImage -> CGImageDestinationAddImage with the kCGImageProperty* dict / CGImageMetadata), which the spec only contemplated as the *arbitrary-ICC* fallback. As written, the metadata layer (ExportMetadata.representationOptions + the copy(kCGImageProperty*Dictionary) calls) will not function.
- ORIENTATION DOUBLE-ROTATION BUG CONFIRMED FOR THE STANDARD/PHOTOS PATHS (high impact). Spec section 3 force-writes kCGImagePropertyOrientation=1 assuming the decoder baked EXIF orientation into pixels on BOTH paths. The real loads are bare `CIImage(contentsOf: url)` (ImageProcessor.swift:59) and `CIImage(data:)` (AppViewModel.swift:122) with NO options. I confirmed in CIImage.h that orientation baking requires the explicit kCIImageApplyOrientationProperty option ('If this option value is @YES, the image will [be] transformed ... and the orientation metadata removed' -- i.e. it is NOT applied otherwise). So for a rotated JPEG/HEIC the exported pixels are NOT upright, and pinning orientation=1 yields a sideways/mis-rotated file. The spec's section-3 premise is false for the standard path. Fix: bake orientation at load (CIImage(contentsOf:options:[.applyOrientationProperty:true]) and the Data variant), THEN force 1; or branch the forced value on whether the path baked. (RAW via CIRAWFilter does apply its own orientation, so only the non-RAW paths are wrong -- but those are the JPEG/HEIC the metadata feature most targets.)
- CIContext Data encoders DO NOT accept an orientation argument, so even the 'force orientation=1' can only travel through the (unverified) properties dict. Confirmed in CIContext.h: only createCGImage:...:orientation: (lines ~913-939) takes a CGImagePropertyOrientation; tiff/jpeg/pngRepresentationOfImage:...:options: take only the options dict. This compounds issue #1: if the options dict doesn't carry kCGImageProperty* through, there is no other channel to set output orientation on the Representation path. Another reason the encode likely must become CGImageDestination-based.
- ExportFormat relocation drops `Identifiable` -> breaks the toolbar. Real enum: `enum ExportFormat: String, CaseIterable, Identifiable { var id: String { rawValue } ... }` (ImageProcessor.swift:149). ContentView.swift:79 does `ForEach(ImageProcessor.ExportFormat.allCases) { fmt in Text(fmt.rawValue).tag(fmt) }` -- requires Identifiable + the 'TIFF'/'JPEG'/'PNG' raw values for display. The spec's new decl is only `String, Codable, Sendable, CaseIterable` (no Identifiable/id) and changes case raw values to lowercase via `case tiff = "TIFF"`? (it keeps display strings, ok) but DROPS Identifiable. Moving it as written fails to compile at ContentView. Also every `ImageProcessor.ExportFormat` reference (ContentView.swift:79, AppViewModel.swift:29) must be repointed to the new top-level type.
- makeFilter optionality mismatch. Real `CubeLUT.makeFilter()` returns `CIFilter?` and guards `CIFilter(name: "CIColorCubeWithColorSpace")` (CubeLUT.swift:131-137). Spec section 5 declares `func makeFilter(colorSpace:) -> CIFilter` (non-optional) and applyWithIntensity/RenderPipeline consume it as non-optional. A faithful port keeps the optional (the guard is correct defensive code); the spec's downstream non-optional usage then won't compile. Either keep `-> CIFilter?` and handle nil, or document the force-unwrap.
- Stale line citations (file changed after spec authored; derive-decoupling already landed). Verified real positions: ImageProcessor.export 174-214 (spec 157-197); hardcoded sRGB in export :180 (spec :163); ExportFormat enum :149 (spec :132); preview createCGImage WITHOUT colorSpace :109 (spec :92); jpeg quality-only options already present :198. CubeLUT :135/:141 correct. RecipeExtractor sRGB :81 correct, its own context makeContext() at :421 (spec ~419). Note developRAWNeutral extraction + supportedExtensions consolidation are ALREADY in the working tree, so the RecipeExtractor change really is doc-comment-only as the spec says (good) -- but implementers must edit by symbol, not the spec's line numbers.
- LUTID registry glue is bigger than 'one wiring task'. CubeLUT is keyed by String id (file path or synthetic `derived://name/UUID`, CubeLUT.swift:35), not UUID; there is no registry. The export body's `document.lut.lutID.flatMap { lutRegistry[$0] }` presumes a LUTID type + a populated [LUTID:CubeLUT] map that nothing currently builds, and deriveRecipe today calls selectLUT(lut) directly (AppViewModel.swift:387). Introducing LUTID, registering file-backed AND derived LUTs, and replacing selectLUT is real work owned by sibling components but silently required by this export method.
- exportDialog rewrite: `defer { Task { @MainActor in self.isExporting = false } }` inside an outer Task is an ordering/efficiency smell vs the existing inline `await MainActor.run { self.isExporting=false; self.statusMessage=... }` (AppViewModel.swift:300-308). The defer schedules a SEPARATE main-actor hop that can run out of order with the catch's status update and spawns an extra Task each export. Reset isExporting in the same continuation as the success/failure status.

**Open questions:**
- Explicit arbitrary-ICC embedding: the CIContext Data encoders embed the ICC of the `colorSpace:` argument only. Embedding a *custom* ICC profile (e.g. a camera-matched or non-system profile) is not reliably exposed through CIImageRepresentationOption — it would require switching the export encode to CGImageDestination (createCGImage + CGImageDestinationAddImage + kCGImageDestination* + a CGImageMetadata). v1 ships color tag = WorkingSpace's system profile (correct + sufficient). Decide if custom-ICC is in Phase 2 scope; if yes, the encode core changes from CIContext.*Representation to CGImageDestination and §4 must be revised.
- CIContext working space (kCIContextWorkingColorSpace / kCIContextWorkingFormat): the binding decision deliberately does NOT set these. If a later decision sets extendedLinearSRGB + RGBAh for higher-precision intermediates, the 8-bit JPEG/PNG export path needs an explicit soft-clip/tone-map before encode or out-of-gamut values produce hue shifts/banding that the TIFF16 path hides. This sits at the RenderEngine.init boundary, but its consequence (clamp before 8-bit encode) lands in THIS component's encode switch. Resolve before enabling.
- Display P3 enablement + derive build-space stamp: flipping WorkingSpace.current to .displayP3 moves LUT-interp and export in lockstep automatically, but derived LUTs are fit in sRGB. Before P3 ships, a derived CubeLUT must record the space it was built in so makeFilter applies it in the matching space (or derive must re-fit in P3). The RecipeExtractor doc comment is the tracked checkpoint; the concrete follow-up is a `buildSpace: WorkingSpace` field on CubeLUT consulted by makeFilter.
- MetadataPolicy default + UI: spec defaults to .preserve and adds an optional `exportQuality`/policy to AppViewModel. Confirm whether a power-user export panel (format + quality slider + 'preserve metadata' toggle + bit-depth picker) is in Phase 2 scope, or whether sensible defaults (preserve, 0.95, per-container depth) ship headless for now. The ExportRequest descriptor supports either.
- Orientation assumption verification: §3 assumes the decoder bakes EXIF orientation into pixels for BOTH paths (CIRAWFilter via its `orientation` property; CIImage(contentsOf:) only if `.applyOrientationProperty` is used — and the maps note loadImage DOES pass applyOrientationProperty at ImageProcessor.swift:36 while loadRAW relies on CIRAWFilter's internal default). Must verify the standard-image load path actually applies orientation before pixels reach export; if a path leaves orientation unbaked, the forced `orientation = 1` in ExportMetadata would be wrong for that path. Validate with test #7 across BOTH RAW and standard sources.
- GPS/IPTC privacy: .preserve copies GPS coordinates into exports. Some users will not want location leaked. Decide if .preserve should default to stripping GPS specifically (a third policy `.preserveExceptGPS`) while keeping EXIF — easy to add as a MetadataPolicy case that omits kCGImagePropertyGPSDictionary in representationOptions.

**Risks:**
- ORIENTATION DOUBLE-ROTATION: this is the single highest-risk item. Today export writes NO metadata, so files are orientation-naive and look fine because pixels are pre-baked upright. The moment we start writing metadata with .preserve, naively copying the source's EXIF orientation tag onto already-upright pixels makes every viewer re-rotate -> sideways exports. The ExportMetadata spec forces orientation=1; test #7 must guard it across RAW and standard sources. Getting this wrong is a visible, shipping-blocking regression introduced BY the metadata feature.
- LUT-INTERP vs OUTPUT SPACE DESYNC: if a future edit threads WorkingSpace into export (#2) but forgets makeFilter (#1) — or vice versa — LUTs interpolate in the wrong space and tones shift silently with NO error. Mitigated structurally (one value threaded through buildImage), but a careless refactor that reintroduces an inline literal in either site breaks it. Keep both reading request.space.
- 8-BIT GAMUT ON P3 EXPORT: enabling Display P3 without a soft-clip before the 8-bit JPEG/PNG encoders pushes out-of-gamut values -> hue shifts/banding the TIFF16 path won't reveal. The seam itself is safe at sRGB; the risk activates only with the P3 open question. Document the clamp requirement at the encode switch.
- DERIVE LEAKAGE: if export's refactor centralizes color-space handling and someone later routes RecipeExtractor.sRGB through WorkingSpace.current 'for consistency', every derived LUT silently changes meaning (it would be fit in P3 but the neutral baseline is an sRGB-default render). The doc comment + tests #10/#11 are the guard. This is an explicit hard-constraint violation if it slips.
- CIImageRepresentationOption KEY CORRECTNESS: the metadata keys passed to CI encoders must be the kCGImageProperty* dictionary keys wrapped as CIImageRepresentationOption(rawValue: key as String). If a wrong key (e.g. a CGImageDestination option vs a property key) is used, the encoder silently ignores it -> metadata appears to 'not preserve' with no error. Verify against ImageIO headers; test #6 catches silent drops.
- SENDABLE OF CGImageSource PROPERTIES: [CFString: Any] is not Sendable. The spec avoids the boundary by doing all metadata reading INSIDE the actor (passing only source URL across). If an implementer instead reads metadata on @MainActor and passes the dict into the actor, Swift 6 will flag it. Keep ExportMetadata.read called from within RenderEngine.export.
- EXPORT NOW RE-RENDERS (cost/behavior change): export previously reused the already-computed processedImage (instant). It now re-runs the full graph at full res. For large RAW + heavy node chains this is a real wall-clock cost during the save. Acceptable (it is the correctness fix that makes export honor develop+intensity), but the isExporting spinner must remain and the call must stay off the main actor (it does — inside the actor). Flag so no one 'optimizes' it back to reusing a preview-scale buffer.
- FILTER CACHE + VALUE SEMANTICS: caching CIFilter on the CubeLUT value type is awkward (mutation-on-read on a struct). Recommended fix is to memoize in RenderEngine (option b) so CubeLUT stays a clean Sendable value and no non-Sendable CIFilter is stored in app/document state. If an implementer instead stores the cache on CubeLUT as a stored property, it can break Sendable/Equatable and leak CIFilter lifetime outside the actor.

### UI inspector panel + tests

**Verdict:** minor-issues (concurrency FLAG · color FLAG · preview/export FLAG)

**Summary.** Adds a right-hand SwiftUI `.inspector` to ContentView with three collapsible groups (RAW Develop / Tone & Color / LUT) bound to a single `@Published var document: EditDocument` on AppViewModel. Every control mutates `document` value-fields; a Combine `.removeDuplicates().debounce()` pipeline calls `await RenderEngine.shared.renderPreview(...)` off the main actor and writes back `previewNSImage = NSImage(cgImage:)`. The RAW group is shown only when `source.kind == .raw` and each knob row is gated by per-image CIRAWFilter availability flags captured at open into a `RAWCapabilities` value. The Tone & Color group edits the closed enum `[AdjustmentNode]` (append + reorder + delete). The LUT group binds `document.lut.intensity` (0-100%) and the selected `LUTID`. Toolbar gains an inspector-toggle and a menu command; the existing NSEvent KeyMonitor is fixed so single-key shortcuts (v / [ / ] / space / arrows) do NOT fire while a text field is focused (new `isEditingTextField` gate + first-responder fallback). The PreviewView `selectedLUT`/`previewNSImage` references are migrated to the new state and the side-by-side guard switches to `document.lut.lutID != nil`. IMPORTANT DISCOVERY: this is a Swift Package Manager project (Package.swift, single executableTarget, NO .xcodeproj) with no test target — tests are added as a new SPM `.testTarget`, which requires extracting the app code into a library target so it is `@testable`-importable (an executable's `@main` cannot be imported). The test plan adds: cube round-trip, AdjustmentNode order correctness, preview==export parity, identity-pipeline no-op, LUT-intensity endpoints, and derive-baseline invariance.

**Implementation:**

## Component: UI inspector panel + tests

### 0. Ground truth from the real repo (verified by reading every file)

- **Build system is Swift Package Manager**, not Xcode. `Package.swift` (swift-tools-version 5.9) declares ONE `.executableTarget(name: "LUTzy", path: "LUTzy")`. There is **no `.xcodeproj`** and **no test target**. (The architecture brief's pbxproj/`TEST_HOST` guidance does not apply — see §7.)
- `ImageProcessor` is a non-`@MainActor` `final class` singleton with one Metal `CIContext`. RAW neutral decode is the **static** `ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral(at:)` (already the single source of truth; `loadRAW` and `RecipeExtractor.derive` both call it). Supported extensions are already unified in `ImageProcessor.supportedExtensions` (the brief's "two sources of truth" claim is stale — `ImageCollection` uses `ImageProcessor.supportedExtensions`).
- `CubeLUT.makeFilter()` hardcodes `CGColorSpace.sRGB` (line 135) and takes no params; `apply(to:)` is full strength (no intensity). `tableData` is `private`.
- `AppViewModel` is `@MainActor`. Real symbols: `sourceImage`, `selectedLUT`, `processedImage`, `private func applyLUT()`, `showOriginal(_:)`, `exportDialog()` (uses `Task.detached { [processor, exportFormat] ... }`), `private let maxPreview = CGSize(1600,1200)`, `isSideBySide` defaults to **true**.
- **PreviewView compiles today.** I read it: `sideBySideView`'s graded branch is `panelView(image: viewModel.previewNSImage, label: viewModel.selectedLUT?.name ?? "LUT", ...)`. There is **no undefined `preview` symbol and no FIXME**. The brief's "won't compile" claim is inaccurate; do NOT chase a non-existent bug. The real PreviewView edits are only the state migrations below.
- `LUTLibrary` exposes `@Published var allLUTs: [CubeLUT]` and `categories`. `CubeLUT.id` is a `String` (file path or `derived://...`), and `CubeLUT: Hashable`.
- `LUTSidebar.lutList` uses `List(selection: Binding(get: viewModel.selectedLUT, set: viewModel.selectLUT))` and `.tag(lut)`. This must be migrated to select by the new state too (covered).

This component **binds to** the model/engine types delivered by the render-pipeline component. Their exact required signatures (pinned so the inspector + tests compile):

```swift
struct EditDocument: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    var version: Int = 1
    var rawDevelop: RAWDevelopSettings = .neutral
    var adjustments: [AdjustmentNode] = []
    var lut: LUTSettings = .none
}
enum AdjustmentNode: Codable, Sendable, Equatable {
    case exposure(ev: Double)
    case colorControls(brightness: Double, contrast: Double, saturation: Double)
    case highlightShadow(highlights: Double, shadows: Double)
    case temperatureTint(temp: Double, tint: Double)
    case vibrance(amount: Double)
    func apply(to image: CIImage) -> CIImage
}
struct LUTSettings: Codable, Sendable, Equatable { var lutID: LUTID? = nil; var intensity: Double = 1.0; static let none = LUTSettings() }
struct LUTID: Codable, Sendable, Equatable, Hashable { let raw: UUID }
struct ImageSource: Sendable, Equatable { enum Kind: Sendable, Equatable { case raw, standard }; let url: URL; let kind: Kind; let nativeExtent: CGSize }
enum RenderScale: Sendable, Equatable { case preview(maxSize: CGSize); case full; var isPreview: Bool }
enum WorkingSpace: Sendable, Equatable { case sRGB, displayP3; var cgColorSpace: CGColorSpace; static let current: WorkingSpace }

actor RenderEngine {
    static let shared: RenderEngine
    func prepareSource(url: URL) async -> (ImageSource, RAWCapabilities)?
    func register(_ lut: CubeLUT, for id: LUTID)
    func renderPreview(source: ImageSource, document: EditDocument, maxSize: CGSize, space: WorkingSpace) -> CGImage?
    func export(source: ImageSource, document: EditDocument, to url: URL, format: ImageProcessor.ExportFormat, space: WorkingSpace, quality: CGFloat) throws
}
```

---

### 1. AppViewModel — state the inspector binds to

```swift
// BEFORE (real)
@Published var sourceImage: CIImage?
@Published var selectedLUT: CubeLUT?
@Published var processedImage: CIImage?
private let processor = ImageProcessor.shared
private var previewTask: Task<Void, Never>?
private let maxPreview = CGSize(width: 1600, height: 1200)
```
```swift
// AFTER
@Published var document = EditDocument()              // THE look (Codable/Equatable/Sendable)
@Published private(set) var source: ImageSource?      // URL+kind+extent; NO CIImage retained
@Published private(set) var rawCaps = RAWCapabilities.none
@Published var previewNSImage: NSImage?
@Published var originalPreviewNSImage: NSImage?
@Published var isShowingOriginal = false
@Published var isSideBySide = true                    // keep existing default

@Published var isInspectorPresented = true            // toolbar + menu toggle target
@Published var isEditingTextField = false             // KeyMonitor suppression gate

private let engine = RenderEngine.shared
private let workingSpace = WorkingSpace.current
private let maxPreview = CGSize(width: 1600, height: 1200)
private var renderTask: Task<Void, Never>?
private var lastToken: RenderToken?
private var cancellables = Set<AnyCancellable>()
private var lutIDs: [String: LUTID] = [:]             // CubeLUT.id (String) -> engine LUTID

private struct RenderToken: Equatable {
    let source: ImageSource; let document: EditDocument; let space: WorkingSpace
}
```

**Debounced render driven by document mutation.** `EditDocument: Equatable` makes `.removeDuplicates()` free and coalesces slider drags:
```swift
// in init(), keep the two existing library/collection objectWillChange sinks (move into cancellables),
// then add:
$document
    .removeDuplicates()
    .debounce(for: .milliseconds(80), scheduler: RunLoop.main)
    .sink { [weak self] _ in
        guard let self else { return }
        self.scheduleRender(scale: .preview(maxSize: self.maxPreview))
    }
    .store(in: &cancellables)
```

```swift
func scheduleRender(scale: RenderScale) {
    guard let source else { return }
    let token = RenderToken(source: source, document: document, space: workingSpace)
    if scale.isPreview, token == lastToken { return }
    renderTask?.cancel()
    let doc = document, ws = workingSpace, maxSize = maxPreview
    renderTask = Task { [weak self, engine] in
        guard let cg = await engine.renderPreview(source: source, document: doc,
                                                  maxSize: maxSize, space: ws) else { return }
        if Task.isCancelled { return }
        await MainActor.run {
            guard let self else { return }
            self.previewNSImage = NSImage(cgImage: cg,
                size: NSSize(width: cg.width, height: cg.height))
            self.lastToken = token
        }
    }
}
```

**openImage rewrite** (both the URL and the `data:` variant). Builds `ImageSource` + `RAWCapabilities`, renders a cached neutral "original" for A/B, then renders the live document. CIImage/CIRAWFilter stay inside the actor:
```swift
func openImage(url: URL) {
    isLoading = true
    statusMessage = "Loading \(url.lastPathComponent)..."
    let ws = workingSpace, maxSize = maxPreview
    Task { [weak self, engine] in
        guard let (src, caps) = await engine.prepareSource(url: url) else {
            await MainActor.run { self?.isLoading = false
                self?.statusMessage = "Cannot load \(url.lastPathComponent)" }
            return
        }
        let originalDoc = EditDocument()   // neutral, no LUT
        let originalCG = await engine.renderPreview(source: src, document: originalDoc,
                                                    maxSize: maxSize, space: ws)
        await MainActor.run {
            guard let self else { return }
            self.isLoading = false
            self.source = src; self.rawCaps = caps
            self.sourceURL = url; self.sourceName = url.lastPathComponent
            self.sourceSize = src.nativeExtent
            self.statusMessage = "\(self.sourceName)  \(Int(src.nativeExtent.width))×\(Int(src.nativeExtent.height))"
            if let originalCG {
                self.originalPreviewNSImage = NSImage(cgImage: originalCG,
                    size: NSSize(width: originalCG.width, height: originalCG.height))
            }
            // Keep current document so a power user A/Bs the same look across a folder (see open Qs).
            self.scheduleRender(scale: .preview(maxSize: self.maxPreview))
        }
    }
}
```
The `data:` import path needs an `ImageSource` from bytes. Add an actor overload `prepareSource(data:name:) async -> (ImageSource, RAWCapabilities)?` that writes to a temp file (Photos data is always standard, never RAW) and returns `.standard`; or extend `ImageSource` with a `.data(Data)` kind. Spec choice: temp-file route (keeps `ImageSource` URL-only and Sendable-clean).

**selectLUT** updates the document instead of `selectedLUT`:
```swift
func selectLUT(_ lut: CubeLUT?) {
    document.lut.lutID = lut.map { engineLUTID(for: $0) }
    if let lut { statusMessage = "LUT: \(lut.name)" }
    // $document observer fires -> debounced render. No direct render call needed.
}
private func engineLUTID(for lut: CubeLUT) -> LUTID {     // also exposed internally for the Picker
    if let existing = lutIDs[lut.id] { return existing }
    let id = LUTID(raw: UUID())
    lutIDs[lut.id] = id
    Task { [engine] in await engine.register(lut, for: id) }
    return id
}
func engineLUTID(forCube lut: CubeLUT) -> LUTID { engineLUTID(for: lut) }  // public alias for views
```
`selectPreviousLUT`/`selectNextLUT` keep their `library.allLUTs.firstIndex(of:)` math but must derive the "current" from the document: resolve `document.lut.lutID` back to a `CubeLUT` by scanning `library.allLUTs` for one whose `engineLUTID` matches, or maintain a reverse map. Simplest: keep a `private var currentLUT: CubeLUT?` mirror updated in `selectLUT` purely for index math (it is NOT the source of truth; document is).

**showOriginal** stops re-rendering — swap cached images:
```swift
func showOriginal(_ show: Bool) { isShowingOriginal = show }   // PreviewView picks the buffer
```

**applyLUT()** is deleted; its old callers (openImage, selectLUT) now go through the document observer.

**exportDialog** renders the SAME document at full res through the actor (no captured non-Sendable singleton):
```swift
func exportDialog() {
    guard let source else { statusMessage = "Open an image first"; return }
    let suffix = currentLUT.map { "_" + $0.name.replacingOccurrences(of: " ", with: "_") } ?? ""
    let base = sourceURL?.deletingPathExtension().lastPathComponent ?? "image"
    let panel = NSSavePanel()
    panel.nameFieldStringValue = base + suffix + "." + exportFormat.fileExtension
    panel.allowedContentTypes = [exportFormat.utType]
    guard panel.runModal() == .OK, let url = panel.url else { return }
    isExporting = true; statusMessage = "Exporting..."
    let doc = document, fmt = exportFormat, ws = workingSpace
    Task { [weak self, engine] in
        do {
            try await engine.export(source: source, document: doc, to: url, format: fmt, space: ws, quality: 0.95)
            await MainActor.run { self?.isExporting = false
                self?.statusMessage = "Exported: \(url.lastPathComponent)" }
        } catch {
            await MainActor.run { self?.isExporting = false
                self?.statusMessage = "Export failed: \(error.localizedDescription)" }
        }
    }
}
```

**Binding helpers** the inspector uses:
```swift
func binding<T>(_ kp: WritableKeyPath<EditDocument, T>) -> Binding<T> {
    Binding(get: { self.document[keyPath: kp] }, set: { self.document[keyPath: kp] = $0 })
}
func rawBinding<T>(_ kp: WritableKeyPath<RAWDevelopSettings, T>) -> Binding<T> {
    Binding(get: { self.document.rawDevelop[keyPath: kp] },
            set: { self.document.rawDevelop[keyPath: kp] = $0 })
}
var intensityBinding: Binding<Double> {
    Binding(get: { self.document.lut.intensity }, set: { self.document.lut.intensity = $0 })
}
var editingBinding: Binding<Bool> {
    Binding(get: { self.isEditingTextField }, set: { self.isEditingTextField = $0 })
}
func moveAdjustment(_ idx: Int, by delta: Int) {
    let j = idx + delta
    guard document.adjustments.indices.contains(idx),
          document.adjustments.indices.contains(j) else { return }
    document.adjustments.swapAt(idx, j)
}
```

**Derive wiring** (`deriveRecipe` success branch, after building `lut`): it already calls `selectLUT(lut)` when `sourceImage != nil`. Change the guard to `if self.source != nil { self.selectLUT(lut) }`. `selectLUT` now registers the derived LUT under a fresh `LUTID` (via `engineLUTID`) and sets `document.lut.lutID`. RecipeExtractor itself is untouched — see test §F.

### 2. RAWCapabilities (new value type)

```swift
// RAWCapabilities.swift
import Foundation
struct RAWCapabilities: Sendable, Equatable {
    var isRAW = false
    var baselineExposureAvailable = false
    var enableEDRAvailable = false
    var sharpnessSupported = false
    var moireReductionSupported = false
    var lensCorrectionSupported = false
    var dustRemovalSupported = false
    var defaultTemperature: Double? = nil   // f.neutralTemperature at decode (metadata)
    var defaultTint: Double? = nil          // f.neutralTint at decode
    var baselineExposure: Double? = nil
    static let none = RAWCapabilities()
}
```
Builder lives inside `RenderEngine.prepareSource` (the only place CIRAWFilter is probed, so the non-Sendable filter never escapes). It must mirror `ImageProcessor.developRAWNeutral`'s RAW detection (`ImageProcessor.supportedExtensions` minus standard, or expose a `static var rawExtensions`):
```swift
// inside actor RenderEngine
private func probeRAW(_ url: URL) -> RAWCapabilities? {
    guard let f = CIRAWFilter(imageURL: url) else { return nil }
    return RAWCapabilities(
        isRAW: true,
        baselineExposureAvailable: f.isBaselineExposureAvailable,
        enableEDRAvailable: f.isEnableEDRModeAvailable,
        sharpnessSupported: f.isSharpnessSupported,
        moireReductionSupported: f.isMoireReductionSupported,
        lensCorrectionSupported: f.isLensCorrectionSupported,
        dustRemovalSupported: f.isDustRemovalSupported,
        defaultTemperature: Double(f.neutralTemperature),
        defaultTint: Double(f.neutralTint),
        baselineExposure: f.isBaselineExposureAvailable ? Double(f.baselineExposure) : nil)
}
```
(SDK-verified names: `isBaselineExposureAvailable`, `isEnableEDRModeAvailable`, `isSharpnessSupported`, `isMoireReductionSupported`, `isLensCorrectionSupported`, `isDustRemovalSupported`, `neutralTemperature`, `neutralTint`, `baselineExposure`.) `RAWDevelopSettings.apply(to:)` is defined in the render component and must consult the same `is…Supported` flags before setting gated knobs.

### 3. ContentView — inspector, toolbar, key monitor

**Inspector** (macOS 14 `.inspector`, zero-dependency, collapsible):
```swift
// body, BEFORE:
mainContent
    .toolbar { ToolbarItemGroup(placement: .primaryAction) { toolbarContent } }
    .modifier(KeyboardShortcuts(viewModel: viewModel))
// AFTER (insert .inspector before .toolbar):
mainContent
    .inspector(isPresented: $viewModel.isInspectorPresented) {
        InspectorView(viewModel: viewModel)
            .inspectorColumnWidth(min: 260, ideal: 300, max: 380)
    }
    .toolbar { ToolbarItemGroup(placement: .primaryAction) { toolbarContent } }
    .modifier(KeyboardShortcuts(viewModel: viewModel))
```
Keep `.sheet`, `.photosPicker`, `.onChange`, `MenuCommandReceivers`.

**Toolbar toggle** (add into `toolbarContent`, next to the side-by-side button); also fix the Export disable:
```swift
Button { viewModel.isInspectorPresented.toggle() } label: {
    Label("Inspector", systemImage: "sidebar.trailing")
}
.help("Show or hide the adjustments inspector")
// change:
.disabled(viewModel.sourceImage == nil)   ->   .disabled(viewModel.source == nil)
```
Optional: add a `CommandGroup` button in `LUTzyApp` posting `.toggleInspector` and a receiver in `MenuCommandReceivers` calling `viewModel.isInspectorPresented.toggle()` (mirrors existing notification pattern). `keyboardShortcut("i", modifiers: [.command, .control])` to avoid clashing with the two existing ⌘⇧I / ⌘⌥I import shortcuts.

**KeyMonitor fix (critical).** The real `handle` bails only on `vm.isRecipeSheetPresented` and `.command`. With an always-visible inspector full of `TextField`s, typing `v`/digits would toggle side-by-side / be lost. Add the gate:
```swift
// KeyMonitor.handle, BEFORE:
if vm.isRecipeSheetPresented { return event }
let mods = event.modifierFlags.intersection(.deviceIndependentFlagsMask)
if mods.contains(.command) { return event }
// AFTER:
if vm.isRecipeSheetPresented || vm.isEditingTextField { return event }
if isTextFieldFirstResponder() { return event }   // belt-and-suspenders, see below
let mods = event.modifierFlags.intersection(.deviceIndependentFlagsMask)
if mods.contains(.command) { return event }
```
```swift
@MainActor private func isTextFieldFirstResponder() -> Bool {
    guard let r = NSApp.keyWindow?.firstResponder else { return false }
    if r is NSText { return true }                       // field editor while editing
    if let v = r as? NSView, v is NSTextField || v is NSTextView { return true }
    return false
}
```
`isEditingTextField` is the deterministic primary mechanism (set by SwiftUI `@FocusState` in LabeledSlider), the first-responder check is a fallback for non-SwiftUI fields (the sidebar search field, sheet fields). Note the LUTSidebar search `TextField` already exists and currently relies on the sheet-only bail; this fix also protects it.

**LUTSidebar selection migration** (so sidebar and inspector agree): change the selection binding to resolve through the document:
```swift
List(selection: Binding(
    get: { viewModel.currentLUT },                       // mirror kept in VM
    set: { viewModel.selectLUT($0) })) { ... }
// and LUTRow isSelected: viewModel.currentLUT == lut
```

### 4. InspectorView + sections (new file)

```swift
import SwiftUI

struct InspectorView: View {
    @ObservedObject var viewModel: AppViewModel
    var body: some View {
        Form {
            if viewModel.source == nil {
                ContentUnavailableView("No Image", systemImage: "slider.horizontal.3",
                                       description: Text("Open an image to adjust it"))
            } else {
                if viewModel.source?.kind == .raw { RAWDevelopSection(viewModel: viewModel) }
                ToneColorSection(viewModel: viewModel)
                LUTSection(viewModel: viewModel)
            }
        }
        .formStyle(.grouped)
        .scrollContentBackground(.hidden)
    }
}
```

**RAW develop** (each row an `OptionalSlider`; nil = decoder default; gated rows hidden by capability). CIRAWFilter property mapping is documented inline so a reviewer can cross-check `RAWDevelopSettings.apply(to:)`:
```swift
struct RAWDevelopSection: View {
    @ObservedObject var viewModel: AppViewModel
    @State private var expanded = true
    var body: some View {
        DisclosureGroup("RAW Develop", isExpanded: $expanded) {
            OptionalSlider("Exposure", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.exposure),         // f.exposure (stops)
                           range: -10...10, defaultValue: 0, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            OptionalSlider("Temperature", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.neutralTemperature), // f.neutralTemperature (K)
                           range: 2000...10000,
                           defaultValue: viewModel.rawCaps.defaultTemperature ?? 5500,
                           editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            OptionalSlider("Tint", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.neutralTint),          // f.neutralTint
                           range: -150...150,
                           defaultValue: viewModel.rawCaps.defaultTint ?? 0, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            OptionalSlider("Shadow Bias", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.shadowBias),    // f.shadowBias
                           range: 0...1, defaultValue: 0, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            OptionalSlider("Boost", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.boostAmount),         // f.boostAmount
                           range: 0...1, defaultValue: 1, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            OptionalSlider("Boost Shadow", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.boostShadowAmount), // f.boostShadowAmount
                           range: -1...1, defaultValue: 0, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            OptionalSlider("Contrast", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.contrastAmount),   // f.contrastAmount
                           range: 0...1, defaultValue: 0.35, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            OptionalSlider("Local Tone Map", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.localToneMapAmount), // f.localToneMapAmount
                           range: 0...1, defaultValue: 0, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            if viewModel.rawCaps.sharpnessSupported {
                OptionalSlider("Sharpness", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.sharpnessAmount), // f.sharpnessAmount
                               range: 0...1, defaultValue: 0, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            }
            OptionalSlider("Luminance NR", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.luminanceNoiseReductionAmount),
                           range: 0...1, defaultValue: 0, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            OptionalSlider("Color NR", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.colorNoiseReductionAmount),
                           range: 0...1, defaultValue: 0, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            if viewModel.rawCaps.enableEDRAvailable {
                Toggle("Extended Dynamic Range", isOn: viewModel.rawBinding(\.enableEDR).orFalse) // f.isEDRModeEnabled
                OptionalSlider("EDR Amount", value: viewModel.rawBinding(\.extendedDynamicRangeAmount),
                               range: 0...2, defaultValue: 0, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            }
            Button("Reset RAW") { viewModel.document.rawDevelop = .neutral }
        }
    }
}
// Binding<Bool?>.orFalse: get { self.wrappedValue ?? false } set { self.wrappedValue = $0 }
```

**Tone & Color** (ordered editor over the closed enum):
```swift
struct ToneColorSection: View {
    @ObservedObject var viewModel: AppViewModel
    @State private var expanded = true
    var body: some View {
        DisclosureGroup("Tone & Color", isExpanded: $expanded) {
            ForEach(Array(viewModel.document.adjustments.enumerated()), id: \.offset) { idx, node in
                AdjustmentRow(node: node,
                    onChange:   { viewModel.document.adjustments[idx] = $0 },
                    onMoveUp:   idx > 0 ? { viewModel.moveAdjustment(idx, by: -1) } : nil,
                    onMoveDown: idx < viewModel.document.adjustments.count - 1
                                ? { viewModel.moveAdjustment(idx, by: +1) } : nil,
                    onDelete:   { viewModel.document.adjustments.remove(at: idx) },
                    editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
            }
            Menu("Add Adjustment") {
                Button("Exposure")            { viewModel.document.adjustments.append(.exposure(ev: 0)) }
                Button("Color Controls")      { viewModel.document.adjustments.append(.colorControls(brightness: 0, contrast: 1, saturation: 1)) }
                Button("Highlights / Shadows"){ viewModel.document.adjustments.append(.highlightShadow(highlights: 1, shadows: 0)) }
                Button("Temperature / Tint")  { viewModel.document.adjustments.append(.temperatureTint(temp: 6500, tint: 0)) }
                Button("Vibrance")            { viewModel.document.adjustments.append(.vibrance(amount: 0)) }
            }
        }
    }
}
```
`AdjustmentRow` switches on the case and writes a rebuilt case via `onChange`. The CIFilter keys the row's defaults correspond to (and that `AdjustmentNode.apply` must use): `CIExposureAdjust`/`kCIInputEVKey`; `CIColorControls`/`kCIInputBrightnessKey,kCIInputContrastKey,kCIInputSaturationKey`; `CIHighlightShadowAdjust`/`inputHighlightAmount,inputShadowAmount`; `CITemperatureAndTint`/`inputNeutral`+`inputTargetNeutral` (CIVector); `CIVibrance`/`inputAmount`. Example arm:
```swift
case let .colorControls(b, c, s):
    LabeledSlider("Brightness", value: .init(get: { b }, set: { onChange(.colorControls(brightness: $0, contrast: c, saturation: s)) }),
                  range: -1...1, defaultValue: 0, editing: editing)
    LabeledSlider("Contrast",   value: .init(get: { c }, set: { onChange(.colorControls(brightness: b, contrast: $0, saturation: s)) }),
                  range: 0.25...4, defaultValue: 1, editing: editing)
    LabeledSlider("Saturation", value: .init(get: { s }, set: { onChange(.colorControls(brightness: b, contrast: c, saturation: $0)) }),
                  range: 0...2, defaultValue: 1, editing: editing)
```

**LUT** section:
```swift
struct LUTSection: View {
    @ObservedObject var viewModel: AppViewModel
    @State private var expanded = true
    private var lutSelection: Binding<LUTID?> {
        Binding(get: { viewModel.document.lut.lutID },
                set: { viewModel.document.lut.lutID = $0 })   // direct set; or route via selectLUT for status text
    }
    var body: some View {
        DisclosureGroup("LUT", isExpanded: $expanded) {
            Picker("LUT", selection: lutSelection) {
                Text("None").tag(LUTID?.none)
                ForEach(viewModel.library.allLUTs) { lut in
                    Text(lut.name).tag(Optional(viewModel.engineLUTID(forCube: lut)))
                }
            }
            LabeledSlider("Intensity", value: viewModel.intensityBinding, range: 0...1,
                          defaultValue: 1, percent: true, editing: viewModel.editingBinding)
                .disabled(viewModel.document.lut.lutID == nil)
            Button("Reset LUT") { viewModel.document.lut = .none }
        }
    }
}
```

**LabeledSlider** (focus-reporting):
```swift
struct LabeledSlider: View {
    let title: String
    @Binding var value: Double
    let range: ClosedRange<Double>
    var defaultValue: Double
    var percent: Bool = false
    @Binding var editing: Bool
    @FocusState private var focused: Bool
    init(_ title: String, value: Binding<Double>, range: ClosedRange<Double>,
         defaultValue: Double, percent: Bool = false, editing: Binding<Bool>) {
        self.title = title; self._value = value; self.range = range
        self.defaultValue = defaultValue; self.percent = percent; self._editing = editing
    }
    var body: some View {
        VStack(alignment: .leading, spacing: 2) {
            HStack {
                Text(title); Spacer()
                TextField("", value: $value, format: .number.precision(.fractionLength(2)))
                    .frame(width: 64).multilineTextAlignment(.trailing).focused($focused)
                Button { value = defaultValue } label: { Image(systemName: "arrow.uturn.backward") }
                    .buttonStyle(.borderless).help("Reset to \(defaultValue, format: .number)")
            }
            Slider(value: $value, in: range)
        }
        .onChange(of: focused) { _, now in editing = now }
    }
}
```
`OptionalSlider` wraps `Binding<Double?>`: a leading `Toggle("Default", isOn:)` whose on-state writes `nil` and off-state materializes `defaultValue`; when overridden it embeds a `LabeledSlider` bound to a non-optional projection `Binding(get: { value.wrappedValue ?? defaultValue }, set: { value.wrappedValue = $0 })`.
